| Publish and subscribe data shares | Publish id, subscription id, object id, target workspace id, name-check result, or UI-selected share target. | Publish/subscribe/delete requires one publication/subscription/object/target workspace and reviewed share decision. | Publish/subscription/object/target lists and name checks are scoped to current filters and time. | If name/object/target matches are ambiguous or changed, ask for exact publish/subscription/object/target selection. | Do not use first target workspace, stale name availability, similar object names, or inferred subscriber state. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Database/table row, workbook id, version id, query id, statement id, history row, or UI-selected SQL item. | Execute/cancel/drop/kill/save requires one workbook/query/statement or metadata target and explicit SQL/action decision. | Metadata, history, statement result, and workbook version lists are page/scope/time bounded. | If multiple tables/workbooks/queries match or history is incomplete, ask for exact database/table/workbook/query/statement selection. | Do not select by SQL text snippet, first history row, latest query unless selected, or similar table name. |

### User Story Ordering and Recency Coverage

Use this table after Selection Disambiguation Coverage and before Outcome Contracts so AI-written code does not implement latest, recent, oldest, first, top, sorted, priority, or time-window wording by client-side guessing. Every promoted task must state ordering intent, authoritative order source, recency/time-window source, forbidden ordering shortcut, and AI-safe ordering behavior. Code entries in ordering and recency cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If order or recency cannot be proven from a typed SDK result, backend-declared sort/filter, UI-selected sorted view, or explicit user input, code must ask for the sort/window instead of reordering, picking newest, or treating list order as meaningful.

| User task | Ordering intent | Authoritative order source | Recency/time-window source | Forbidden ordering shortcut | AI-safe ordering behavior |
|-----------|-----------------|----------------------------|----------------------------|-----------------------------|---------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Current workspace, recently used workspace, or first matching workspace only when explicitly requested. | Typed workspace result, backend-declared list sort, UI-selected workspace row, or explicit workspace id/name. | Caller-provided time window, backend workspace timestamps, or UI recent-workspace state when returned. | Do not treat list order, account name order, or local cache order as latest workspace. | Return the sourced ordering evidence; ask for workspace id/name or sort/window when recency is not explicit. |
| Manage workspace members | Latest member audit events, active members, or a caller-requested member ordering. | Backend list/audit order, typed member fields, or the explicit UI sort. | Backend timestamps and the caller-provided audit window. | Do not infer role priority or select a member from a partial page by display name. | Preserve authoritative order and page/window evidence; ask before selecting a mutation target. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Latest table, sorted catalog tree, top rows, recent load, or ordered preview/data rows. | Backend catalog/table order, SQL/table result order, explicit order-by/sort option, or UI-selected table row. | Backend create/update/load timestamps, query time window, or caller-provided data window. | Do not reorder table rows, treat preview order as full-table order, or pick newest table by name. | Use typed sort/order fields when present; ask for order-by/window before latest/top table or row decisions. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Latest upload, sorted files, recent preview, newest ref, or ordered folder listing. | Backend file/folder/ref list order, typed timestamps, upload result, requested sort, or UI-selected file row. | Upload/create/update timestamps, caller time window, or backend file history fields. | Do not infer latest file from filename, local path mtime, first page order, or preview-link issue time. | Return timestamp/sort evidence and page boundary; ask for file id/sort/window when latest is ambiguous. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Latest run, active run, most recent rerun, ordered nodes, or recent workflow history. | Backend run/history/branch order, typed run status timestamps, requested sort/filter, or UI-selected execution row. | Run start/end/update time, terminal status time, caller time window, or backend history cursor. | Do not choose latest run from a partial page, sort node labels, or cancel first active run without selection. | Use backend run order and status evidence; ask before latest/active run actions when page/window is incomplete. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Latest template, newest operator version, sorted design assets, or recent test result. | Backend operator/template list order, typed version/test timestamps, requested sort, or UI-selected design row. | Version/test/update timestamp, caller review window, or backend design history fields. | Do not infer version priority from names, use local file mtime, or treat first template page as newest. | Show version/timestamp evidence; ask for exact operator/template/version when ordering is not authoritative. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Latest service version, preferred version, recent invocation, or sorted service catalog. | WorkItem catalog metadata, service version fields, backend list order, requested sort, or UI-selected service version. | Version publish/update timestamp, invocation timestamp, or caller-supplied version/window. | Do not choose latest version by lexical version string, first catalog row, or route path order. | Require explicit version or backend-selected preferred/latest evidence before publish/invoke. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Latest revision, effective revision, ordered blocks, recent artifact output, or newest rerun. | Lineage revision metadata, effective revision field, block order from backend, rerun status order, or UI-selected lineage row. | Revision/create/update timestamp, producing run time, rerun time, or caller review window. | Do not choose latest revision from rendered labels, block display order, or first revision page. | Prefer effective revision when exposed; ask for revision/block/rerun choice when latest is not proven. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Latest semantic entry, newest model, sorted terms, import order, or validation diagnostic priority. | Backend model/entry order, typed update timestamps, validation diagnostic order, requested sort, or UI-selected semantic row. | Entry/model update timestamp, import/export time, validation time, or caller review window. | Do not infer priority from term text, first entry page, or local export order. | Return order/timestamp/diagnostic evidence; ask for exact model/entry or sort when latest/top is ambiguous. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Latest task run, recent file, sorted source tables, retry-file order, or newest connector state. | Backend task/run/file/source order, typed task status timestamps, requested sort/filter, or UI-selected connector/task row. | Task create/update/run time, file timestamp, connector status time, or caller transfer window. | Do not pick first task run, infer source table priority from names, or use local file order. | Use backend status/run order and timestamps; ask before retry/resume/delete when latest task/run is not proven. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Latest task event, final stream order, recent conversation, or priority input request. | A2A task status/event order, stream final event, typed task/message timestamps, or UI-selected task/message. | Task/message timestamp, stream event time, resubscribe cursor, or caller conversation window. | Do not treat first stream chunk, local event order, or first task list row as latest/final task state. | Preserve task/event ordering from A2A results; ask before acting on latest task/turn without sourced ids. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Preferred model, sorted backend list, latest probe, active router, or recent endpoint status. | Backend provider/router metadata, requested sort/filter, probe timestamp, endpoint status result, or UI-selected backend/router. | Probe time, status update time, backend update timestamp, or caller-provided provider window. | Do not choose default model, first backend, lexicographic model name, or stale probe as latest status. | Return provider/router/probe timestamp evidence; ask for model/backend/router choice when order is unclear. |
| Manage compute resources | Recent resource action, sorted resources, newest image/spec, or top capacity usage. | Resource/action timestamps, requested sort/filter, or UI-selected compute row. | Action/update timestamp, resource create time, or caller-selected list scope. | Do not infer latest resource from response arrival, image names, first resource page, or local clock. | Return list order evidence; ask before top/latest resource action when sort is missing. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Latest publication, recent subscription, sorted target workspaces, or newest object list. | Backend share list order, typed publish/subscription timestamps, requested sort/filter, or UI-selected share row. | Publish/subscription/update time, name-check time, or caller-provided share window. | Do not use stale name-check time, first target workspace, or object name order as latest share state. | Return timestamp/sort evidence; ask before latest publication/subscription actions when scope is unclear. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Latest query, recent workbook version, ordered result rows, top rows, or recent metadata changes. | Backend query history order, workbook/version metadata, SQL `ORDER BY`, statement result order, requested sort/filter, or UI-selected query row. | Query start/end time, workbook version time, result page time, or caller history/result window. | Do not sort SQL rows client-side, choose latest query from partial history, or infer order from SQL text snippets. | Require SQL/backend order evidence; ask for `ORDER BY`, query id, statement id, or history window when order matters. |

### User Story Outcome Contracts

Use this contract after the flow recipe to decide whether a promoted task has actually reached a user-visible outcome. Every task in the User Story Matrix must state the result the user can inspect, the handle/spec to continue with, and caller-owned inputs or decisions. AI-written code must not treat a route call as complete unless the outcome and continuation contract are satisfied.

| User task | User-visible outcome | Continue with | Caller-owned inputs/decisions |
|-----------|----------------------|---------------|-------------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | The user can inspect account identity, selected workspace scope, and workspace DB connection status. | `Client.EnsureWorkspace`, `Client.Workspace`, `WorkspaceHandle`, `Client.ensure_workspace`, `Client.workspace` | Product endpoint, API key/session, timeout, workspace id/choice, and duplicate-name resolution remain application or user decisions. |
| Manage workspace members | The caller can inspect member detail, canonical role bindings, status, and supported general audit logs. | `WorkspaceMemberHandle`, `IAMService.UserRoleBindings` / `user_role_bindings`, and `WorkspaceMemberService.AuditLogs` | Member ids, canonical role ids, status changes, and delete decisions must come from typed IAM results, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | The user can inspect catalog/database/table structure, table data rows, preview rows, and table operation status. | `CatalogHandle`, `DatabaseHandle`, `TableHandle`, `TableColumn` | Catalog/database/table ids, column definitions, file URLs, and destructive table choices remain typed result, UI, or reviewed input values. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | The user can inspect uploaded files, preview/download links, folder state, connector-file previews, refs, and file-derived artifacts. | `VolumeHandle`, `FolderHandle`, `FileHandle`, `UploadedFilePreview`, `ConnectorFileHandle` | File bytes/paths, volume/folder/file ids, connector-file ids, rename targets, and delete/clean decisions remain caller-owned inputs or selected results. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | The user can inspect deployed workflow metadata, run data, execution result/status, run handle state, node detail, rerun branches, and cancellation outcome. | `WorkflowHandle`, `WorkflowRunHandle`, `WorkflowRunNodeHandle`, `WorkflowRunRerunHandle` | Workflow DSL, run inputs, workflow/run/node/rerun ids, rerun mode, and cancellation choices must come from reviewed input, backend results, or UI state. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | The user can inspect custom-operator create/test/update state, enabled state, code, and workflow template metadata. | `CustomOperatorHandle`, `CustomOperatorSpec`, `CustomOperatorTestInput`, `WorkflowTemplateHandle` | Operator/template ids, schemas, code, DSL, test payloads, and enable/delete decisions must be reviewed caller input or typed result values. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | The user can inspect one versioned WorkItem API service, publish/enable state, invocation result, and disable outcome. | `WorkitemAPIServiceHandle`, `WorkitemAPIServicePublishSpec`, `WorkitemAPIServiceInvokeSpec` | Node id, version, service name/type, compute resource, limits, and invoke payload must come from catalog metadata, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | The user can inspect artifact overview, output blocks, revisions, effective revision switches, node details, and rerun result. | `ArtifactHandle`, `ArtifactBlockHandle`, `ArtifactNodeHandle`, `ArtifactNodeBlockHandle`, `LineageRevisionSwitch` | Artifact/node/block/revision ids, revision switches, rerun mode, and review decisions must come from lineage results, run detail, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | The user can inspect semantic model metadata, entry lifecycle state, validation result, import result, and export result. | `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, `SemanticEntryInput` | Model ids, entry ids, entry specs, table bindings, import payloads, and validation choices must come from typed results, editor state, or reviewed input. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | The user can inspect connector state, source/target metadata, connector-file previews, import task files/runs, and export task state. | `ConnectorHandle`, `FileImportTaskSpec`, `ImportTaskUpdateSpec`, `ExportTaskSpec`, `ExportTaskConfig`, `ExportTaskFile`, `ImportTaskHandle`, `ExportTaskHandle` | Connector credentials, storage config, source type, usage type, task ids, volume/file ids, and enum choices must come from metadata, secrets, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Use product agents through A2A | The user can inspect agent card/configuration, A2A task/message result, stream state, task status, cancellation, resubscribe, and input-submit state. | `AgentHandle`, `AgentTextMessage`, `AgentTaskRequest`, `AgentInputSubmission` | Agent code/id, request/message ids, task ids, turn ids, text, and input payloads remain caller-generated, typed result, UI, or explicit input values. |
| Configure model providers and routers | The user can inspect LLM provider handle state, provider backend setup/create/update state, endpoints, model probe results, and router configuration. | `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, `ProviderRouterHandle` | Provider family, backend ids, endpoints, secrets, model names, endpoint status, and router strategy remain configuration, secret, metadata, or explicit input values. |
| Manage compute resources | The user can inspect compute resource detail, update result, delete preflight, and suspended/resumed action status. | `ComputeResourceHandle` | Resource ids, spec ids, sizing values, action choices, and delete approval must come from compute metadata, typed results, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | The user can inspect workspace share summary, name availability, publication state, subscription state, subscribed/unsubscribed state, and delete outcome. | `DataSharePublishHandle`, `DataShareSubscriptionHandle` | Publish/subscription/object/location/target workspace ids, object choices, and share decisions must come from typed results, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | The user can inspect SQL metadata, workbook/version state, query execution state, statement result rows/detail/profile, and cancellation outcome. | `SQLMetadataHandle`, `SQLDatabaseMetadataHandle`, `SQLTableMetadataHandle`, `SQLWorkbookHandle`, `SQLWorkbookVersionHandle`, `SQLQueryHandle`, `SQLStatementHandle` | Database/table names, workbook/version/query/statement ids, SQL text, execution context, and cancel decisions must come from metadata, results, UI state, or explicit input. |

### User Story Interaction Mode Coverage

Use this table to keep promoted SDK tasks user-operable instead of merely route-reachable. Every User Story Matrix task must state whether the user interaction is synchronous, long-running, streaming, paginated, batched, or destructive. AI-written code must choose the interaction mode before selecting supplemental methods, and must not invent polling, streaming, pagination, batch mutation, delete, cancel, or force behavior when the row says the value must come from typed results, UI state, or explicit user input. Code entries in interaction mode cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task.

| User task | Interaction modes | Long-running or streaming behavior | Pagination or batch behavior | Destructive or approval boundary |
|-----------|-------------------|------------------------------------|------------------------------|----------------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Synchronous read/create/bind bootstrap. | No long-running or streaming path in the promoted bootstrap; errors return directly from endpoint/auth/workspace calls. | Workspace list and user reads may be paginated by backend result shape, but promoted bootstrap examples do not invent page limits. | Workspace selection and duplicate-name resolution require typed workspace results, UI state, or explicit user input; promoted bootstrap does not delete workspace state. |
| Manage workspace members | Synchronous member read/write, role-binding, status, and supported general audit operations. | No long-running or streaming path; reads return typed results or explicit errors. | Member and general audit lists use caller-selected limits or typed Backend pages. | Role-binding replacement, status changes, and member deletion require an explicit member target and caller approval. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Synchronous discover/create/read/load/truncate/delete table operations. | Table load/status returns typed operation state; no invented polling or background success is allowed. | Table data, preview, overview, and child lists use caller-selected page size, preview lines, or typed list results. | Table truncate/delete/load choices require reviewed table handle, data URL, and explicit user decision; no table id is inferred from names or labels. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Upload/download/preview/list/stream plus folder/file mutation. | File download or connector-file stream is explicit when selected; uploads and trigger-workflow calls return typed results or explicit errors. | File lists, refs, connector processed files, preview rows, and Excel preview use caller-selected limits, offsets, or backend typed result pages. | Folder clean/delete, file delete/delete-ref, volume delete, detach workflow, and trigger workflow require sourced handles plus explicit user decision. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Deploy/run/status/result/retry/rerun/cancel lifecycle. | Workflow runs and reruns are long-running; callers inspect result, refresh, node detail, rerun branches, or cancellation through run/node/rerun handles. | Run lists, branch lists, and history-style reads use typed list results or caller-selected limits where exposed. | Retry, rerun, and cancel require sourced run/node/rerun ids, reviewed rerun mode, cancellation reason, and explicit user decision. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Synchronous create/test/update/enable/disable/delete design operations. | Operator test returns typed test result or explicit error; no background completion is assumed. | Operator/template list reads use backend typed list results when exposed. | Code/schema changes, enable/disable, template update, and delete require reviewed design input and explicit user decision. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Synchronous publish/enable/disable/invoke for one versioned WorkItem service. | Invocation returns the typed invoke result or explicit error; no async polling is provided for service calls. | Service catalog/list discovery uses typed WorkItem metadata when exposed. | Publish, enable/disable, concurrency, rate limit, timeout, and invoke payload require sourced node/version metadata plus explicit user decision. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Read lineage/revision state plus rerun from artifact nodes or blocks. | Rerun is long-running through workflow/artifact handles; callers inspect rerun result/state through typed handles. | Output blocks, revisions, node blocks, and lineage lists use typed list results or caller-selected review scope. | Effective revision switch and node/block rerun require sourced artifact/node/block/revision ids and explicit review decision. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Synchronous create/read/update/delete/import/export/validate semantic operations. | Validation/import/export return typed results or explicit errors; no invented polling is allowed. | Entry lists and import/export payloads use typed result lists or reviewed caller-provided payloads. | Model/entry delete, import, validation, and spec updates require sourced ids, reviewed specs, table bindings, and explicit user decision. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector configuration, source browsing, file preview/stream, import/export task lifecycle. | Import/export tasks are long-running stateful operations; pause/resume/retry/state reads use import/export task handles. | Source DB/schema/table lists, connector files, task files/runs, preview rows, and export files use typed lists or caller-selected limits. | Credentials, storage config, task create/update/delete, pause/resume/retry, and export targets require secret storage, typed metadata, UI state, or explicit user input. |
| Use product agents through A2A | A2A synchronous send, streaming, task status read, cancel, resubscribe, and input-submit lifecycle. | Streaming and resubscribe are explicit A2A interaction modes; task status/cancel/input-submit use caller-generated request ids and prior task/turn ids. | A2A task/message collections use returned typed task/message envelopes; promoted facade does not infer history pagination. | Cancel and input-submit require sourced task/turn ids and explicit user decision; Agent resource CRUD/runtime proxy stays route-aligned. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Synchronous provider/backend/router read/write setup, endpoint management, model probing, and router update. | Probe/model discovery returns typed results or explicit errors; no background provider state is assumed. | Backend/model/endpoint lists use typed metadata collections or caller-selected provider family. | Endpoint status, secrets, backend delete/update, router strategy, and provider family require configuration, secret storage, typed metadata, or explicit user input. |
| Manage compute resources | Compute create/read/update/preflight/action/delete lifecycle. | Suspend/resume/retry are long-running action-style operations; callers inspect action results through compute handles. | Resource/spec lists use typed results or caller-selected page/limit scope when exposed. | Delete requires preflight result plus explicit force/delete decision; sizing, image/spec, suspend/resume/retry choices remain caller-owned. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Data-share publish/update/delete/subscribe/unsubscribe and workspace summary reads. | Subscribe/unsubscribe and publication mutations return typed results or explicit errors; no background share success is assumed. | Share summary, publish/subscription lists, object tables, and target workspace discovery use typed results or caller-selected scope. | Publish/delete/subscribe/unsubscribe and target workspace/object choices require typed discovery results, UI state, or explicit user input. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | SQL metadata browse, workbook/version editing, query execution, polling, result/profile, cancellation. | Query execution is long-running when backend returns query/statement ids; callers poll/query detail or fetch statement result/profile through handles. | Metadata lists, workbook/version/history, statement results, and previews use typed list/result shapes or caller-selected page limits. | SQL text, execution context, workbook/version mutations, query kill, and table/database drop require sourced ids and explicit user decision. |

### User Story Execution Budget Coverage

Use this table after Interaction Mode Coverage and before Pagination and Partial Result Coverage so AI-written code does not poll, stream, paginate, retry, or wait without an explicit budget. Every promoted task must state timeout/deadline source, polling/stream stop condition, page/batch limit, retry budget, and approval/escalation boundary. Code entries in execution budget cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If the budget is missing, code must use the SDK/client timeout, caller-provided limit, or stop and ask for an explicit budget instead of running unbounded loops or background work. SDK clients never transport-retry HTTP requests; callers that need retry must implement it at the business layer after confirming safety; write, runtime, destructive, and external side-effect calls are never transport-retried by the SDK request layer.

| User task | Timeout/deadline source | Polling/stream stop condition | Page/batch limit | Retry budget | Approval/escalation boundary |
|-----------|-------------------------|-------------------------------|------------------|--------------|------------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Client timeout, context deadline, or caller auth/bootstrap deadline. | No polling or streaming; stop after account, workspace, or DB connection call returns result or error. | Workspace and user list reads use caller page size, backend default, or selected workspace scope; no full account scan. | Retry transient endpoint, auth, or DB reads only within caller retry count and deadline. | Ask before creating another workspace or retrying with changed endpoint, workspace name, or auth. |
| Manage workspace members | Context/client timeout plus caller list, audit, or mutation deadline. | No streaming; stop after the member result, audit read, or explicit error. | Member and audit lists use caller page/size/window; no unbounded audit scan. | Retry transient calls only within an explicit budget and with unchanged member and role ids. | Ask before broad role-binding replacement, status changes, deletion, or continuing after permission denial. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Context/client timeout plus caller load/status/read deadline. | Poll load/status only until terminal status, timeout/deadline, or caller max polls. | Table data, preview, overview, and tree lists use page size, preview rows, or selected scope; no full table scan. | Retry transient reads/status with same handles; do not retry load/truncate/delete without approval. | Ask before destructive table operations or extending long load/status polling. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Upload/download/preview/trigger deadline from context, client, or caller transfer budget. | Stop upload/download/stream/trigger wait on result, error, EOF, terminal status, or deadline. | File/ref/preview/connector-file lists use caller page, limit, offset, or preview rows. | Retry transient upload/download/preview only with same bytes/path/handles and retry count; do not repeat trigger/delete without approval. | Ask before large upload, workflow trigger, delete/clean, or extending transfer time. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Run/rerun/cancel/status deadline from context, client, or caller workflow budget. | Poll run/rerun until terminal/cancelled status, max polls, or deadline. | Run, branch, node, and history lists use caller page/limit or selected run scope. | Retry status reads within count; retry run/rerun only with explicit confirmation and budget for same DSL/input. | Ask before rerun, retry, cancel, or extended workflow wait. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Create/test/update deadline from context, client, or caller design/test budget. | Operator test waits only until test result, error, or deadline; no background wait after response. | Operator/template lists use caller page/limit or selected design scope. | Retry reads and test result fetches with same spec/input; do not retry create/update/enable/delete without approval. | Ask before expensive test, enable/disable/delete, or extending test wait. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Publish/invoke deadline from context, client, or caller service timeout. | Invoke stops on result, error, or deadline; no async polling unless a typed result handle exists. | WorkItem service catalog/list reads use caller page/limit or selected node/version scope. | Retry info/invoke transport within count and same payload; do not duplicate publish/invoke without approval or idempotency. | Ask before publish, enable/disable, re-invoke, or increasing concurrency, rate, or timeout. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Lineage read/rerun deadline from context, client, or caller review budget. | Rerun/status waits until terminal status, max polls, or deadline. | Blocks, revisions, node outputs, and lineage lists use review scope, page, or limit. | Retry lineage reads/status with same artifact/node ids; retry rerun/switch only with confirmation. | Ask before revision switch, rerun, or extended lineage wait. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Validation/import/export/read deadline from context, client, or caller semantic budget. | Validation/import/export stops on result, error, or deadline; no invented polling. | Entry lists and import/export payloads are bounded by caller size, page, or limit. | Retry reads and validation fetches within count; do not retry import/export/update/delete without approval. | Ask before bulk import/export/delete or extending validation. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector/source/task deadline from context, client, or caller transfer budget. | Import/export task status polling stops on terminal status, max polls, or deadline. | Source DB/schema/table, files, runs, and preview rows use caller page, limit, or source scope. | Retry source/status reads within count; task retry/resume/pause only with explicit budget and same task id. | Ask before external transfer, retry failed files, resume/delete, or increasing scan/page limits. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Send/stream/task deadline from context, client, or caller conversation budget. | Stream/resubscribe stops on final event, EOF, error, or deadline; task polling stops on terminal status, max polls, or deadline. | Task/message collections are bounded by result scope or caller limit; no history scan. | Retry task status/resubscribe within count; send/input-submit retry only with same request id and budget. | Ask before duplicate send, cancel, input-submit, or long stream. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Probe/backend/router deadline from context, client, or caller provider budget. | Probe/model list stops on result, error, or deadline; no background health loop. | Backend/model/endpoint lists are bounded by provider family, backend scope, page, or limit. | Retry probe/read within count; do not repeat create/update/delete/router change without approval. | Ask before repeated external probes, deleting backend, or increasing timeout. |
| Manage compute resources | Create/action/preflight deadline from context, client, or caller compute budget. | Action polling stops on terminal status, max polls, or deadline; read stops by requested window. | Resource/spec/image lists and windows use caller limit, page, size, or bounded time window. | Retry detail/action status within count; do not retry create/delete/action without approval. | Ask before cost-affecting action, force delete, or extended action wait. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Share/list/name-check deadline from context, client, or caller sharing budget. | No streaming; stop after summary/name/list/mutation read-back result or error. | Publish, subscription, object, and target lists use page, limit, or selected target scope. | Retry reads/name-check within count; do not repeat publish/subscribe/delete without approval. | Ask before broad target scan, publish/subscribe/delete, or increasing list scope. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | SQL execute/poll/result deadline from context, client, or caller query budget. | Query polling stops on terminal/cancelled status, max polls, or deadline; result fetch stops after page/limit. | Metadata, history, result, statement, and preview reads use page, limit, or selected workbook/query scope. | Retry metadata/status/result reads within count; execute retry only with same SQL and explicit confirmation/budget. | Ask before long query, kill/drop, re-execute, or broad result export. |

### User Story Pagination and Partial Result Coverage

Use this table after Execution Budget Coverage and before Error Recovery Coverage so AI-written code distinguishes complete results from first pages, previews, partial streams, capped batches, and caller-limited reads. Every promoted task must state paginated/partial surfaces, continuation token/source, complete-result proof, forbidden partial-result shortcut, and AI-safe pagination behavior. Code entries in pagination and partial-result cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If complete coverage cannot be proven within the documented budget, code must return the explicit partial result boundary and ask before continuing.

| User task | Paginated/partial surfaces | Continuation token/source | Complete-result proof | Forbidden partial-result shortcut | AI-safe pagination behavior |
|-----------|----------------------------|---------------------------|-----------------------|-----------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Workspace and account/user list reads may be page-scoped; single account/profile and workspace bind reads are not paginated. | Page, limit, selected workspace id, or none for single read results. | Requested page/scope is returned, or workspace/user list next page is exhausted. | Do not treat the first workspace page or one profile read as all workspaces/users. | Return whether the result is a requested page or complete list; ask before scanning all account/workspace pages. |
| Manage workspace members | Member lists and supported general audit-log lists can be paginated or windowed. | Page, limit, window, or continuation data from typed results. | The requested scope is exhausted or the requested page/window is explicitly returned. | Do not treat the first member or audit page as complete workspace state. | State page/window boundaries, return partial results when capped, and ask before a broad member inventory scan. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Catalog/database/table trees, child lists, table data, previews, overviews, and downloads may be paginated, previewed, or streamed. | Catalog/database/table refs, page, limit, preview rows, download handle, or typed next token. | All requested pages are exhausted, requested preview/page is returned, or download stream terminates cleanly. | Do not treat a preview, sample rows, or first table-data page as the full table. | Label previews versus complete reads, return continuation data, and ask before full-table export or unbounded tree scans. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Volume/folder/file/ref lists, file previews, connector processed files, Excel previews, and download streams can be partial. | Volume/folder/file refs, page, limit, offset, preview rows, stream EOF, or next token. | List next page is exhausted, requested preview/page is returned, or download stream reaches EOF. | Do not treat preview rows, first file page, or a signed preview link as full file content. | Return partial boundaries and continuation handles; ask before expanding folder scans, previews, or downloads beyond the budget. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow/run/node/history/branch lists, logs, result pages, and streaming status can be partial. | Workflow/run/node/rerun handles, page/limit, log cursor, branch cursor, stream final event, or terminal status. | Terminal run/rerun status, exhausted list/log pages, or requested page/scope is returned. | Do not treat partial logs, first run page, or a non-terminal stream chunk as complete workflow result. | Report terminal versus partial status, return next cursor/page, and ask before continuing logs or status polling after budget. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator/template lists and test output collections may be paginated; single test result is one typed read. | Page/limit/template id/operator id, test result handle, or none for single test result. | Requested list page is returned, list pages are exhausted, or the single test result is complete. | Do not treat first template/operator page or partial test output as all design artifacts. | Return list scope and page state; ask before broad template/operator inventory or extended test output collection. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Service catalog/list discovery can be paginated; invoke result may be a partial payload only when the service contract says so. | Page/limit/node/version/service handle, invoke result state, or contract-defined continuation. | Requested service page is returned or exhausted; invoke result has contract completion status. | Do not treat first service page as all invokable services or partial invoke output as final service output. | Surface service list pages and invoke completion state; ask before re-invoking or expanding service discovery. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Output blocks, node blocks, revisions, artifact graphs, and rerun status lists may be page/block scoped. | Artifact/node/block/revision handles, block cursor, revision cursor, page/limit, rerun handle, or terminal status. | All requested blocks/revisions/pages are loaded, requested scope is returned, or rerun status is terminal. | Do not treat the first block, revision page, or partial graph as complete lineage. | Return block/revision/page boundaries, preserve continuation handles, and ask before expanding artifact graph reads. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Semantic model/entry lists, import/export payloads, validation diagnostics, and entry pages can be partial. | Model/entry ids, page/limit, import/export result handle, validation diagnostic cursor, or entry next token. | Requested entry/diagnostic page is returned, all pages are exhausted, or import/export result is complete. | Do not treat first entry page, diagnostic sample, or partial export payload as the complete semantic model. | Return partial model/entry boundaries and ask before continuing import/export, validation, or full-model reads. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Source database/schema/table/file lists, task files/runs, preview rows, retry-file lists, and transfer status can be partial. | Connector/task/file handles, page/limit, run cursor, preview row limit, next token, or terminal task status. | Requested page/preview is returned, list pages are exhausted, or task status reaches terminal state. | Do not treat a preview, first run page, or partial task-file list as complete connector/task state. | Return continuation tokens and partial status; ask before expanding external source scans, previews, or retry-file reads. |
| Use product agents through A2A | A2A message/task collections, streaming events, resubscribe output, and input-required state can be partial. | Request id, task id, message id, stream final event/EOF, resubscribe cursor, or task status. | Final stream event/EOF, terminal task status, or requested message/task page is returned. | Do not treat first stream chunk, resubscribe page, or non-terminal task event as final agent output. | Return task/message state and partial stream boundary; ask before continuing long streams or resubscribe loops. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Provider/backend/model/endpoint/router lists, model probes, and endpoint status reads may be page- or family-scoped. | Provider family, backend/router id, page/limit, model list token, or probe/status result state. | Requested family/page is returned, pages are exhausted, or probe/status result is complete. | Do not treat first model page, provider family sample, or partial probe output as all models/providers. | Return selected scope and page state; ask before broad provider/model discovery or repeated probes. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource/spec/image lists and windows can be page-, limit-, or time-window scoped. | Resource/spec/image ids, page/limit/size time window, or action/status handle. | Requested list page or window is returned, pages are exhausted, or action status is terminal. | Do not treat a sample, first resource page, or partial image list as complete capacity/state. | Return requested window/page and next continuation; ask before expanding range or resource inventory. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Share summary, publication/subscription/object/target-workspace lists, and subscriber reads can be paginated. | Publish/subscription/object ids, target workspace id, page/limit, selected scope, or next token. | Requested scope/page is returned or publication/subscription/object pages are exhausted. | Do not treat first subscription page, object sample, or target list page as all share state. | Return page/scope state and ask before broad target scans, all-subscriber reads, or expanding object lists. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Metadata trees, workbook/version/history lists, statement result rows, previews, profiles, and polling reads may be partial. | Metadata handle, workbook/query/statement id, page/limit, row cursor, history cursor, profile handle, or terminal query status. | Result row pages/history pages are exhausted, requested page is returned, or query status is terminal. | Do not treat the first result page, preview rows, partial metadata tree, or non-terminal poll as the complete SQL result. | Return row/page/history boundaries and terminal state; ask before full result export, wider metadata scans, or continuing polling after budget. |

### User Story Error Recovery Coverage

Use this table after Pagination and Partial Result Coverage and before Permission and Scope Coverage so AI-written code handles failures explicitly instead of adding fallback, silent repair, default values, or route-level bypasses. Every promoted task must state retryable failures, user-correctable failures, forbidden recovery behavior, and the AI-safe next action. Code entries in error recovery cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If a failure is not listed here, code must return the explicit error with context and stop.

| User task | Retryable failures | User-correctable failures | Forbidden recovery | AI-safe next action |
|-----------|--------------------|---------------------------|--------------------|---------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Retry transient product endpoint, auth transport, and DB connection reads only after preserving the original error context. | Duplicate workspace names, missing endpoint/API key, expired session, or invalid workspace id require explicit user or application correction. | Do not silently choose a different workspace, create a fallback workspace, downgrade to core catalog, or infer workspace id from name. | Return the explicit error or ask the user/application for corrected auth, endpoint, workspace name, or workspace id before retrying. |
| Manage workspace members | Retry transient member read or status queries while the same sourced ids remain valid; general audit reads use `WorkspaceMemberService.AuditLogs`. | Missing member ids, invalid canonical role ids, rejected role updates, and unauthorized actions require corrected input or approval. | Do not invent role ids, broaden bindings, reuse another member's bindings, or suppress IAM failures. | Return the explicit IAM error with member context and ask for corrected input or authorized access. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Retry transient catalog tree, table data, preview, or download reads; retry load/status reads only with the same table handle and file URL. | Missing catalog/database/table ids, invalid schema, bad file URL, duplicate names, and destructive table choices require reviewed input. | Do not infer ids from names, auto-rewrite schema, load a different file, truncate/delete after failure, or bypass catalog handles. | Return the catalog/table error and ask for corrected schema, file URL, selected table, or explicit destructive approval. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Retry transient upload, preview, download, refs, and file-list reads when the same file bytes/path and handles are retained. | Missing volume/folder/file ids, invalid local path, unsupported file content, rename conflicts, and delete/clean choices require user correction. | Do not parse ids from paths/preview links, upload a substitute file, auto-rename, auto-clean, or hide failed workflow triggers. | Return the file/folder error with volume/file context and ask for corrected path, selected handle, name, or approval. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Retry transient run status/result/node detail/rerun branch reads; retry run/rerun only when caller explicitly confirms same DSL/input. | Invalid DSL, missing workflow/run/node/rerun ids, failed run inputs, and cancel/rerun decisions require reviewed user input. | Do not invent ids, auto-rerun failed nodes, skip failed nodes, change DSL/input, or mark a run successful after partial failure. | Return the workflow error with workflow/run/node context and ask for corrected DSL/input/id, rerun mode, or cancel decision. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Retry transient operator/template info/code/list reads and test result fetches with the same spec/input. | Invalid operator spec, schema/code/test payload errors, duplicate identifiers, and enable/delete decisions require reviewed design changes. | Do not auto-rewrite code/schema, fill missing spec fields, disable/delete after test failure, or route around spec validation. | Return the design error and ask for corrected spec, code, schema, DSL, test input, or lifecycle decision. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Retry transient service info and invoke transport failures when the same node/version/payload is retained. | Missing node/version, invalid publish limits, unavailable compute resource, bad service type/name, and invalid payload require correction. | Do not infer version/service fields, publish with default compute, rewrite payload, or treat error-only enable/disable as returned service data. | Return the service error with node/version context and ask for corrected publish spec, compute resource, or invoke payload. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Retry transient lineage overview/block/revision/node reads and rerun status reads with the same artifact/node ids. | Missing artifact/node/block/revision ids, invalid revision switch, and rerun mode choices require reviewed lineage selection. | Do not parse rendered labels, invent revisions, auto-switch effective revisions, or rerun a different node after failure. | Return the lineage error with artifact/node/block context and ask for corrected selection, revision switch, or rerun decision. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Retry transient model info/entry list/validation result/import/export reads with the same model/entry ids. | Invalid entry specs, missing model/entry ids, table binding errors, import payload errors, and delete decisions require correction. | Do not infer ids from names, auto-rewrite semantic specs, silently drop invalid entries, or validate against a different table. | Return the semantic error and ask for corrected model/entry id, spec, table binding, import payload, or delete approval. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Retry transient source browse, target metadata, preview/download, task state, run list, and retry-file reads with same connector/task ids. | Invalid credentials, storage config, connector source type, mappings, volume/file ids, task specs, and pause/resume/delete choices require correction. | Do not generate credentials, copy storage config from another module, rewrite mappings, skip failed files silently, or fabricate task success. | Return the connector/task error and ask for corrected credentials, mapping, storage config, selected files, task id, or action decision. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Retry transient agent card reads, task status reads, stream resubscribe, and input-submit transport errors with the same request/task ids. | Missing agent code/id, request/message/task/turn ids, invalid input payload, and cancel/input decisions require correction. | Do not promote resource proxy fallback, invent task ids, rewrite messages, submit guessed input, or fabricate task status. | Return the A2A error and ask for corrected agent selector, request/message/task id, text, turn id, or input payload. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Retry transient provider/backend list, model probe, endpoint status read, and router read operations. | Invalid endpoint URL, secrets, backend ids, model names, endpoint status, router strategy, and delete choices require corrected config. | Do not write secrets to core directly, infer backend ids from logs, choose default models, or bypass provider handles. | Return the provider error and ask for corrected endpoint, secret, backend id, model list, status, or router strategy. |
| Manage compute resources | Retry transient resource detail, spec/image reads, action status, and preflight reads with the same resource id. | Invalid resource/spec ids, worker image, sizing values, replica bounds, action choice, and delete/force approval require correction. | Do not infer resource ids from image names, force delete after failed preflight, or resize with default values. | Return the compute error and ask for corrected resource/spec/image, sizing, action, or delete approval. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Retry transient summary/name-check/publication/subscription reads and subscribe status reads with same target choices. | Invalid publish/subscription ids, object ids, locations, target workspace, name conflict, and unsubscribe/delete choices require correction. | Do not infer target workspace, publish a different object, auto-subscribe, or hide failed unsubscribe/delete. | Return the data-share error and ask for corrected object/location/target workspace, publish/subscription id, or share decision. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Retry transient metadata/history/result/detail/profile reads and polling with the same query/statement ids. | SQL syntax errors, invalid database/table names, missing workbook/version/query/statement ids, and cancel/drop decisions require correction. | Do not derive ids from SQL text, rewrite SQL, drop/kill after failure without approval, or switch to streaming transport fallback. | Return the SQL error with statement/query context and ask for corrected SQL, selected metadata, statement id, or cancel/drop approval. |

### User Story Permission and Scope Coverage

Use this table after Error Recovery Coverage and before Ownership and Collaboration Coverage so AI-written code keeps product endpoint, workspace scope, and RBAC boundaries explicit. Every promoted task must state product scope, permission-sensitive operations, caller-owned authorization boundary, forbidden scope/RBAC behavior, and AI-safe permission handling. Code entries in permission and scope cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If permission or scope is unclear, code must stop and ask for explicit authorization or a sourced workspace/resource selection instead of bypassing RBAC.

| User task | Product scope | Permission-sensitive operations | Caller-owned authorization boundary | Forbidden scope/RBAC behavior | AI-safe permission handling |
|-----------|---------------|----------------------------------|-------------------------------------|-------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Product endpoint account scope plus selected workspace scope. | Workspace create/claim, API key, and DB connection reads are product-RBAC governed. | Endpoint, API key/session, workspace name, and workspace id come from configuration, UI selection, or explicit input. | Do not bypass product RBAC with core catalog, reuse another workspace id, or silently switch accounts. | Return auth/scope errors and ask for corrected product endpoint, credentials, workspace name, or workspace id. |
| Manage workspace members | Workspace-scoped member administration through product IAM. | Member creation, role-binding replacement, status changes, deletion, and protected audit reads require their canonical IAM actions. | The member target, canonical role ids, and approval decisions remain caller-owned. | Do not broaden bindings, invent role ids, cross workspace boundaries, or ignore unauthorized responses. | Return IAM errors with workspace/member context and ask for authorized access or corrected input. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Workspace catalog/database/table scope through product routes. | Table create/load/truncate/delete/download and catalog/database mutations require workspace catalog permission. | Catalog/database/table ids, schemas, file URLs, and destructive decisions come from typed results, UI state, or explicit input. | Do not bypass product routes with core catalog, use another workspace catalog id, or run destructive table actions without approval. | Return permission/scope errors and ask for corrected catalog selection, schema, file URL, or destructive approval. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Workspace catalog/database/volume/file scope. | Upload/download/preview, workflow attach/trigger, folder clean/delete, and file delete require workspace file permission. | File bytes/paths, volume/folder/file ids, workflow ids, and delete/clean choices remain caller-owned. | Do not read local paths implicitly, move files across workspace scope, or hide denied workflow/file operations. | Return file permission errors with volume/file context and ask for corrected handle, path, workflow id, or approval. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workspace workflow and execution scope. | Deploy/update/run/rerun/cancel/delete and result inspection require workflow permission for the workspace. | Workflow DSL/input, run/node/rerun ids, rerun mode, and cancel decisions come from reviewed input or typed results. | Do not run in another workspace, auto-rerun without approval, bypass workflow handles, or mask denied execution access. | Return workflow permission errors with workflow/run/node context and ask for corrected scope or explicit run/rerun/cancel approval. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Workspace workflow-design scope. | Operator/template create/update/test/enable/disable/delete require design permission. | Code, schema, DSL, template/operator ids, and lifecycle decisions are reviewed caller-owned inputs. | Do not auto-enable/delete, route around spec validation, or publish design changes across workspace scope. | Return design permission errors and ask for corrected operator/template selection, reviewed spec, or lifecycle approval. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Workspace WorkItem API service scope for one node/version. | Publish/enable/disable/invoke require service and compute permissions. | Node id, version, service name/type, compute resource, and invoke payload come from WorkItem metadata or explicit input. | Do not publish with default compute, infer version, invoke unauthorized services, or treat enable errors as success. | Return service permission errors with node/version context and ask for corrected publish spec, compute approval, or payload. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Workspace artifact, node, block, and revision scope. | Revision switch, revision create, rerun, and artifact output access require lineage/workflow permission. | Artifact/node/block/revision ids and rerun/revision-switch approvals come from lineage results, UI state, or explicit input. | Do not switch revisions across artifacts, rerun unselected nodes, or expose artifact output from another workspace. | Return lineage permission errors and ask for corrected artifact/node/block/revision selection or rerun approval. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Workspace semantic model and entry scope. | Model/entry create/update/delete/import/export/validate require semantic permission and source-table visibility. | Model/entry ids, specs, imports, table bindings, and delete approvals remain caller-owned. | Do not validate against inaccessible tables, drop invalid entries silently, or mutate models across workspace scope. | Return semantic permission errors and ask for corrected model/entry selection, table binding, payload, or approval. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Workspace connector, credential, file, import task, and export task scope. | Connector update/delete, credential-backed source browsing, import/export create/update/pause/resume/retry/delete require connector permission. | Credentials, storage config, mappings, volume/file/task ids, and task actions come from secret storage, typed results, UI state, or explicit input. | Do not copy credentials across modules, bypass secret ownership, skip denied files, or fabricate task success. | Return connector permission errors and ask for corrected credentials, mapping, storage config, selected files, task id, or action approval. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Workspace agent selector and A2A task scope. | Message send/stream, task get/cancel/resubscribe, and input submit require agent/A2A permission. | Agent code/id, request/message ids, task/turn ids, text, and input payload remain caller-owned or result-owned. | Do not fallback to Agent resource proxy, invent task ids, submit guessed input, or cross agent selector scope. | Return A2A permission errors and ask for corrected agent selector, message/task id, turn id, text, or input approval. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Workspace provider/backend/router scope. | Backend create/update/delete, endpoint management, probing, secrets, and router update require provider administration permission. | Secrets, endpoints, backend ids, model lists, status values, and routing strategy come from config, secret storage, metadata, or explicit input. | Do not write secrets outside product routes, choose default models, bypass provider handles, or leak credentials in errors. | Return provider permission errors and ask for corrected secret, endpoint, backend id, model list, status, or router approval. |
| Manage compute resources | Workspace compute resource scope. | Create/update/preflight/delete, suspend/resume/retry, and force delete require compute permission. | Resource/spec/image ids, sizing, action choices scope, and force/delete decisions are caller-owned. | Do not force delete after denied preflight, resize with defaults, infer resource ids from, or cross workspace scope. | Return compute permission errors and ask for corrected resource/spec/image, sizing, action scope, or delete approval. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Workspace data-share publication/subscription and target workspace scope. | Publish/update/delete/subscribe/unsubscribe require share owner/subscriber permission. | Object ids, locations, target workspaces, publish/subscription ids, and share choices come from typed results, UI state, or explicit input. | Do not infer target workspace, publish inaccessible objects, auto-subscribe, or hide denied unsubscribe/delete. | Return data-share permission errors and ask for corrected object/location/target workspace, publish/subscription id, or share approval. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Workspace SQL metadata, workbook, query, and statement scope. | SQL execution, workbook edits, query kill, statement result/profile, and table/database drop require SQL/workbook permission. | SQL text, database/table/workbook/query/statement ids, execution context, and drop/kill decisions remain caller-owned. | Do not bypass SQL product routes, derive scope from SQL text, execute/drop in another workspace, or kill without approval. | Return SQL permission errors with statement/query context and ask for corrected metadata, SQL text, statement id, or cancel/drop approval. |

### User Story Ownership and Collaboration Coverage

Use this table after Permission and Scope Coverage and before Input Construction Coverage so AI-written code does not treat workspace/RBAC permission as caller ownership in shared workspaces. Every promoted task must state ownership evidence, collaborator/shared scope, caller-owned decision boundary, forbidden cross-user shortcut, and AI-safe collaboration behavior. Code entries in ownership and collaboration cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If ownership or collaborator scope cannot be proven from typed SDK state, UI-selected state, or explicit user input, code must stop and ask instead of modifying, exposing, deleting, sharing, or continuing another user's resource.

| User task | Ownership evidence | Collaborator/shared scope | Caller-owned decision boundary | Forbidden cross-user shortcut | AI-safe collaboration behavior |
|-----------|--------------------|---------------------------|--------------------------------|-------------------------------|--------------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Account profile, current user id, selected workspace id/name, workspace membership, and role state. | Shared workspace membership, organization accounts, and DB connection visibility can affect other users. | Caller owns workspace create/switch/bind decisions and DB connection exposure only for the selected account/workspace. | Do not assume a default workspace, another user's membership, or account visibility proves ownership. | Show current user, workspace, membership, and role evidence; ask before switching, creating, binding, or exposing workspace resources. |
| Manage workspace members | Current principal, target member detail, canonical role bindings, and supported general audit results. | IAM mutations affect another member's workspace access. | The caller owns role-binding, status, and delete decisions only with explicit authority and a selected member id. | Do not mutate on behalf of another administrator or select members by similar names. | Show the member id and current bindings, then require explicit approval before mutation. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Catalog/database/table detail, owner/creator fields when returned, typed handles, UI-selected resource, and table refs. | Shared catalogs and tables may be used by workflows, Explore, connectors, SQL, and other users. | Caller owns load/truncate/delete/download decisions only for the selected table and reviewed file/data source. | Do not delete, truncate, load, or expose a shared table because workspace permission exists or a name matches. | Show selected catalog/database/table, refs, and owner evidence when available; ask before shared table mutation or exposure. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Volume/folder/file info, upload result, creator fields when returned, file refs, UI-selected path, and typed handles. | Shared volumes, folders, files, and signed links may be referenced by workflows, connectors, agents, and other users. | Caller owns file bytes, path, link exposure, workflow trigger, clean, and delete decisions only for selected or uploaded files. | Do not clean or delete shared folders/files, another user's folders/files, or files not created, uploaded, or explicitly selected by the caller. | Show volume/folder/file ids, path, creator/ref evidence, and link scope; ask before shared file cleanup, deletion, trigger, or exposure. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow/run detail, owner/creator fields when returned, run initiator, UI-selected workflow/run ids, and typed handles. | Shared workflow definitions, executions, node outputs, and external node side effects may affect team work. | Caller owns deploy/update/run/rerun/cancel/delete decisions only for selected workflows/runs and approved inputs. | Do not cancel, delete, or rerun another user's run or a shared workflow selected only by display name. | Show workflow/run owner, initiator, status, and selected ids; ask when the run is not caller-created or explicitly selected. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator/template detail, owner/creator fields when returned, code/test result, enabled state, and selected ids. | Shared operators and templates may be consumed by workflows and other designers. | Caller owns code, schema, test, enable/disable, update, and delete decisions only for selected design artifacts. | Do not update, enable, disable, or delete a shared operator/template from a broad design request. | Show artifact owner, consumers when available, validation/test state, and selected ids; ask before shared design lifecycle changes. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Service/node/version detail, publisher/owner fields when returned, enabled state, and selected node/version. | Shared service endpoints and invocations may be used by other users and workflows. | Caller owns publish/enable/disable/invoke payload decisions only for the selected service version and explicit payload. | Do not disable/unpublish a shared service or invoke it with another user's payload or guessed version. | Show service owner/version/limits/state evidence; ask before lifecycle changes or invoking shared endpoints. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact/node/block/revision detail, producing workflow/run, owner/creator fields when returned, and selected lineage handles. | Shared artifacts, revisions, and outputs may be visible to team members and downstream workflows. | Caller owns revision switch, rerun, and output exposure decisions only for selected artifacts/nodes/blocks. | Do not expose, switch, or rerun another user's artifact/output based on a similar node or block label. | Show artifact/run/owner/revision evidence and selected handles; ask before revision changes, reruns, or output exposure. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector/task/file detail, credential owner or secret boundary, task creator/state, and selected source/target ids. | Connectors and import/export tasks move external data into shared files/tables and may use shared credentials. | Caller owns credentials, mappings, storage config, selected files, and task actions only when explicitly supplied or selected. | Do not reuse another user's credentials/config/task or retry/delete shared tasks from broad task wording. | Show connector/task owner, source-target mapping, credential boundary, and task state; ask before shared data movement or task lifecycle changes. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent handle/card, task/message/request ids, task creator/session when returned, and selected conversation context. | Agent tools, messages, tasks, and streamed outputs may be shared or contain collaborator data. | Caller owns message text, input payload, cancel, and resubscribe decisions only for selected request/task/message ids. | Do not submit input or cancel on another user's task, invent answers, or continue a session from similar ids. | Show task/message/request ownership, current state, and selected agent; ask before continuing or canceling shared agent work. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Provider/backend/router detail, secret owner/redacted state, endpoint validation, and selected backend/router ids. | Shared providers and routers affect workspace model traffic, secrets, and downstream AI calls. | Caller owns secret, endpoint, router, probe, status, update, and delete decisions only with explicit admin/operator intent. | Do not rotate/delete secrets or change routers based on model name, logs, or another user's secret. | Show backend/router owner, shared traffic impact, redacted secret state, and endpoint evidence; ask before provider/router mutation. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource detail, creator/owner fields when returned, workload/preflight state, and selected resource id. | Shared compute resources may host workflows, services, and other users' workloads. | Caller owns sizing, action, force, delete, and retry decisions only for selected resources with approved impact. | Do not resize, suspend, retry, force delete, or delete shared compute from image names or alone. | Show resource owner, workloads, preflight result, and selected id; ask before actions that affect shared capacity. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Publication/subscription detail, source object owner, target workspace, name-check result, and selected share ids. | Data shares expose data across workspaces and affect subscribers, governance, and shared object visibility. | Caller owns publish/subscribe/unsubscribe/update/delete decisions only for selected source object and target workspace. | Do not share, delete, or unsubscribe on behalf of another workspace/user or from a broad object name. | Show source owner, target workspace, subscriber, governance, and name-check evidence; ask before cross-workspace sharing changes. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Workbook/version/query/statement detail, query owner, SQL metadata, history, and selected statement ids. | Shared workbooks, query history, results, and SQL mutations may affect shared databases and collaborators. | Caller owns execute/cancel/drop/kill/result exposure decisions only for selected SQL/workbook/query/statement ids. | Do not execute, cancel, kill, or mutate another user's query or shared database from display labels or SQL text alone. | Show workbook/query owner, database/table scope, statement status, and selected ids; ask before shared SQL execution, mutation, or result exposure. |

### User Story Input Construction Coverage

Use this table after Ownership and Collaboration Coverage and before Value Format and Units Coverage so AI-written code constructs specs, options, enum values, secrets, and payloads from explicit sources instead of guessing. Every promoted task must state required caller inputs, derived typed-result inputs, option/enum/secret rules, forbidden construction behavior, and AI-safe construction. Code entries in input construction cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If an input source is not listed here or in the Handle ID Source Matrix, code must stop and ask for explicit input instead of filling defaults, rewriting values, or inferring from labels.

| User task | Required caller inputs | Derived typed-result inputs | Option/enum/secret rules | Forbidden construction behavior | AI-safe construction |
|-----------|------------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------------|---------------------------------|----------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Product endpoint, auth token/API key, workspace name for ensure, or explicit workspace id for bind. | Account profile, workspace list/create result, workspace users, and DB connection result feed later handles. | Endpoint and API keys come from configuration or secret storage; workspace name/id are not defaulted. | Do not invent endpoint, token, workspace id, or default workspace name; do not parse ids from display names. | Build client from explicit endpoint/auth, call ensure/list/create, then construct workspace handles only from typed result or explicit id. |
| Manage workspace members | Member display data, email when required, canonical role ids, status action, and delete approval. | Typed member/list results supply member ids and existing bindings; supported general audit results supply audit context. | Role ids and status actions come from canonical IAM results, UI state, or explicit input. | Do not invent role ids, auto-assign administrator, infer ids from labels, or fill missing identity/status values. | Construct member options from reviewed caller values and typed IAM ids; ask for missing targets or decisions. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Catalog/database/table names, table columns, file URL/bytes, preview/download/load/truncate/delete decisions. | Catalog/database/table/column/list results supply ids, handles, schemas, preview links, and file refs. | Column types, load mode, preview/page limits, and destructive choices are explicit variables or typed result fields. | Do not synthesize schema, rewrite column names/types, infer ids from names, use default file URLs, or auto-truncate/delete. | Construct table/file calls from typed handles plus reviewed schema/file/limit/destructive variables; ask when schema or file source is missing. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Volume/folder/file names, bytes or local path, rename value, workflow id, upload/download/preview/delete/clean decisions. | Catalog/database/volume/folder/file/upload/preview/list results supply scoped handles, ids, paths, and preview links. | Local paths, file bytes, workflow ids, and clean/delete choices are caller-owned; no hidden local file discovery. | Do not read arbitrary local files, invent file bytes, auto-rename on conflict, parse preview URLs as ids, or auto-clean folders. | Construct upload/folder/file requests from explicit path/bytes/name plus typed volume/file handles; ask for missing path, handle, or approval. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow DSL, workflow name, run input, trace/config, node key, rerun mode, cancel/delete decisions. | Deploy/get/list/run/result/node/rerun results supply workflow ids, execution ids, node keys, rerun ids, and result handles. | Rerun mode, run input, trace, and cancel/delete choices are explicit variables; options mirror Go/Python helpers. | Do not generate DSL, change run input, invent node keys, choose rerun mode defaults, or auto-cancel/delete. | Construct workflow specs/options from reviewed DSL/input and typed run/node results; ask for missing DSL, rerun mode, or decision. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator name/identifier/description, input/output schema, code, DSL, test payload, template content, enable/delete decisions. | Operator/template create/get/list/test results supply operator ids, template ids, code, and test result context. | Schema/code/test payload are caller-owned; local validation must fail on missing required fields. | Do not rewrite code/schema, fill missing spec fields, invent identifiers, use raw maps for common paths, or auto-enable/delete. | Construct `CustomOperatorSpec`, `CustomOperatorTestInput`, and template calls from reviewed inputs; ask for missing schema/code/payload. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Node id, service version/name/type, compute resource id, timeout/concurrency/rate limits, invoke payload, enable/disable decisions. | WorkItem metadata/service publish/invoke results supply node/version/service status and response payloads. | Service type/version and compute resource come from metadata, UI state, or explicit input; payload remains caller-owned. | Do not infer version, choose default compute, rewrite payload, or treat enable/disable error-only writes as service data. | Construct publish/invoke specs from explicit service variables and typed node/version metadata; ask for missing compute or payload. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact id, node id, block id, revision ids, revision content, revision switch, rerun mode/input, approval decisions. | Lineage overview/artifact/block/node/revision/rerun results supply handles, output blocks, revision ids, and rerun ids. | Revision switch and rerun specs are caller-owned decisions based on typed lineage results. | Do not invent revisions, parse rendered labels, auto-switch effective revision, or rerun a different node. | Construct lineage/rerun calls from typed artifact/node/block/revision handles and explicit revision/rerun choices. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Model name/id, entry kind/key/spec, table bindings, import payload, export location, validate/delete decisions. | Semantic model/entry/list/validate/import/export results supply model ids, entry ids, table validation context, and export data. | Entry specs and import payloads are caller-owned; table bindings come from catalog/semantic metadata or explicit input. | Do not rewrite semantic specs, infer model ids from names, drop invalid entries, or validate against a different table. | Construct semantic inputs from reviewed spec/table bindings and typed model/entry handles; ask for missing spec, binding, or approval. |
