| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector id/name/type, credentials, storage config, source/table selections, mappings, volume/file/task ids, task action decisions. | Connector/source/target/file/import/export/task/run results supply connector ids, file ids, task ids, previews, mappings, and state. | Credentials and storage config come from secret storage or explicit input; enum values and mappings come from metadata or reviewed input. | Do not generate credentials, copy storage config across modules, rewrite mappings, skip failed files, or default task mode. | Construct connector specs from explicit secrets/config/mappings plus typed connector/file/task results; ask for missing secret or mapping. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent code/id, request/message id, text, task id, turn id, input payload, cancel/resubscribe decisions. | Agent card/send/stream/task/input results supply task ids, turn ids, message envelopes, and stream tokens. | Request/message ids are caller-generated; task/turn ids must come from `AgentA2ATaskID` / `agent_a2a_task_id` and `AgentA2ATurnID` / `agent_a2a_turn_id`; payload remains caller-owned. | Do not invent task ids, rewrite messages, submit guessed input, fallback to resource proxy, or fabricate task state. | Construct `AgentTextMessage`, `AgentTaskRequest`, and `AgentInputSubmission` from explicit text/payload and typed A2A ids. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Provider family, backend id/name, endpoint URL, secrets, model names, endpoint status, router strategy, delete decisions. | Provider/backend/model/probe/router results supply backend ids, endpoint ids, model metadata, probe status, and router state. | Secrets come from secret storage or explicit input; model list/status/router strategy are reviewed caller values. | Do not choose default models, infer backend ids from logs, write secrets outside product routes, or leak secret values in examples. | Construct provider/backend/router calls from typed metadata plus explicit endpoint/secret/model/status/strategy variables. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource name/id, spec/image ids, sizing/replicas, action choice time range, preflight/delete/force decisions. | Compute create/list/detail/spec/image/preflight/action results supply resource ids, spec ids, and action state. | Sizing, image/spec, action window, and force/delete approval are caller-owned. | Do not default sizing, infer ids from image names, force delete after failed preflight, or auto-resize. | Construct compute specs/options from explicit sizing/action variables and typed resource/spec/preflight results. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Publish name, object ids, location, target workspace, subscription id, subscribe/unsubscribe/delete decisions. | Summary/name-check/publication/subscription/object results supply publish ids, subscription ids, objects, conflicts, and target info. | Target workspace/object selections are typed results, UI state, or explicit input; share names are reviewed caller values. | Do not infer target workspace, publish a different object, auto-subscribe, default location, or hide failed unsubscribe/delete. | Construct publish/subscription specs from explicit share variables and typed object/workspace/publish results. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | SQL text, database/table names, workbook/version/query/statement ids, execution context, page limits, cancel/drop decisions. | SQL metadata/workbook/version/query/statement/result/profile results supply ids, metadata, execution state, and result handles. | SQL text and execution context are caller-owned; metadata ids/names come from typed metadata or UI selection. | Do not rewrite SQL, derive workbook/query ids from SQL text, default database/table scope, or drop/kill without approval. | Construct SQL/workbook/query calls from explicit SQL/context plus typed metadata/workbook/query/statement handles. |

### User Story Value Format and Units Coverage

Use this table after Input Construction Coverage and before Default and Omission Coverage so AI-written code does not serialize identifiers, timestamps, durations, byte sizes, limits, enum values, URLs, SQL, cron, or payload fields with guessed formats or units. Every promoted task must state value/format contract, unit/timezone authority, enum/identifier format, forbidden format shortcut, and AI-safe value behavior. Code entries in value format and units cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If the SDK type, backend contract, typed result, UI-selected value, or explicit caller input does not prove the expected format/unit, code must stop and ask instead of converting units, changing timezone, normalizing identifiers, or rewriting payload text.

| User task | Value/format contract | Unit/timezone authority | Enum/identifier format | Forbidden format shortcut | AI-safe value behavior |
|-----------|-----------------------|-------------------------|------------------------|---------------------------|------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Endpoint URLs, API keys, workspace names, workspace ids, account ids, and DB connection strings keep the SDK/backend string formats. | Client timeout units come from typed SDK options or caller duration values; backend timestamps remain as returned. | Workspace/account ids and auth tokens are opaque identifiers; status strings come from backend results. | Do not trim, lowercase, URL-rewrite, token-mask in requests, timezone-convert timestamps, or infer id prefixes. | Pass explicit endpoint/auth/workspace values unchanged through SDK constructors; display returned ids/timestamps without reinterpretation. |
| Manage workspace members | Emails, member ids, canonical role ids, status actions, and audit filters retain their contract formats. | Audit windows, pagination limits, and timestamps use Backend-returned values or explicit caller units. | Member ids, role ids, and status actions are opaque canonical IAM values. | Do not case-fold identifiers, split role ids, translate status values, or convert audit timezones implicitly. | Use typed IAM fields as-is and ask for exact identifiers, actions, or windows when absent. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Catalog/database/table ids, names, column definitions, file URLs, SQL snippets, and table data values keep catalog/SQL contract formats. | Preview limits, page sizes, byte/download sizes, load timestamps, and data row counts use backend fields or explicit caller numeric units. | Column types, load modes, database/table ids, and catalog ids come from typed metadata or reviewed schema input. | Do not rewrite column names/types, normalize SQL, convert file URL schemes, infer byte units, or parse ids from names. | Serialize reviewed schema and file values exactly through typed SDK fields; ask for exact type, limit, URL, or SQL scope when unclear. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Volume/folder/file ids, paths, filenames, local paths, upload bytes, preview URLs, and connector-file ids keep file-service formats. | File sizes are backend byte counts; upload/download timeouts and preview limits use SDK/caller units. | Volume/file/folder ids, ref ids, workflow ids, and connector-file ids are opaque typed-result identifiers. | Do not path-normalize remote ids, change filename case, infer MIME/type from extension, convert size units, or parse ids from URLs. | Pass local path/bytes and returned ids unchanged; display byte counts with labels only outside SDK calls; ask for exact path/id. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow DSL, workflow ids, execution ids, node keys, rerun ids, run input payloads, trace ids, and cancellation reasons keep workflow contract formats. | Poll intervals/timeouts use SDK/caller duration units; run timestamps and durations use backend-returned values. | Rerun modes, run statuses, node keys, and execution ids are backend/UI values and remain case-sensitive when documented. | Do not rewrite DSL, translate statuses, guess duration units, convert local time to backend time, or derive node keys from labels. | Use typed workflow ids/status/duration fields and reviewed DSL/input as-is; ask for exact mode, timeout, node key, or reason. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator identifiers, schemas, code, DSL, test payloads, template ids, and version fields keep design/runtime contract formats. | Test timeout and execution budget use caller/SDK duration units; code timestamps and validation times use backend fields. | Schema field types, enable states, operator ids, template ids, and code envelopes come from typed results or reviewed input. | Do not reformat code, normalize schema field names, infer language/runtime, translate enable states, or default timeout units. | Submit reviewed code/schema/payload exactly; ask for explicit runtime, timeout, schema type, or template id when missing. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Node ids, versions, service names/types, compute resource ids, timeout/concurrency/rate limits, and invoke payload fields keep WorkItem API contract formats. | Timeout/rate/concurrency units come from typed publish spec fields or explicit caller values; invocation timestamps come from backend. | Node id/version/service type/status and compute id are opaque metadata values. | Do not choose version strings, convert timeout units, rewrite payload JSON, infer service type from name, or map compute labels to ids. | Build publish/invoke specs from exact metadata and caller payload; ask for unit-bearing limits, version, service type, or compute id. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact ids, node ids, block ids, revision ids, revision content, rerun mode/input, and revision switches keep lineage contract formats. | Revision timestamps, rerun durations, and output block ordering use backend-returned metadata; caller budgets use SDK/caller units. | Revision ids, block ids, node ids, rerun modes, and artifact ids are opaque typed-result identifiers. | Do not parse ids from rendered text, normalize revision content, convert revision timezones, or infer rerun mode. | Use lineage typed ids/revision metadata exactly; ask for explicit revision, block, rerun mode, or budget when unclear. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Model ids, entry ids, entry kind/key/spec, table bindings, import/export payloads, and validation diagnostics keep semantic contract formats. | Import/export sizes and validation timestamps use backend fields; caller timeouts use SDK/caller duration units. | Entry kinds, model ids, validation statuses, and table binding identifiers come from typed results or reviewed input. | Do not rewrite semantic keys/specs, translate entry kinds, normalize table bindings, convert export locations, or drop invalid diagnostics. | Submit reviewed semantic specs unchanged and preserve diagnostics; ask for exact kind, binding, location, or timeout. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector ids/types, credentials, source database/schema/table names, mappings, storage config, task ids, and file ids keep connector contract formats. | Transfer limits, file sizes, task windows, retry budgets, and timestamps use backend/caller units. | Source type, usage type, task status, connector id, file id, and mapping keys come from typed metadata or explicit input. | Do not rewrite credentials, normalize mappings, convert size units, change timezone windows, infer enum values, or copy storage config formats. | Pass secrets/config/mappings exactly through typed specs; ask for explicit enum, unit-bearing limit, time window, or mapping. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent codes/ids, request ids, message ids, task ids, turn ids, text, JSON-RPC method names, and input payloads keep A2A contract formats. | Stream timeouts and resubscribe budgets use caller/SDK duration units; task timestamps use backend/A2A values. | A2A method constants, task states, agent selectors, task ids, and turn ids are opaque protocol values. | Do not rewrite prompts, generate task ids, translate states, change JSON-RPC method casing, or infer timeout units. | Use SDK constants/types and exact caller text/payload; ask for explicit task/turn id, method path, or stream budget. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Provider family, backend ids, endpoint URLs, secrets, model names, endpoint statuses, router strategies, and probe payloads keep provider contract formats. | Probe timeouts and endpoint budgets use SDK/caller units; provider timestamps/status times use backend values. | Provider family, status, router strategy, backend id, endpoint id, and model name formats come from typed metadata or explicit config. | Do not normalize model names, rewrite URLs, infer provider family, convert timeout units, expose secret formats, or translate statuses. | Preserve exact endpoint/model/strategy values and redacted secret handling; ask for explicit family, URL, model, or timeout unit. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource ids/names, spec ids, image ids, sizing, replicas, action values ranges, and preflight flags keep compute contract formats. | CPU/memory/storage units, replica counts windows, and action timeouts use backend/spec/caller unit authority. | Resource/action/status/spec/image ids are opaque typed-result values; sizing fields follow compute spec units. | Do not convert CPU/memory/storage units, infer image ids from names, translate statuses/actions, or force-delete from string flags. | Use typed compute specs/preflight exactly; ask for explicit sizing unit range, action, or force flag. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Publish ids, subscription ids, object ids, locations, target workspace ids, names, and share decisions keep data-share contract formats. | Share timestamps, name-check conflicts, and subscription states use backend fields; caller budgets use SDK/caller units. | Publish/subscription/object/location/workspace ids and states are typed-result or explicit values. | Do not normalize share names, infer workspace ids, convert location strings, translate states, or parse object ids from names. | Use typed share/object/workspace ids and exact names/locations; ask for explicit target, object, location, or decision. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | SQL text, database/table names, workbook/version/query/statement ids, result column types, profile fields, and execution context keep SQL/editor contract formats. | Query timeouts, page limits, result sizes, profile durations, and history windows use SQL backend/caller unit authority. | Workbook/query/statement ids, database/table names, result types, and query statuses come from typed SQL metadata/results. | Do not rewrite SQL, convert timestamp literals, default database scope, translate statuses, infer ids from SQL text, or change page units. | Send SQL/context exactly as caller supplied and use typed SQL metadata for ids/types; ask for exact timezone, limit, scope, or id. |

### User Story Default and Omission Coverage

Use this table after Value Format and Units Coverage and before Completion Verification Coverage so AI-written code treats omitted fields, empty values, zero values, and backend defaults as explicit contract choices instead of auto-filling missing input. Every promoted task must state optional omissions, invalid empty/zero values, backend/default authority, forbidden defaulting behavior, and AI-safe omission behavior. Code entries in default and omission cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If a caller value is missing and this table does not mark it as safely omittable, code must stop and ask for the value instead of inventing, normalizing, silently dropping, or substituting a default.

| User task | Optional omissions | Invalid empty/zero values | Backend/default authority | Forbidden defaulting behavior | AI-safe omission behavior |
|-----------|--------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------|-------------------------------|---------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Optional DB connection reads and user listing may be omitted when the user only needs workspace binding. | Empty product endpoint, token/API key, workspace name for ensure, and workspace id for bind are invalid. | Backend may assign workspace id/status/timestamps; SDK must not choose tenant defaults. | Do not default endpoint, token, workspace name, workspace id, or DB connection fields. | Omit only optional reads; ask for endpoint/auth/workspace name or id before constructing client or handle. |
| Manage workspace members | Optional read filters, pagination, and supported general audit reads may be omitted. | Empty member ids, required email, empty role ids, and missing status actions are invalid where required. | Backend owns audit timestamps and policy evaluation; the caller owns targets and role-binding decisions. | Do not default administrator roles, invent email addresses, or treat omitted actions as approval. | Omit only optional read controls; ask for missing member, roles, status action, or approval. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Optional preview limits, download options, and nonessential metadata reads may be omitted. | Empty catalog/database/table names, required table id, schema columns, file URL/bytes, and destructive approval are invalid. | Backend may assign ids, timestamps, load status, and preview defaults; caller owns schema/file/destructive intent. | Do not synthesize schema, default file URLs, choose truncate/delete, or coerce empty column names/types. | Let backend choose documented read limits only when allowed; ask for schema, file source, or approval otherwise. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Optional preview/download/workflow trigger reads may be omitted after upload or listing. | Empty file/folder/volume ids, upload bytes/path, rename target, workflow id for trigger, and delete/clean approval are invalid. | Backend assigns file ids, refs, preview links, and storage paths; caller owns local path/bytes and destructive choices. | Do not scan local files, invent bytes, default rename names, or clean/delete on omitted approval. | Omit optional follow-up reads; ask for file source, rename value, workflow id, or cleanup approval. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Optional trace options, node inspection, and result polling may be omitted only for read-only metadata requests. | Empty workflow DSL/name for deploy, workflow/run ids, run input required by workflow, node key, rerun mode, and cancel/delete decision are invalid. | Backend assigns workflow/run/rerun ids and execution status; caller owns DSL/input/mode/destructive intent. | Do not generate DSL, default rerun mode, invent run input, or cancel/delete because a flag is omitted. | Omit optional trace/detail reads; ask for DSL, input, node key, rerun mode, or approval. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Optional descriptions, tags, and list filters may be omitted when not required by the operation. | Empty operator identifiers, required schemas, code, test payload, template id/content, and enable/delete decision are invalid. | Backend owns ids, validation result, and enabled state after explicit calls; caller owns code/schema/content. | Do not fill missing spec fields, default code/schema, auto-enable, or delete on omitted approval. | Omit nonessential metadata only; ask for missing spec, code, payload, or lifecycle decision. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Optional limits may be omitted only when backend contract documents a safe default for the service. | Empty node id/version, service name/type when required, compute resource id when required, invoke payload, and enable/disable decision are invalid. | Backend owns service lifecycle status and documented limit defaults; caller owns payload and compute selection. | Do not choose compute defaults, invent version/type, rewrite payload, or enable/disable on omitted decision. | Omit documented optional limits; ask for node/version/compute/payload/lifecycle input otherwise. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Optional revision listing, output-block expansion, and node detail reads may be omitted. | Empty artifact/node/block ids, revision id for switch, rerun input/mode when required, and approval are invalid. | Backend owns lineage graph state and rerun ids; caller owns selected revision and rerun decision. | Do not default effective revision, invent block ids, auto-switch revisions, or rerun on missing approval. | Omit optional expansions; ask for artifact/block/revision/rerun choice before mutation. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Optional validation/export reads may be omitted when only listing or binding existing models. | Empty model id/name for target operation, entry kind/key/spec, table binding when required, import payload, export location, and delete approval are invalid. | Backend owns model/entry ids, validation diagnostics, and import/export task state; caller owns semantic spec and bindings. | Do not default entry kind, drop invalid entries, invent model id, or delete on omitted approval. | Omit optional reads; ask for spec, binding, import/export target, or approval. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Optional previews, run history, and pagination may be omitted when task state is enough. | Empty connector id/type, credentials required by connector, storage config, source/target selection, mapping, task id, and action decision are invalid. | Backend owns task ids/status and documented pagination defaults; caller owns credentials/config/mapping/action. | Do not generate credentials, default task mode, skip failed files, or infer mappings. | Omit optional previews/history; ask for secret, mapping, source/target, or task action. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Optional stream resubscribe, cancel, and input-submit calls may be omitted when send result is enough. | Empty agent selector, text/message payload, task id for task actions, turn id when required, and input payload are invalid. | Backend owns task state and stream events; caller owns prompt text, request ids, and input submission. | Do not fabricate task ids, default input payloads, rewrite prompts, or fallback to resource proxy. | Omit optional follow-up task actions; ask for agent selector, text, task id, or required input. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Optional model probes, router reads, and list filters may be omitted. | Empty provider family, backend id/name, endpoint URL when creating, secret, model name, router strategy, and delete decision are invalid. | Backend owns endpoint ids, probe results, and router state; caller/secret storage owns secrets and strategy. | Do not choose default models, invent secrets, default router strategy, or delete on omitted approval. | Omit optional probes/reads; ask for endpoint, secret, model, strategy, or lifecycle decision. |
| Manage compute resources | Optional reads and list filters may be omitted. | Empty resource id/name, spec/image ids, sizing/replicas, action choice range when requested, and delete/force approval are invalid. | Backend owns resource status samples, and action state; caller owns sizing/action/destructive intent. | Do not default sizing, auto-resize, force delete after omitted preflight, or infer ids from. | Omit optional detail reads; ask for spec, sizing, action range, or approval. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Optional summaries and name checks may be omitted only when caller already has typed publish/subscription state. | Empty publish name, object ids, target workspace/location, subscription id, and subscribe/unsubscribe/delete approval are invalid. | Backend owns publication/subscription ids and conflict detection; caller owns target object/workspace and sharing decision. | Do not default location, infer target workspace, auto-subscribe, or hide failed unsubscribe/delete. | Omit optional summary/name-check reads only with typed state; ask for target, object, or approval. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Optional profiling, result paging, and workbook metadata reads may be omitted. | Empty SQL text for execution, workbook/version/query/statement ids for bound operations, database/table scope when required, and cancel/drop approval are invalid. | Backend owns statement ids, status, profile, and documented page defaults; caller owns SQL text and execution scope. | Do not rewrite SQL, default database/table scope, derive ids from SQL text, or cancel/drop on omitted approval. | Omit optional profile/page reads; ask for SQL text, selected scope, ids, or destructive approval. |

### User Story Completion Verification Coverage

Use this table after Default and Omission Coverage and before Result Accessor Coverage so AI-written code proves task completion through user-visible read-back, status, result, list, or inspection calls instead of assuming a write, async start, delete, cancel, or error-only method is complete. Every promoted task must state completion signal, verification call, async/delete/cancel handling, forbidden completion shortcut, and AI-safe stop condition. Code entries in completion verification cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If the verification signal is unavailable, code must return the explicit partial state and stop instead of fabricating success.

| User task | Completion signal | Verification call | Async/delete/cancel handling | Forbidden completion shortcut | AI-safe stop condition |
|-----------|-------------------|-------------------|------------------------------|-------------------------------|------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Workspace workspace handle, or DB connection result is visible through typed product reads. | Verify with `EnsureWorkspace`/`Workspace`, or `DBConnection`. | Workspace create/claim is complete only after the returned or selected workspace can be read through product endpoint. | Do not treat client construction, endpoint string, or error-only update/delete as workspace readiness. | Stop when typed account/workspace/DB result is returned; otherwise return auth/workspace error context. |
| Manage workspace members | The created or updated member, role bindings, status, or supported general audit state is readable. | Verify through member detail, list, bindings, or supported general audit reads. | Status, binding replacement, and deletion are complete only when the API result or read-back reflects the target state. | Do not treat an error-only write as member data or skip an unauthorized read-back. | Stop after typed IAM state or audit evidence; otherwise return the explicit error with member context. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Catalog/database/table metadata, data preview, download, load status, or mutation result is inspectable. | Verify with catalog/database/table info, children/tables, `TableHandle` data/preview/download reads, or load/status reads. | Table load/truncate/delete is complete only after status/read-back/list confirms the requested table state. | Do not treat load/truncate/delete request success as data availability or deletion confirmation. | Stop when typed table metadata/data/status/list result confirms state; otherwise return catalog/table error context. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Volume/folder/file/upload/preview/download/workflow trigger result is visible. | Verify with volume file list, folder refs, file info, preview link, download result, or workflow trigger result. | Upload/delete/clean/trigger completion requires read-back, list disappearance, preview/download availability, or trigger result. | Do not treat local file path, upload request, or delete error-only write as persisted file state. | Stop when typed file/folder/list/preview/download/trigger result confirms the action; otherwise return file context. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow, run, node, result, rerun branch, or cancel state is inspectable. | Verify with workflow get/list, run result/info, node detail, rerun info/branches, or cancel status read. | Run/rerun are complete only when result/status is inspected; cancel is complete only when status reflects cancellation. | Do not treat run/rerun request ids or cancel request success as final workflow outcome. | Stop when typed run/result/node/rerun/cancel state is returned; otherwise return workflow/run context. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator/template info, code, test result, enabled state, or deletion/list state is visible. | Verify with operator info/code/test result/list or template get/list after create/update/enable/disable/delete. | Enable/disable/delete completes only after info/list/read-back confirms state or absence. | Do not treat validation pass, create request, or error-only enable/disable/delete as published design state. | Stop when typed operator/template/test/read-back confirms state; otherwise return design context. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Service info, published status, enable/disable state, or invoke result is visible. | Verify with service info after publish/enable/disable and invoke result after invocation. | Publish/enable/disable are complete only after service info/read-back confirms lifecycle state. | Do not treat error-only enable/disable as service data or publish request as invokable readiness. | Stop when typed service info or invoke result is returned; otherwise return node/version/service context. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact overview, output blocks, revisions, effective switch, or rerun result is inspectable. | Verify with lineage overview, artifact output blocks, revision list, effective revision read, rerun info, or branch reads. | Revision switch/rerun completion requires read-back of effective revisions or rerun state. | Do not treat switch/rerun request success as visible artifact output or rerun completion. | Stop when typed lineage/revision/rerun state is returned; otherwise return artifact/node/block context. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Model/entry info, list, validation, import/export, or deletion/list state is visible. | Verify with model info, entry list/detail, validation result, import/export result, or list absence after delete. | Import/export/validation completion requires typed result; delete requires list/detail read-back where supported. | Do not treat create/update/delete/import request success as semantic consistency. | Stop when typed semantic model/entry/validation/import/export state is returned; otherwise return semantic context. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector info/source metadata/file preview/task state/run/file result is visible. | Verify with connector info/source browse, connector file preview/download, import/export task info, files, runs, or state reads. | Import/export/pause/resume/retry/delete completes only after task state/list/read-back confirms requested state. | Do not treat task create/update/pause/resume/retry/delete request success as data moved or task complete. | Stop when typed connector/file/task/run/state result confirms progress or completion; otherwise return connector/task context. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent card, send/stream result, task status, resubscribe stream, cancel state, or input-submit result is visible. | Verify with agent card, send/stream response, `GetTask`, resubscribe stream, cancel status, or input-submit response. | A2A stream completes on stream termination; cancel/input-submit require task/status read-back where available. | Do not treat request/message id creation or send acceptance as final task completion. | Stop when typed A2A task/message/stream/status is returned; otherwise return agent/task context. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Backend, endpoint, probe/model list, router config, or deletion/list state is visible. | Verify with provider backend info/list, endpoint status, model probe/list, router read, or list absence after delete. | Endpoint status/router update/delete completes only after read-back confirms state. | Do not treat secret submission, probe request, or router update request as usable provider configuration. | Stop when typed provider/backend/endpoint/router/probe state is returned; otherwise return provider context. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource detail, preflight result, action state, or deletion/list state is visible. | Verify with resource info/list, preflight delete, action result/status, or list absence after delete. | Suspend/resume/retry/delete completes only after action/status/list/read-back confirms state. | Do not treat create/update/action/delete request success as resource readiness or deletion. | Stop when typed compute resource/action/preflight state is returned; otherwise return compute context. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Summary, publication/subscription info, name-check, subscribe state, or deletion/list state is visible. | Verify with data-share summary, publish/subscription info/list, name availability, subscribe result, or list absence after delete/unsubscribe. | Subscribe/unsubscribe/delete completes only after summary/list/read-back confirms requested state. | Do not treat publish/subscribe/delete request success as share availability or removal. | Stop when typed share summary/publish/subscription/read-back state is returned; otherwise return data-share context. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Metadata, workbook/version, query status, statement result/detail/profile, cancel/drop state is visible. | Verify with SQL metadata reads, workbook/version get/list, query detail/status, statement result/detail/profile, or metadata/list after drop. | Query execution completes only after status/result read; kill/drop completes only after status or metadata read-back confirms state. | Do not treat execute request, query id, kill request, or drop request as final SQL result/state. | Stop when typed SQL result/status/metadata/workbook/statement state is returned; otherwise return SQL context. |

### User Story Result Accessor Coverage

Use this table after Completion Verification Coverage and before Output Consumption Coverage so AI-written code reads typed result fields and public handle accessors instead of parsing raw JSON, display labels, URLs, log text, SQL text, or private fields. Every promoted task must state public result/accessor source, user-readable fields, continuation fields, forbidden extraction shortcut, and AI-safe accessor behavior. Code entries in result/accessor cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If a required value is not available from the documented public fields or accessors, code must stop and ask for the value or call the documented typed read instead of deriving it.

| User task | Public result/accessor source | User-readable fields | Continuation fields | Forbidden extraction shortcut | AI-safe accessor behavior |
|-----------|-------------------------------|----------------------|---------------------|-------------------------------|---------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Use `WorkspaceHandle` identity accessors, workspace list/ensure result, and DB connection typed result. | Workspace id/name/status and DB connection status. | Workspace handle/id/name, DB connection result, and user ids from typed results. | Do not parse endpoint URLs, auth headers, logs, or raw workspace payloads for identity. | Read public fields/accessors only; ask for workspace id/name or credentials when typed reads do not provide them. |
| Manage workspace members | Use `WorkspaceMemberHandle` for member state, `IAMService.UserRoleBindings` / `user_role_bindings` for canonical bindings, and supported general audit rows. | Member id, display data, lifecycle status, canonical role ids, and audit summaries. | Member id from typed member results and role ids/binding versions from IAM results continue later calls; audit row ids come only from `WorkspaceMemberService.AuditLogs`. | Do not parse display names, policy blobs, denied errors, or audit text to infer identifiers or bindings. | Read scoped member and IAM accessors and ask for explicit canonical role ids when missing. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Use catalog/database/table handles, child list refs, table info/schema/data/preview typed results. | Catalog/database/table names, ids, columns, preview rows, status, and operation summaries. | Catalog/database/table/volume/file handles, ids, column definitions, refs, and preview/download result fields. | Do not parse storage URLs, rendered paths, SQL text, display labels, or raw table payloads for ids/schema. | Use typed refs, schema fields, and handle accessors; ask for reviewed schema or selected table when unavailable. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Use volume/folder/file/connector-file handles, list refs, upload preview, info, preview link, and download typed results. | Volume/folder/file names, ids, size/status, upload preview, preview link, and download summary. | Volume/folder/file handles, ids, refs, preview/download result fields, and workflow trigger result ids. | Do not parse local file paths, signed URLs, storage paths, or raw preview payloads for file identity. | Use public file/folder accessors and typed upload/info/download fields; ask for explicit file/path when missing. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Use workflow/run/node/rerun handles, deploy/run/result/detail/rerun/cancel typed results. | Workflow id/name/status, run id/status/result, node key/state, rerun id/status, and cancel state. | Workflow/run/node/rerun handles, execution ids, node keys, rerun ids, result envelopes, and status fields. | Do not parse workflow DSL, logs, trace text, request ids, or raw engine payloads to derive run/node/rerun ids. | Read typed workflow fields/accessors and ask for run/node/rerun selection when the value is absent. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Use custom operator/template handles, create/test/code/info/list typed results, and spec/test-input wrappers. | Operator/template id/name/status, code metadata, test result, validation output, and enabled state. | Operator/template handles, ids, specs, code/test result fields, and template metadata. | Do not parse code text, schema strings, logs, or raw test payloads for operator/template identity. | Use typed operator/template fields and validation results; ask for reviewed code/schema/payload when missing. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Use WorkItem service handle, publish/info/enable/invoke typed results, and publish/invoke specs. | Service id/name/type, node id/version, publish state, enabled state, and invocation result/status. | Service handle, node id/version, service metadata, invoke result fields, and lifecycle state. | Do not parse node labels, default compute text, request ids, or raw invoke payloads as service readiness. | Use typed service info and invocation fields; ask for node/version/payload when not publicly available. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Use artifact/block/node/node-block handles, lineage overview, output blocks, revisions, switch, and rerun typed results. | Artifact id/name, block id, node id/key, revision ids, effective revision, output summary, and rerun status. | Artifact/block/node handles, block ids, revision ids, rerun ids, output refs, and switch fields. | Do not parse rendered labels, artifact blobs, run logs, or raw lineage payloads for ids/revisions. | Read typed lineage fields/accessors and ask for selected artifact/node/block/revision when absent. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Use semantic model/entry handles, model info/list, entry list/detail, validation, import/export typed results. | Model id/name/status, entry id/name/type, validation diagnostics, import/export summary, and table binding. | Model/entry handles, ids, entry specs, validation fields, import/export result fields, and table binding ids. | Do not parse entry names, raw knowledge payloads, validation logs, or export blobs to infer ids or validity. | Read typed semantic fields and validation result; ask for reviewed entry spec or model/table binding when missing. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Use connector/file/import/export task handles, source/target metadata, preview/download, task info/runs/files typed results. | Connector id/name/type/status, source/target metadata, file preview, task id/state/progress, and run/file summaries. | Connector/file/task handles, connector ids, file ids, task ids, mappings, run ids, and task state fields. | Do not parse credentials, external system labels, file preview text, logs, or raw task payloads for ids/status. | Read redacted typed connector/task fields; ask for explicit connector/file/task/config when unavailable. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Use agent handle selector, agent card, send/stream response, task get/cancel/resubscribe/input-submit typed results. | Agent code/id/name, task id/status, message id, stream final state, cancel state, and input-submit result. | Agent handle/selector, task ids, message ids, request ids, turn ids, and input-required fields. | Do not parse JSON-RPC payloads, prompt text, stream chunks, resource proxy responses, or local agent output for task ids. | Read typed A2A card/message/task fields and ask for task/input selection when the public result lacks it. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Use provider/backend/router handles, backend info/list, endpoint status, probe/model list, and router typed results. | Provider family, backend id/name/status, endpoint status, model names, probe status, and router config summary. | Provider/backend/router handles, backend ids, endpoint ids, model names, probe result fields, and router strategy fields. | Do not parse secret values, provider error bodies, endpoint URLs, or raw provider responses for model availability. | Read typed provider/router fields with secrets redacted; ask for backend/endpoint/model selection when missing. |
| Manage compute resources | Use compute resource handle, create/get/list/update/preflight/action typed results. | Resource id/name/status, spec/image ids, action status summary, preflight warnings, and delete state. | Resource handle/id, spec/image ids, action ids/status fields, and preflight result fields. | Do not parse labels, worker logs, runtime internals, or raw capacity payloads for resource identity/state. | Read typed compute fields/accessors and ask for resource/spec/action selection when missing. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Use data-share summary, publish/subscription handles, info/list, name-check, subscribe/unsubscribe typed results. | Publication id/name/status, subscription id/status, object id/name, target workspace, name availability, and governance summary. | Publish/subscription handles, ids, object ids, target workspace ids, name-check result, and governance fields. | Do not parse catalog labels, target workspace names, policy text, or raw share payloads for share/subscription ids. | Read typed share fields/accessors and ask for target/object/publication/subscription selection when missing. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Use SQL metadata/workbook/version/query/statement handles, metadata reads, execute/status/result/detail/profile typed results. | Database/table names, columns, workbook/version ids, query/statement ids, status, rows, detail, and profile summary. | SQL metadata handles, workbook/version/query/statement handles, ids, result rows, status fields, and profile fields. | Do not parse SQL text, query labels, raw DB errors, logs, or direct DB responses to derive ids/results. | Read typed SQL metadata/result/status fields and ask for SQL text or selected workbook/query/statement when missing. |

### User Story Output Consumption Coverage

Use this table after Result Accessor Coverage and before Sensitive Data Handling Coverage so AI-written code consumes returned values as user-visible product output or typed continuation state instead of exposing raw/debug/internal fields, secrets, credentials, partial streams, or error-only write results. Every promoted task must state user-facing output, continuation output, sensitive/internal output boundary, forbidden output consumption, and AI-safe consumption behavior. Code entries in output consumption cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If a field is not documented as user-facing or continuation output, code must keep it internal and ask for explicit review before displaying or reusing it.

| User task | User-facing output | Continuation output | Sensitive/internal output boundary | Forbidden output consumption | AI-safe consumption behavior |
|-----------|--------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------|------------------------------|------------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Selected workspace id/name/status and DB connection status may be shown. | `WorkspaceHandle`, workspace id/name, and DB connection result fields feed later setup. | API key/session tokens and raw endpoint/auth headers remain secret; DB connection fields are caller-owned infrastructure output. | Do not print tokens, reuse endpoint text as identity, or expose raw auth errors as profile data. | Display typed identity/workspace/DB status only; pass handles/results forward and redact secrets. |
| Manage workspace members | Member detail, canonical role bindings, status, and supported general audit summaries may be shown. | Member ids and role ids feed later member calls; permitted general audit rows remain audit-only. | Denial details, auth context, and internal policy traces remain diagnostic only. | Do not show raw policy blobs as bindings, use denial as proof of access, or expose unrelated audit rows. | Display workspace-scoped IAM results and continue only with typed member and role ids. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Catalog/database/table metadata, columns, preview rows, download/load/status, and operation status may be shown. | Catalog/database/table/volume/file handles, table ids, schemas, refs, and preview/download outputs feed later data operations. | File URLs, signed download links, row samples, and external load URLs must follow caller visibility policy. | Do not expose raw storage URLs broadly, treat preview samples as full data, or use truncate/delete success as data output. | Show typed metadata/preview/status with caller-approved scope; pass handles and reviewed schema/results forward. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Volume/folder/file metadata, upload status, preview/download links, refs, and workflow trigger result may be shown. | Volume/folder/file/connector-file handles, file ids, refs, and preview/download results feed later file workflows. | Local paths, file bytes, signed links, and connector temporary file contents remain caller-controlled. | Do not log file bytes, leak signed URLs, display local filesystem paths by default, or treat delete success as file content. | Display typed file state and links only to authorized callers; pass file handles/refs forward. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow metadata, execution status/result, node detail, rerun branch state, and cancel outcome may be shown. | Workflow/run/node/rerun handles, execution ids, node keys, rerun ids, and result envelopes feed later workflow actions. | Run inputs, traces, node logs, and error payloads may contain sensitive data and need caller-approved display. | Do not show raw trace/log payloads as final answers, expose secrets from run input, or treat request ids as results. | Summarize typed status/result first; expose logs/traces only when the caller asks and scope is authorized. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator/template metadata, code view, test result, enabled state, and delete/list state may be shown. | Operator/template handles, ids, specs, test outputs, and template metadata feed later design or workflow calls. | Operator code, schemas, test payloads, and template DSL are caller-owned design artifacts. | Do not publish code/test payloads in logs, treat validation output as production result, or expose disabled/deleted resources as active. | Display reviewed design/test state; keep code/schema/payload under caller-controlled output channels. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Service info, publish/enable state, invocation result, and disable outcome may be shown. | Service handle, node id/version, service metadata, invoke result, and limits feed later service operations. | Invoke payloads/results can contain caller data; compute resource ids and limits may be operationally sensitive. | Do not log payloads/results by default, expose disabled services as invokable, or use enable/disable writes as result data. | Display service lifecycle and invocation result with caller-approved redaction; continue with typed service metadata. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact overview, output blocks, revisions, effective switch, node detail, and rerun result may be shown. | Artifact/block/node/node-block handles, revision ids, switch values, rerun ids, and output refs feed later review/rerun calls. | Artifact outputs and revisions may contain derived user data and must keep lineage scope. | Do not expose raw lineage blobs, infer hidden revisions, or display unreviewed revision content as approved output. | Show typed lineage summaries and selected output blocks; require review before displaying revision content or rerun outputs. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Semantic model metadata, entry rows, validation result, import/export result, and delete/list state may be shown. | Model/entry handles, ids, entry specs, validation diagnostics, and export/import outputs feed later semantic operations. | Entry specs and export/import payloads may contain sensitive business logic or internal schema details. | Do not expose raw entry specs outside the caller context, treat failed validation as usable model state, or hide invalid entries. | Display typed model/entry/validation summaries; pass reviewed specs and handles forward. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector state, source/target metadata, file preview, task files/runs/state, and progress may be shown. | Connector/file/task handles, task ids, file ids, mappings, previews, run state, and export outputs feed later task actions. | Credentials, storage config, source connection detail, file previews, and failure rows may be sensitive. | Do not print credentials/storage config, expose raw preview data broadly, or treat partial task progress as completed movement. | Display redacted connector/task state; continue only with typed task/file ids and approved mappings. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent card, message/task result, stream final state, task status, cancel state, and input-submit outcome may be shown. | Agent handle, task ids, turn ids, message ids, request ids, and input-submit results feed later A2A calls. | Prompt text, tool outputs, stream chunks, and task payloads may contain sensitive user data. | Do not expose raw JSON-RPC payloads, show partial stream as final output, or reuse caller-generated ids as backend task state. | Display typed A2A task/message state; redact prompts/tool outputs unless the caller requested them. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Backend info, endpoint status, model probe/list, router config, and delete/list state may be shown. | Provider/backend/router handles, backend ids, endpoint ids, model names, probe results, and router state feed later provider calls. | Secrets, endpoint credentials, API keys, and provider error bodies remain secret. | Do not print secrets or full endpoint auth, treat probe failure as model availability, or expose raw provider errors as config. | Display redacted provider/router state; pass typed backend/router handles and model metadata forward. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource detail, preflight result, action state, and delete/list state may be shown. | Resource handle/id, spec/image ids, preflight result, action result, and feed later compute actions. |, worker image details, capacity, and failure logs may reveal operational internals. | Do not expose raw runtime logs by default, treat preflight warnings as approval, or show deleted resources as active. | Display typed compute status summaries; require explicit approval before destructive follow-up. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Share summary, publication/subscription info, name-check, subscribe state, and unsubscribe/delete outcome may be shown. | Publish/subscription handles, ids, object ids, target workspace ids, name-check result, and summary feed later share operations. | Shared object names, target workspaces, and subscriber metadata may be governance-sensitive. | Do not expose unauthorized target workspaces, treat name availability as publish success, or hide unsubscribe/delete failures. | Display scoped share state and governance-relevant ids; continue with typed publish/subscription handles. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Metadata, workbook/version state, query status, statement result/detail/profile, and cancel/drop state may be shown. | Metadata/database/table/workbook/version/query/statement handles, ids, result rows, profile, and status feed later SQL calls. | SQL text, result rows, profile details, and connection errors may contain sensitive data. | Do not log SQL/results by default, treat query id as statement result, or expose profile/internal errors as user facts. | Display typed SQL status/result with caller-approved row limits; pass handles/results forward and redact diagnostics. |

### User Story Sensitive Data Handling Coverage

Use this table after Output Consumption Coverage and before Automation Safety Coverage so AI-written code does not leak secrets, credentials, PII, SQL text/results, file contents, prompts, traces, provider errors, or governance-sensitive metadata into logs, examples, persistence, or later AI context. Every promoted task must state sensitive inputs, sensitive outputs, logging/example boundary, persistence/AI-context boundary, and AI-safe handling. Code entries in sensitive data handling cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If sensitivity is unclear, code must redact by default and ask for explicit permission before storing, logging, displaying, or forwarding the value.

| User task | Sensitive inputs | Sensitive outputs | Logging/example boundary | Persistence/AI-context boundary | AI-safe handling |
|-----------|------------------|-------------------|--------------------------|---------------------------------|------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Product endpoint, API key, session token, auth headers, workspace id/name when tenant-sensitive. | Account profile, user list, DB connection fields, auth errors, and workspace membership. | Log only operation, status, workspace id when authorized, and trace id; examples must use variables, never real tokens or DB fields. | Store credentials only in caller secret storage; do not put tokens, DB connection details, or full profile into AI context. | Redact secrets by default, pass `WorkspaceHandle` and typed status forward, and ask before exposing identity or DB details. |
| Manage workspace members | Member email/display data, canonical role ids, status actions, and administrator decisions. | General audit rows, denied-policy details, bindings, and lifecycle status may be sensitive. | Log only ids, status, and operation outcome; examples must not include real email addresses or audit rows. | Persist changes only through product IAM; do not pass full logs or PII into AI context without approval. | Redact PII and policy traces, show scoped summaries, and ask before sharing audit details. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Table names, schemas, file URLs, external load URLs, preview limits, destructive decisions. | Preview rows, sample data, signed URLs, table refs, download links, and load errors. | Log table ids/status only; examples must not include row samples, signed URLs, file URLs, or schema-sensitive fields. | Do not persist preview rows or signed URLs outside caller-approved storage; avoid forwarding row samples to AI context unless approved. | Redact data values and URLs, pass handles/schema metadata when authorized, and ask before showing samples. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Local paths, file bytes, file names, folder paths, workflow ids, connector-file ids. | File previews, signed download links, file-derived artifacts, refs, trigger results, and connector temporary contents. | Log file ids/status only; examples must not contain local paths, file bytes, signed URLs, or real folder names. | Store file bytes only through explicit upload APIs; do not cache bytes/previews or pass file contents to AI context without approval. | Redact paths/links/content, pass file handles forward, and ask before displaying or summarizing file contents. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workflow DSL, run input, trace config, cancel reason, node keys, rerun choices. | Run outputs, traces, node logs, error payloads, execution ids, and branch details. | Log workflow/run ids, status, and trace id only; examples must not include real DSL secrets, inputs, logs, or traces. | Persist workflow definitions/results only through product workflow APIs; do not forward logs/traces/run inputs to AI context without approval. | Redact inputs/logs/traces, show typed status first, and ask before exposing run output or debugging payloads. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Operator code, schemas, DSL, test payloads, template content, enable/delete decisions. | Test outputs, code views, schema validation errors, enabled state, and template metadata. | Log operator/template ids and status only; examples must not include proprietary code, schemas, or real test payloads. | Keep code/schema/test payloads in caller-controlled design storage; do not place full code or payloads into AI context without approval. | Redact code/payloads, show summaries and validation state, and ask before sharing design artifacts. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Service payload, node id/version, compute resource id, service limits, publish config. | Invocation result, service metadata, errors, rate/concurrency limits, and payload-derived output. | Log service id/version/status only; examples must not include real payloads, compute ids, or invocation output. | Persist payloads/results only if caller owns that storage; do not forward invocation payload/result to AI context without approval. | Redact payloads/results, pass typed service handles and status forward, and ask before showing invoke data. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact ids, revision content, node/block ids, rerun inputs, revision switch decisions. | Artifact outputs, revisions, derived files, lineage graph details, and rerun outputs. | Log artifact/node ids and status only; examples must not include raw artifact content, revisions, or derived data. | Keep lineage outputs within product artifact storage; do not persist or forward raw revisions/output blocks to AI context without approval. | Redact output content, show lineage summaries, and ask before exposing revisions or rerun artifacts. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Entry specs, business terms, table bindings, import payloads, model ids, delete decisions. | Validation diagnostics, export payloads, semantic entries, model metadata, and import results. | Log model/entry ids and validation status only; examples must not include proprietary semantic specs or exports. | Persist semantic specs only through product semantic APIs; do not forward full specs/exports/import payloads to AI context without approval. | Redact business logic, show validation summaries, and ask before exposing specs or exports. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Credentials, storage config, connector endpoints, source schemas, mappings, task configs, file ids. | Preview rows, run failures, task files, external paths, export locations, and connector state. | Log connector/task ids and status only; examples must not include credentials, endpoints, storage config, preview rows, or external paths. | Store credentials only in secret storage/product connector config; do not pass credentials, previews, or mappings to AI context without approval. | Redact secrets/config/previews, show task summaries, and ask before sharing external metadata. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent prompt, request/message ids, text, task ids, turn ids, input answers, payloads. | Tool outputs, stream chunks, task payloads, final messages, cancellation state, and input-submit result. | Log agent selector, request id, task id, and status only; examples must not include real prompts, answers, payloads, or tool outputs. | Persist conversation/task state only through product A2A APIs; do not forward prompts/tool outputs to later AI context without approval. | Redact prompts/tool outputs, show typed task state, and ask before reusing message content. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Endpoint URLs, API keys, secrets, backend ids, model names, router strategy. | Probe errors, provider error bodies, endpoint status, model lists, router config, and secret validation results. | Log backend/router ids and status only; examples must not include real endpoints, secrets, API keys, or provider error bodies. | Store secrets only in caller secret storage/product provider config; do not pass secrets or raw provider errors into AI context. | Redact endpoints/secrets/errors, show model/router summaries, and ask before exposing provider details. |
| Manage compute resources | Worker image, spec ids, sizing window, force-delete decision, capacity policy. |, runtime failures, worker image details, preflight warnings, action status, and cost-relevant state. | Log resource id/action/status only; examples must not include internal image names, capacity data, or runtime logs. | Persist action state only through product compute APIs; do not forward runtime logs or capacity details to AI context without approval. | Redact logs/capacity, show typed summaries, and ask before exposing operational internals. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Object ids, object names, target workspace ids, location ids, share remarks, subscription names. | Target workspace lists, subscriber metadata, governance state, name-check results, and shared object tables. | Log publish/subscription ids and status only; examples must not include real target workspaces, object names, or subscriber details. | Persist share state only through product data-share APIs; do not forward governance-sensitive share metadata to AI context without approval. | Redact target/subscriber details, show scoped share summaries, and ask before sharing governance metadata. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | SQL text, workbook names, database/table names, execution context, result limits, cancel/drop decisions. | Result rows, query profile, statement detail, history, errors, and metadata tree. | Log query/statement ids and status only; examples must not include real SQL, result rows, connection errors, or profile details. | Persist SQL/results only through product SQL/workbook APIs; do not forward SQL text/results/profile to AI context without approval. | Redact SQL/results/profile, show bounded typed status/result summaries, and ask before exposing rows or query text. |

### User Story Automation Safety Coverage

Use this table after Sensitive Data Handling Coverage and before Concurrent Submission Coverage so AI-written code can be safely re-run by scripts, assistants, and workflow automation without duplicating resources, hiding external side effects, or spending compute/API quota unexpectedly. Every promoted task must state repeat-safe behavior, idempotency key or lookup, external/cost side-effect boundary, forbidden automation shortcut, and AI-safe automation behavior. Code entries in automation safety cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If repeat safety is unclear, code must stop for explicit user approval or perform the documented read/list/preflight check before issuing another write/action.

| User task | Repeat-safe behavior | Idempotency key or lookup | External/cost side-effect boundary | Forbidden automation shortcut | AI-safe automation behavior |
|-----------|----------------------|---------------------------|------------------------------------|-------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Repeat reads are safe; repeated `EnsureWorkspace` must return the existing workspace or fail on ambiguity instead of creating a second workspace. | Lookup workspace by typed workspace id/name and workspace identity result before create. | Workspace creation affects product account state but has no external compute cost beyond the product API call. | Do not create duplicate workspaces, choose one of several matching names, or treat endpoint/auth text as an existing workspace. | Read account/workspace state first, return the existing handle when the lookup is unique, and ask for approval when names are ambiguous. |
| Manage workspace members | Repeat reads are safe; repeated role-binding or status writes require fresh member and IAM binding state. | Lookup by workspace/member id and current typed member/list state, plus `IAMService.UserRoleBindings` / `user_role_bindings`. | Mutations change workspace access and audit history. | Do not replace bindings, delete members, or change status blindly during automated retry. | Read current member and IAM binding state first, return unchanged state when it already matches, and ask before destructive access changes. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Metadata and preview reads are safe; create/load/truncate/delete must be guarded by existing catalog/table state. | Lookup catalog/database/table ids, names, schemas, load status, and list results. | Loads consume storage/compute and truncate/delete can destroy data. | Do not rerun load, truncate, delete, or create table from guessed names without a preflight read. | Read/list table state first, reuse existing handles when present, and require approval for destructive table operations. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Repeated list/info/download reads are safe; upload/delete/clean/trigger must be tied to a reviewed file decision. | Lookup volume/folder/file ids, file names, refs, upload preview, and workflow trigger result. | Uploads consume storage and triggers can start workflow compute or external processing. | Do not duplicate uploads, delete/clean files, or trigger workflows automatically on retry. | List/get existing file state, reuse returned file handles, and ask before upload replacement, delete, clean, or trigger. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Status/result reads are safe; run/rerun/cancel can be repeated only when tied to a specific workflow/run state. | Lookup workflow id, execution id, node key, rerun id, branch state, and run status. | Runs and reruns consume workflow compute and may call external systems through workflow nodes. | Do not rerun, retry, cancel, or deploy blindly because a previous status is unknown. | Inspect workflow/run/rerun status first, reuse existing run handles, and ask before starting another run or canceling. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Info/list/code reads are safe; create/update/enable/disable/delete must follow current design state. | Lookup operator/template id, name, version, enabled state, test result, and list entry. | Tests may execute code and lifecycle changes alter workflow design behavior. | Do not create duplicate operators, enable unreviewed code, delete templates, or rerun tests silently. | Read current operator/template state, reuse matching handles, and require approval before code execution or lifecycle changes. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Service info reads are safe; publish/enable/disable/invoke must be tied to node/version/service state. | Lookup node id, version, service id/name, enabled state, and invocation request/result. | Publishing changes service availability and invocation can spend compute/API quota. | Do not publish duplicate versions, enable disabled services, or invoke repeatedly without a caller-approved payload. | Get service status first, reuse service handles, and ask before publish, enable/disable, or repeated invocation. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Artifact/revision reads are safe; switch/rerun must be tied to selected artifact/node/block/revision state. | Lookup artifact id, node id, block id, revision id, effective switch, and rerun id/status. | Reruns consume workflow compute and revision switches change which output users see. | Do not rerun from a guessed node, switch revisions, or reuse stale block ids blindly. | Read lineage and effective revision first, reuse typed artifact handles, and ask before switch or rerun. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Model/entry reads and validation are safe; create/update/delete/import/export must follow current model state. | Lookup model id, entry id, entry kind/name, validation result, import/export result, and list state. | Import/export can move semantic data and validation may spend backend compute. | Do not duplicate entries, delete models, import/export, or overwrite specs blindly. | List/get entries first, reuse matching handles, and ask before destructive or data-moving semantic changes. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Connector/source reads are safe; import/export/pause/resume/retry/delete must follow task state. | Lookup connector id, file id, task id, run id, task status, and file/result list. | Tasks move data, use credentials, consume storage/compute, and may call external systems. | Do not retry, resume, delete, or create duplicate import/export tasks blindly. | Inspect task state and source metadata first, reuse task handles, and require approval before retry/resume/delete or new transfer. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Agent card/task reads and resubscribe are safe; send/stream/cancel/input-submit must be tied to request and task ids. | Lookup caller-generated request id, message id, task id, turn id, and task status. | Sends/streams can spend model/tool/API quota and may trigger agent tools. | Do not generate new request ids on retry of the same action, send duplicate messages, or cancel blindly. | Reuse idempotency request/message ids for retries, get task status first, and ask before duplicate send or cancel. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Provider/backend/router reads are safe; create/update/probe/delete changes routing or spends provider calls. | Lookup provider family, backend id, endpoint id, router id/state, model name, and probe result. | Endpoint probes can call external providers; config changes affect model routing and secrets. | Do not probe, update secrets, change routers, or delete backends blindly. | Read provider/router state first, redact secrets, and require approval before probe, secret update, router change, or delete. |
| Manage compute resources | Resource/spec/image/resource reads are safe; create/update/action/delete can change capacity and spend compute. | Lookup resource id, spec id, image id, action id/status, preflight result, and window. | Compute operations affect capacity, runtime cost, and operational availability. | Do not create duplicate resources, resize, suspend/resume, force delete, or ignore failed preflight blindly. | Read resource state and preflight first, reuse resource handles, and require approval before cost or destructive actions. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Summary/name-check/list reads are safe; publish/subscribe/unsubscribe/delete changes data sharing state. | Lookup publish id, subscription id, object id, target workspace id, name availability, and summary state. | Sharing can expose data across workspaces and affect governance/audit state. | Do not publish different objects, auto-subscribe, unsubscribe, or delete shares blindly. | Read summary/name availability/list state first, reuse share handles, and ask before publish/subscribe/unsubscribe/delete. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Metadata/history/status/result reads are safe; execute/cancel/drop can spend database resources or mutate data. | Lookup workbook id, version id, query id, statement id, database/table metadata, and query status. | SQL execution consumes database resources; DDL/DML/drop/kill can change or destroy data. | Do not rerun SQL, rewrite SQL, execute DDL/DML/drop/kill, or cancel blindly in automation. | Inspect query status/result first, reuse workbook/query/statement handles, and require approval before destructive or repeated SQL execution. |

### User Story Concurrent Submission Coverage

Use this table after Automation Safety Coverage and before Impact Assessment Coverage so AI-written code does not let UI double-clicks, background polling, async retries, parallel goroutines, or multiple AI workers submit the same mutation twice while treating read calls as safe to parallelize only when scoped. Every promoted task must state operation identity, parallel-safe reads, serialized mutation boundary, forbidden duplicate submission, and AI-safe in-flight behavior. Code entries in concurrent submission cells must be backed by the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome Contract for the matched task. If an operation is already in flight and its identity cannot be matched to the current request, code must return the in-flight handle/status and stop instead of starting another write, run, transfer, query, share, notification, or provider call.

| User task | Operation identity | Parallel-safe reads | Serialized mutation boundary | Forbidden duplicate submission | AI-safe in-flight behavior |
|-----------|--------------------|---------------------|------------------------------|--------------------------------|----------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Endpoint, auth principal, workspace name/id, and create-or-bind intent. | Account profile, workspace list, unique workspace read, and DB connection status reads can run in parallel after endpoint/auth is fixed. | Workspace create, claim, switch/bind, and DB detail exposure must be serialized per account/workspace name or id. | Do not submit two workspace creates, bind two ambiguous workspaces, or reveal DB details from duplicate UI clicks. | Return the existing in-flight workspace lookup/create handle or current account/workspace status; ask before a second create or switch. |
| Manage workspace members | Workspace id, target member id, canonical role-id set, and requested status/delete action. | Member detail and `IAMService.UserRoleBindings` / `user_role_bindings` reads can run in parallel for one target; general audit reads are not member-scoped. | Role-binding replacement, status changes, and deletion must be serialized per member. | Do not submit duplicate binding/status writes or delete while another member mutation is in flight. | Return current member and IAM binding state plus the in-flight action identity; ask before another access-changing call. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Workspace/catalog/database/table id or name plus create/load/truncate/delete/download action. | Catalog/database/table list, schema, preview, refs, and status reads can run in parallel under one selected scope. | Table create/load/truncate/delete and link exposure must be serialized per table or target name. | Do not start duplicate loads, create duplicate tables, truncate while load is in flight, or expose multiple links from repeated submits. | Return table status/load handle/read-back state; ask before a second data-moving or destructive table action. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Workspace/volume/folder/file id or path plus upload/delete/clean/link/trigger action. | Volume/folder/file info, refs, preview, and download-status reads can run in parallel for selected handles. | Upload replacement, folder clean, file/folder delete, signed-link exposure, and workflow trigger must be serialized per path/file/folder. | Do not double-upload replacements, delete while preview/trigger is pending, clean twice, or trigger workflows twice from double-clicks. | Return uploaded file/trigger/link status and selected file identity; ask before duplicate upload, cleanup, delete, link exposure, or trigger. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Workspace/workflow id, execution id, node key, rerun id, and action type. | Workflow detail, run status/result, node detail, rerun branch, and cancel-status reads can run in parallel for the same run. | Deploy/update/run/rerun/cancel/delete must be serialized per workflow/run/node/rerun target. | Do not start duplicate runs/reruns, cancel while rerun submission is pending, or deploy two versions from one request. | Return the current workflow/run/rerun handle and in-flight status; ask before starting another execution-changing action. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Workspace/operator/template id or name plus code/schema/test/lifecycle action. | Operator/template list/detail/code metadata and validation reads can run in parallel after the target is selected. | Create/update/test/enable/disable/delete must be serialized per operator/template id or name. | Do not run duplicate tests, enable while update is pending, or delete while another lifecycle action is in flight. | Return current validation/test/lifecycle state and selected artifact id; ask before another code execution or lifecycle mutation. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Node id, version, service name/type, publish spec, invoke payload identity, and lifecycle action. | Service info, node/version metadata, enabled-state reads, and limits reads can run in parallel. | Publish/enable/disable and identical invoke payload submissions must be serialized per service version and request identity. | Do not publish twice, enable/disable concurrently, or invoke the same payload twice from retry/double-click. | Return service lifecycle or invocation handle/status; ask before duplicate publish/lifecycle change or repeated invocation. |
