### User Story Example Scenario Coverage

Use this table after Representative Method Coverage and before Supplemental Handle Method Coverage so AI-written code starts from a real user goal, copies the right language README anchor, replaces only caller-owned inputs, and checks the expected user-visible result. Every promoted task must state the user scenario, copyable example anchor, inputs to replace, expected output, and AI adaptation boundary. Inputs to replace must mention a Flow Recipe or Outcome Contract source entry. Expected output must mention a Flow Recipe product already named by the matching Outcome Contract. If a task lacks an example scenario row, keep it out of AI-facing quick starts even if individual methods and tests exist.

| User task | User scenario | Copyable example anchor | Inputs to replace | Expected user-visible output | AI adaptation boundary |
|-----------|---------------|-------------------------|-------------------|------------------------------|------------------------|
| Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Create or select a UI workspace, confirm identity, and verify DB connection before workspace-scoped calls. | Go/Python bootstrap README examples using account profile, ensure workspace, and DB connection calls. | Endpoint, credential, workspace name or sourced workspace id, and optional DB connection choice. | Account/workspace identity, workspace handle, and DB connection summary. | Rewrite workspace names, endpoint variables, and output formatting only; do not invent workspace ids or credentials. |
| Manage workspace members | Create a pending or immediately auto-accepted invitation, seat a GitHub user immediately, or select an accepted member, then replace canonical role bindings and inspect active member state. | Go/Python IAM README examples using `InviteMember` / `invite_member` (`WithUserEmail` / `with_user_email`, `WithUserMobileNumber` / `with_user_mobile_number`, `WithUserGithubLogin` / `with_user_github_login`, or optional `WithUserAutoAccept(true)` / `with_user_auto_accept(True)`), explicit `Member` / `member` binding after active membership, and `IAMService.UserRoleBindings` / `user_role_bindings` plus `PutUserRoleBindings` / `put_user_role_bindings`. | Workspace handle, member identity/display data, accepted or immediately seated member id, canonical role ids, binding versions, and mutation approval. | `WorkspaceMemberInviteResult` for an invitation or immediate seat (unknown UC accounts are rejected), or `WorkspaceMemberHandle`, typed member state, and explicit IAM/audit status for an active member. | Keep email/mobile pending invitation results separate from active member handles; treat a non-empty GitHub invite id as already a member. Auto-accept completes the same durable acceptance saga, so its result is an already-accepted invitation. Rewrite display data and role choices only from caller input or typed IAM results, and do not infer or broaden roles. |
| Browse catalog resources and operate tables | Prepare a catalog/database, create or inspect a table, and read table data. | Go/Python catalog README examples using prepare catalog, create table, and table data calls. | Workspace handle, catalog/database/table names, reviewed columns, and optional file or query inputs. | Catalog/database/table handles, schema/data preview, and table operation result. | Rewrite names, columns, and display formatting only; do not fabricate schemas, rows, or storage paths. |
| Manage volumes, folders, and files | Prepare storage, upload a file, preview or download it, and keep file handles for later workflows. | Go/Python file README examples using prepare volume, upload bytes handle, and download calls. | Workspace/database/volume choice, folder/file names, file bytes or explicit path, and preview/download decision. | Volume/folder/file handles, upload preview, download URL/data, or workflow trigger result. | Rewrite local variable names and file names only from caller input; do not read implicit local paths or generate signed links. |
| Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Deploy a workflow, run it, inspect node results, and rerun/cancel from sourced run state. | Go/Python workflow README examples using deploy workflow, run, and rerun calls. | Workflow DSL/spec, run inputs, sourced run/node/rerun ids, and rerun/cancel decision. | Workflow/run handles, run status/result, node result, rerun handle, or cancel result. | Rewrite workflow names and input variables only; do not synthesize node keys, rerun ids, or engine state. |
| Design workflow custom operators and templates | Create a custom operator from reviewed code/schema, test it, and save a reusable template. | Go/Python workflow design README examples using `CreateCustomOperatorFromSpec` / `create_custom_operator_from_spec`, test input, and template calls. | Operator code, schemas, test payload, template DSL, ids from create/list/get, and enable decision. | Operator/template handles, test result, validation output, and lifecycle status. | Rewrite code only when supplied by caller or reviewed source; do not execute local code or enable unvalidated operators. |
| Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Publish a WorkItem node/version as an API service and invoke it with explicit payload. | Go/Python WorkItem API README examples using service publish and invoke calls. | Node id, version, service metadata, compute/runtime config, and invoke payload from caller or metadata. | Published service handle, enable state, invocation result/status, and service summary. | Rewrite payload shape only from documented service schema; do not choose default compute or fabricate readiness. |
| Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Open lineage from a produced artifact, inspect blocks/revisions, and rerun selected nodes. | Go/Python lineage README examples using lineage overview, artifact handle, and output blocks calls. | Artifact/run/node/block/revision ids from lineage results or UI state and rerun/revision decision. | Lineage overview, output blocks, revision state, rerun request/result, and artifact handle. | Rewrite reporting text only; do not parse rendered labels, read blobs directly, or rerun without lineage ids. |
| Manage semantic models and entries | Create or update semantic model entries, validate them, and import/export definitions. | Go/Python semantic README examples using create semantic model, create entry, and validate calls. | Model/table binding, entry specs, import/export payload, ids from semantic results, and validation decision. | Semantic model/entry handles, validation result, import/export result, and entry list/detail. | Rewrite entry text only from reviewed input; do not fabricate validation or infer entries from names. |
| Manage connectors plus import/export tasks | Validate connector metadata, create import/export tasks, update config, and monitor task state. | Go/Python connector README examples using create file import task, update config, and export spec calls. | Connector id/type, credentials handle, source metadata, target metadata, file/task ids, and import/export config. | Connector/task handles, preview, import/export task status, files, and validation result. | Rewrite configs only from caller input or connector metadata; do not copy secrets or call external systems directly. |
| Use product agents through A2A | Select a product agent, send a message, inspect task state, and submit required input. | Go/Python A2A README examples using agent by code, send text, and get task calls. | Agent code/id, message text, request id, task id from A2A result, and input submission payload. | Agent message/task result, stream events when requested, cancellation/input-submit result, and task status. | Rewrite message text and request id generation only; do not use resource proxy, invent task ids, or run local agents. |
| Configure model providers and routers | Configure provider backends/endpoints, probe models, and update router policy. | Go/Python provider README examples using provider handle, create backend, and router update calls. | Provider family, backend endpoint, secret reference, model list/probe result, router strategy, and status decision. | Provider/backend/router handles, setup/probe result, endpoint status, and router config. | Rewrite config variables only from explicit setup; do not log secrets, call providers directly, or choose default models. |
| Manage compute resources | Create a compute resource, run lifecycle actions, and delete with preflight. | Go/Python compute README examples using create compute resource and suspend calls. | Resource spec/image, CPU/memory/replica values, sourced resource id, and action/delete approval. | Compute resource handle, create/update/action status, and delete preflight/result. | Rewrite sizing variables only from caller input or specs; do not infer resources from runtime labels or force actions. |
| Publish and subscribe data shares | Publish a dataset/object to another workspace and manage subscriptions with governance checks. | Go/Python data-share README examples using `CreateDataSharePublish` / `create_data_share_publish`, subscription handle, and subscribe calls. | Object id/location, target workspace, publication/subscription ids, governance decision, and subscribe/unsubscribe approval. | Publication/subscription handles, share summary, name-check, subscribe/unsubscribe result, and governance status. | Rewrite target/object variables only from product results or explicit input; do not infer target workspace or auto-subscribe. |
| Work in the SQL editor and query history | Browse SQL metadata, create workbook versions, execute SQL, and inspect statement results/history. | Go/Python SQL README examples using SQL metadata, create workbook, execute SQL, and statement result calls. | Database/table metadata, SQL text, workbook/version ids, query/statement ids from execution/history, and cancel/drop approval. | Metadata output, workbook/version handle, query/statement status, result/detail/profile, and cancel/drop result. | Rewrite SQL only when caller supplies it; do not derive ids from SQL text, call DB directly, or fabricate results. |

This table is not a quick-start catalog.

Do not infer member targets from account labels. |

### Common Handle Method Boundary

Use this table for public handle methods that are shared by many promoted handles
and intentionally stay out of Supplemental Handle Method Coverage. These methods
are not independent user-story entry points, not README copy anchors, and not a
license to skip the selected User Story Matrix row. AI-written code may use a
common method only after the handle itself is sourced by the matching Flow
Recipe, Outcome Contract, Handle ID Source Matrix, typed result, UI state, or
explicit user input. The method family must then follow the relevant User Story
Operation Intent, Completion Verification, Result Accessor, Progress Visibility,
Freshness and Conflict, and Confirmation and Preview rows for the selected task.
If a public handle method does not fit one of these families, document it in
Representative Method Coverage or Supplemental Handle Method Coverage before
treating it as user-facing.

### Exported Result And Protocol Type Boundary

Exported result, envelope, stream, and protocol types are allowed package-level
symbols because promoted methods return them or accept them as typed request
pieces. They are not independent user-story entry points, not AI task-selection
sources, and not README copy anchors. AI-written code must first choose a
`Client` or handle method from the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, or Outcome
Contract, then consume result/envelope/stream/protocol types only as values
returned by that chosen method or as typed inputs explicitly named by that task.
Do not start from `ProductData`, `RawMessage`, `StreamResponse`, `*Envelope`,
or generated protocol classes to infer routes, ids, payloads, status, or next
actions.

### Option Helper Boundary

Option helpers are allowed package-level symbols because promoted `Client` and
handle methods accept typed options for optional behavior. They are not
independent user-story entry points, not AI task-selection sources, and not
README copy anchors by themselves. AI-written code must first choose the
`Client` or handle method from the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, Outcome
Contract, Example task index, or Representative Method Coverage, then attach
`With...` / `with_...` helpers only to that selected method call. Do not start
from an option helper name to infer the task, target resource, route, required
ids, defaults, retry behavior, destructive action, or payload shape.

### Protocol Constant Boundary

A2A method constants and low-level A2A request classes are allowed package-level
symbols because explicit protocol-control calls sometimes need them. They are
not independent user-story SDK entry points, not AI task-selection sources, and
not README copy anchors by themselves. AI-written code must first choose the
agent task from the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, Example
task index, or Representative Method Coverage, then use `AgentHandle.SendText` /
`send_text`, `AgentHandle.StreamText` / `stream_text`, `AgentHandle.GetTask` /
`get_task`, `AgentHandle.CancelTask` / `cancel_task`,
`AgentHandle.ResubscribeTask` / `resubscribe_task`, or
`AgentHandle.SubmitInput` / `submit_input` for promoted workflows. Use
`AgentHandle.A2A` / `a2a`, `A2AMethod...` / `A2A_METHOD_...`, `A2ARequest`, or
`AgentA2ARequest` only after the caller explicitly asks for route-level A2A
control. Do not start from JSON-RPC `method` values or protocol constants to
infer the task, agent selector, request id, payload, stream mode, task id, input
answers, retry behavior, or continuation state.

### Error Boundary

Error types, helpers, and sentinel values are allowed package-level symbols
because callers need diagnostic context and explicit error-branch handling after
an SDK call fails. They are not user-story SDK entry points, not AI
task-selection sources, not recovery-strategy sources, not input-construction
sources, and not README copy anchors by themselves. AI-written code must first
choose the task from the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract,
Example task index, or Representative Method Coverage, then follow that task's
User Story Error Recovery Coverage before inspecting `Error`, `IsCode` /
`is_code`, or `Err...` sentinel values. Error inspection may report
`request_id`, code, message, and documented diagnostic context, or execute a
documented caller-owned branch. Do not start from `ErrTaskIDRequired`,
`ErrWorkspaceRequired`, `ErrMethodInvalid`, or any other `Err...` name to infer
which handle to call, which id to invent, which payload field to fill, whether a
retry/rerun/fallback is safe, or how to continue after a partial failure.

### Local File Input Boundary

Local file path upload helpers are local file input convenience methods allowed because UI
applications often receive a path or file-picker result from the caller and need
the SDK to perform the actual upload. They are not independent user-story SDK
entry points, not AI task-selection sources, not AI file-discovery tools, not
local-filesystem probing helpers, not filename inference sources, and not README
copy anchors or default upload entry points by themselves. AI-written code must first choose the file task from
the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, Example task index, or
Representative Method Coverage, then use `UploadBytes...` / `upload_bytes...`
when the application already owns bytes. Use `UploadFile...` / `upload_file...`
only when `filePath` / `file_path` is sourced from UI file input, storage picker
state, imported file metadata, or explicit user input and is covered by the
Example Input Variable Source Matrix. Do not search the local filesystem, call
`open` / `os.Open` in examples, infer upload filenames from paths, generate local
paths, read hidden files, or replace missing bytes with a guessed local path.

### Stream Response Boundary

Stream response values are allowed package-level result types because explicit
streaming, preview, and download methods return live response bodies. They are
not independent user-story SDK entry points, not AI task-selection sources, not
task completion signals, and not README copy anchors by themselves. AI-written
code must first choose the task from the User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, Outcome
Contract, Example task index, or Representative Method Coverage, then use
`StreamResponse` only as the returned live body from that selected method.
Callers must consume the stream to the selected task's documented stop condition
or explicitly return a partial-stream handoff state, then close the body with
`Body.Close` / `close`. Do not treat request acceptance, stream construction,
first bytes, partial chunks, preview streams, download streams, or EOF without
task-specific completion evidence as the final task result.

| Family | Go methods | Python method names | AI usage boundary |
|--------|------------|---------------------|-------------------|
| Read and inspect | `Info`, `Refresh`, `Refs`, `FullPath`, `Overview`, `Data`, `Preview`, `Download`, `Files`, `Tables`, `Databases`, `Volumes`, `Children`, `Messages`, `Runs`, `History`, `Metrics`, `State`, `Result`, `Detail`, `Profile`, `DDL`, `Tree`, `Columns`, `SourceDatabases`, `SourceSchemas`, `SourceTables`, `SourceSchema`, `MODatabases`, `MODatabaseTables`, `MOColumnMapping`, `CheckMOTableName`, `LatestMessageID`, `ProcessedFiles`, `PreflightDelete`, `ProbeModels` | snake_case equivalents | Use only as the selected task's read/status/result step after the handle and read scope are sourced from typed result, UI state, Handle ID Source Matrix rows, or explicit input; do not treat first pages, previews, metrics windows, stale reads, or preflight output as authorization for mutation. |
| Mutate and lifecycle action | `Rename`, `Update`, `Delete`, `DeleteRef`, `Clean`, `Cancel`, `Retry`, `Resume`, `Suspend`, `Enable`, `Disable`, `MarkRead`, `Like`, `Dislike`, `SetObjectCodes`, `GrantTo`, `RevokeFrom`, `Grants`, `GrantGraph`, `Logs`, `UpdateStatus`, `Save`, `CreateEndpoint`, `SetEndpointStatus`, `CreateRevision`, `SwitchEffectiveRevisions`, `RerunTargetWithRevisions`, `RetryFiles`, `Pause`, `Stream`, `StreamText`, `ResubscribeTask`, `CancelTask`, `SubmitInput` | snake_case equivalents | Use only after current state, permission scope, impact, and confirmation/preview requirements are satisfied by the selected task from typed result, UI state, Handle ID Source Matrix rows, or explicit input; do not mutate, delete, stream, resubscribe, retry, or grant from stale handles, inferred ids, implicit approval, or fallback paths. |

### Cross-Domain User Journeys

Use these journeys when the user asks for an outcome that spans multiple product areas instead of a single SDK operation. Every task in a journey must still follow its User Story Flow Recipe. The journey only defines composition order, handoff points, and where source data must come from. Entry points for later tasks must be backed by produced handles/results from the same or earlier journey steps.

| Journey | User goal | Task sequence | Entry points | Handoff and source rule |
|---------|-----------|---------------|--------------|-------------------------|
| Data onboarding to queryable analytics | Turn uploaded or selected data into catalog tables, SQL results, and Explore answers. | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity -> Browse catalog resources and operate tables -> Manage volumes, folders, and files -> Browse Explore source tables and columns -> Work in the SQL editor and query history -> Ask Explore questions with selected sources. | `WorkspaceHandle`, `CatalogHandle`, `DatabaseHandle`, `VolumeHandle`, `FileHandle`, `ExploreDataHandle`, `SQLWorkbookHandle`, `SQLStatementHandle`, `ExploreSessionHandle`, `ExploreDataSources`. | Carry `WorkspaceHandle`, `CatalogHandle`, `DatabaseHandle`, `VolumeHandle`, `FileHandle`, `ExploreDataHandle`, `SQLWorkbookHandle`, `SQLStatementHandle`, `ExploreSessionHandle`, and `ExploreDataSources` from previous task typed results, produced handles/results, UI state, or explicit user input; file bytes, SQL text, and questions remain caller-supplied inputs. |
| Workflow execution with traceable outputs | Run a workflow from prepared inputs, inspect outputs, rerun the right node, and monitor failures. | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity -> Manage volumes, folders, and files -> Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows -> Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks -> Manage compute resources -> Manage alert receivers, rules, and history. | `WorkspaceHandle`, `VolumeHandle`, `FileHandle`, `WorkflowHandle`, `WorkflowRunHandle`, `WorkflowRunNodeHandle`, `ArtifactHandle`, `ArtifactNodeBlockHandle`, `ComputeResourceHandle`, `AlertGroupHandle`. | Carry `WorkspaceHandle`, `VolumeHandle`, `FileHandle`, `WorkflowHandle`, `WorkflowRunHandle`, `WorkflowRunNodeHandle`, `ArtifactHandle`, `ArtifactNodeBlockHandle`, `ComputeResourceHandle`, and `AlertGroupHandle` from previous task typed results, produced handles/results, UI state, or explicit user input; workflow DSL and rerun choices remain reviewed caller inputs. |
| Governed knowledge-assisted exploration | Let users ask governed questions over semantic models and selected sources while respecting workspace permissions. | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity -> Manage workspace members -> Manage semantic models and entries -> Browse Explore source tables and columns -> Retrieve consumer memories and render profiles -> Ask Explore questions with selected sources -> Use product agents through A2A. | `WorkspaceHandle`, `WorkspaceMemberHandle`, `IAMService`, `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, `ExploreDataHandle`, `ConsumerMemoryHandle`, `ExploreSessionHandle`, `ExploreDataSources`, `AgentHandle`. | Carry `WorkspaceHandle`, `WorkspaceMemberHandle`, and typed role-binding results returned by the workspace-scoped `IAMService`, plus `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, `ExploreDataHandle`, `ConsumerMemoryHandle`, `ExploreSessionHandle`, `ExploreDataSources`, and `AgentHandle` from previous task typed results, produced handles/results, UI state, or explicit user input; consumer subject ids, questions, agent messages, and caller-generated request/message keys remain caller-owned inputs. |
| Connector data movement and sharing | Move external data into Matrixflow, validate it, and publish selected results to another workspace. | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity -> Manage connectors plus import/export tasks -> Manage volumes, folders, and files -> Browse catalog resources and operate tables -> Work in the SQL editor and query history -> Publish and subscribe data shares. | `WorkspaceHandle`, `ConnectorHandle`, `ConnectorFileHandle`, `FileImportTaskSpec`, `ImportTaskHandle`, `ExportTaskSpec`, `ExportTaskHandle`, `CatalogHandle`, `TableHandle`, `SQLStatementHandle`, `DataSharePublishHandle`, `DataShareSubscriptionHandle`. | Carry `WorkspaceHandle`, `ConnectorHandle`, `ConnectorFileHandle`, `FileImportTaskSpec`, `ImportTaskHandle`, `ExportTaskSpec`, `ExportTaskHandle`, `CatalogHandle`, `TableHandle`, `SQLStatementHandle`, `DataSharePublishHandle`, and `DataShareSubscriptionHandle` from previous task typed results, produced handles/results, UI state, or explicit user input; connector credentials, storage config, and target workspace choices remain caller-provided inputs. |
| Workflow platform extension and API publishing | Build reusable workflow components, publish a WorkItem API service, and run it with configured providers. | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity -> Design workflow custom operators and templates -> Publish and invoke WorkItem API services -> Configure model providers and routers -> Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows. | `WorkspaceHandle`, `CustomOperatorHandle`, `CustomOperatorSpec`, `CustomOperatorTestInput`, `WorkflowTemplateHandle`, `WorkitemAPIServiceHandle`, `WorkitemAPIServicePublishSpec`, `WorkitemAPIServiceInvokeSpec`, `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, `ProviderRouterHandle`, `WorkflowHandle`. | Carry `WorkspaceHandle`, `CustomOperatorHandle`, `CustomOperatorSpec`, `CustomOperatorTestInput`, `WorkflowTemplateHandle`, `WorkitemAPIServiceHandle`, `WorkitemAPIServicePublishSpec`, `WorkitemAPIServiceInvokeSpec`, `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, `ProviderRouterHandle`, and `WorkflowHandle` from previous task typed results, produced handles/results, UI state, or explicit user input; schemas, service payloads, provider endpoints, secrets, and DSL remain reviewed caller inputs. |
| Workspace administration and operations | Operate workspace access, runtime capacity, provider routing, alerting, and shared data as one administrative loop. | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity -> Manage workspace members -> Manage compute resources -> Configure model providers and routers -> Manage alert receivers, rules, and history -> Publish and subscribe data shares. | `WorkspaceHandle`, `WorkspaceMemberHandle`, `IAMService`, `ComputeResourceHandle`, `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, `ProviderRouterHandle`, `AlertGroupHandle`, `AlertReceiverHandle`, `AlertRuleHandle`, `DataSharePublishHandle`, `DataShareSubscriptionHandle`. | Carry `WorkspaceHandle`, `WorkspaceMemberHandle`, and typed role-binding results returned by the workspace-scoped `IAMService`, plus `ComputeResourceHandle`, `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, `ProviderRouterHandle`, `AlertGroupHandle`, `AlertReceiverHandle`, `AlertRuleHandle`, `DataSharePublishHandle`, and `DataShareSubscriptionHandle` from previous task typed results, produced handles/results, UI state, or explicit user input; access decisions, sizing choices, secret references, and share targets remain caller-owned inputs. |

### Cross-Domain Journey Example Anchors

Use this table to keep each cross-domain journey tied to copyable README examples without turning the example catalog into one generated workflow. Every anchor must be a real Go and Python README call from a task in the journey sequence. Every task in the journey sequence must have a Go and Python README anchor, anchors must follow the same order as the journey task sequence, and anchors must not use supplemental-only methods. AI-written code must still copy only the minimal ordered lines needed by the matched journey, then apply the Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, Interaction Mode Coverage, Risk Coverage, and source matrices before running it.

| Journey | Go README anchors | Python README anchors | Anchor rule |
|---------|-------------------|-----------------------|-------------|
| Data onboarding to queryable analytics | `client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx`<br>`workspace.PrepareCatalog(ctx`<br>`database.PrepareVolume(ctx`<br>`workspace.ExecuteSQL(ctx` | `client.ensure_workspace(`<br>`workspace.prepare_catalog(`<br>`database.prepare_volume(`<br>`workspace.execute_sql(` | Start from workspace bootstrap, then copy only catalog, volume/file, and SQL calls after table/file ids and SQL text are sourced. |
| Workflow execution with traceable outputs | `client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx`<br>`volume.UploadBytesHandle(ctx`<br>`workspace.DeployWorkflow(ctx`<br>`workflow.Run(ctx`<br>`runNode.CreateRerun(ctx, workflowRerunMode`<br>`file.LineageOverview(ctx` | `client.ensure_workspace(`<br>`volume.upload_bytes_handle(`<br>`workspace.deploy_workflow(`<br>`workflow.run(`<br>`run_node.create_rerun(workflow_rerun_mode`<br>`file.lineage_overview()` | Start from workspace bootstrap, then copy only file/workflow/run/lineage calls that match the requested recovery or inspection path and whose ids come from typed results, UI state, or explicit user input. |
| Governed knowledge-assisted exploration | `client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx`<br>`workspace.InviteMember(ctx`<br>`workspace.IAM().UserRoleBindings(ctx`<br>`workspace.IAM().PutUserRoleBindings(ctx`<br>`workspace.CreateSemanticModel(ctx`<br>`agent.SendText(ctx` | `client.ensure_workspace(`<br>`workspace.invite_member(`<br>`workspace.iam().user_role_bindings(`<br>`workspace.iam().put_user_role_bindings(`<br>`workspace.create_semantic_model(`<br>`agent.send_text(` | Start from workspace bootstrap, then copy only member, canonical IAM binding, semantic-model, and A2A calls after role assignments, selected model/entry ids, request ids, and messages are sourced. |
| Connector data movement and sharing | `client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx`<br>`connector.CreateFileImportTask(ctx`<br>`connector.CreateExportTaskFromSpec(ctx`<br>`database.PrepareVolume(ctx`<br>`workspace.PrepareCatalog(ctx`<br>`workspace.ExecuteSQL(ctx`<br>`workspace.CreateDataSharePublish(ctx` | `client.ensure_workspace(`<br>`connector.create_file_import_task(`<br>`connector.create_export_task_from_spec(`<br>`database.prepare_volume(`<br>`workspace.prepare_catalog(`<br>`workspace.execute_sql(`<br>`workspace.create_data_share_publish(` | Start from workspace bootstrap, then copy only connector/import/export, volume/catalog, SQL, and share calls after credentials, mappings, storage config, and target workspace choices are sourced. |
| Workflow platform extension and API publishing | `client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx`<br>`workspace.CreateCustomOperatorFromSpec(ctx`<br>`workspace.WorkitemAPIService(`<br>`llm.CreateBackend(ctx`<br>`workspace.DeployWorkflow(ctx` | `client.ensure_workspace(`<br>`workspace.create_custom_operator_from_spec(`<br>`workspace.workitem_api_service(`<br>`llm.create_backend(`<br>`workspace.deploy_workflow(` | Start from workspace bootstrap, then copy only operator/template, WorkItem API, provider/router, and workflow calls after code, schemas, payloads, endpoints, secrets, and DSL are reviewed. |
| Workspace administration and operations | `client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx`<br>`workspace.CreateComputeResource(ctx`<br>`llm.Router().Update(ctx`<br>`workspace.CreateDataSharePublish(ctx` | `client.ensure_workspace(`<br>`workspace.create_compute_resource(`<br>`llm.router().update(`<br>`workspace.create_data_share_publish(` | Start from workspace bootstrap, then copy only compute, provider/router, and data-share calls after admin approval and target ids are sourced. |

### Cross-Domain Journey Risk Coverage

Use this table to keep cross-domain journeys from becoming happy-path-only examples. Failures must return explicit errors with enough context for the caller to decide whether to retry, cancel, rerun, preflight, pause, resume, delete, or ask the user for corrected input. Do not add fallback, silent repair, inferred permissions, generated credentials, generated ids, or automatic external config. The permission/governance and external dependency columns define the boundaries AI-written code must inspect before composing the journey.

| Journey | Failure and recovery path | Permission/governance boundary | External dependency boundary |
|---------|---------------------------|--------------------------------|------------------------------|
| Data onboarding to queryable analytics | Catalog create/list/table ops, volume/folder/file upload, and SQL execute/cancel/result failures return explicit errors; recovery uses catalog/volume/file/SQL handles already sourced by the caller. | Catalog, volume, file, SQL, and workspace access must stay inside the scoped handles and backend RBAC boundary before reading tables or issuing destructive SQL/file actions. | File bytes, SQL text, table/column definitions, and workbook choices remain reviewed external input or typed results from previous task steps. |
| Workflow execution with traceable outputs | Deploy, run, result, retry, cancel, rerun-plan, node rerun, artifact revision switch, compute action return explicit errors; recovery uses run/node/rerun handles, compute preflight/action handles. | Workflow, artifact, compute, volume, file, and workspace access must stay inside the scoped handles and backend RBAC boundary before reading outputs or issuing retry/cancel/rerun actions. | Workflow DSL, run inputs, file inputs, node/rerun choices, compute sizing, and notification targets remain reviewed external input or typed results from previous task steps. |
| Governed knowledge-assisted exploration | Member grant/revoke, semantic model/entry create/update/validate/import/export, and agent A2A send/task failures return explicit errors; recovery uses permission, semantic, and agent handles already sourced by the caller. | Workspace member, semantic model/entry, and agent access must stay inside the scoped handles and backend RBAC boundary before mutating models or sending agent messages. | Member ids/roles, semantic specs/table bindings, agent codes, request/message ids, and message text remain reviewed external input or typed results from previous task steps. |
| Connector data movement and sharing | Connector create/update/source browse, connector-file preview, import/export create/update/pause/resume/retry/delete, SQL validation, and data-share subscribe/unsubscribe failures return explicit errors; recovery uses task state handles or explicit user decisions. | Connector credentials, target catalog/table access, workspace data-share permissions, share target permissions, and SQL/table governance must be checked through connector, catalog, SQL, and data-share handles or backend RBAC errors. | External system credentials, storage config, source schemas, file URIs, column mappings, export paths, target workspace ids, and share object choices remain caller-owned config, secret storage, UI state, or explicit input. |
| Workflow platform extension and API publishing | Custom operator code/test/enable/disable, template update, WorkItem API publish/enable/disable/invoke, provider probe/endpoint status, and workflow run/rerun failures return explicit errors; recovery uses the same promoted handle or requires reviewed corrected code/config/payload. | Operator/template ownership, WorkItem node/version access, provider backend/router permissions, endpoint status changes, and workflow run permissions must stay within workspace-scoped handles and backend RBAC boundaries. | Operator code, schemas, test input, service payloads, provider endpoints, secrets, router strategy, model choices, workflow DSL, and run inputs remain caller-owned reviewed external input, secret storage, or typed discovery results. |
| Workspace administration and operations | Canonical IAM role binding/lifecycle, compute preflight/action/delete, provider endpoint/router, and data-share update/delete/unsubscribe failures return explicit errors; recovery requires an explicit admin decision or corrected target state. | Workspace role bindings, protected objects, compute resources, provider routing, and share targets must be checked through promoted handles and canonical IAM errors before mutation. | Access decisions, object ids, compute sizing, provider secrets, endpoint config, target workspaces, and share objects remain external caller-owned input, secret storage, UI state, or typed discovery results. |

## Handle ID Source Matrix

Code snippets may use placeholder ids to show shape, but generated code and AI-written examples must explain where each id comes from before constructing a handle. Source existing ids from a typed SDK create/list/get result, a UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. High-level create facades return handles only when the product create response contains the authoritative id. Error-only write methods are not ID sources: they only indicate success/failure and do not return product data, ids, or handles. Caller-provided ids are accepted only when the product create request explicitly carries that id; the SDK does not invent ids. Handle constructors and handle factory methods such as `WorkspaceHandle.Workflow(workflowID)`, `workspace.workflow(workflow_id)`, `VolumeHandle.File(fileID)`, and `workspace.connector(connector_id)` only bind an existing resource id; they do not create or discover the resource. Bind-only factory methods that return handles must use the source row for the returned handle type; they do not create, discover, or validate the resource. Go handles expose readable identity accessors such as `ID()`, `Name()`, `Key()`, `NodeID()`, `Version()`, `Kind()`, `Category()`, `MessageSessionID()`, or `Selector()` so callers can pass handles across steps without reading private fields. Python handles expose the matching public attributes such as `.id`, `.name`, `.key`, `.node_id`, `.version`, `.kind`, `.category`, `.message_session_id`, or `.selector`. To create or discover a resource, call the matching `Create...`, `Deploy...`, `Prepare...`, `Ensure...`, list, or get method first and use the typed result. Do not invent placeholder ids such as `workflow-id`, `execution-id`, `artifact-id`, `connector-id`, `resource-id`, or `statement-id`; do not infer ids from `ProductData` or display labels.

| Handle entry | ID source | Do not |
|--------------|-----------|--------|
| `WorkspaceHandle`, `workspace(...)` | `EnsureWorkspace` / `ensure_workspace`, workspace list/create/claim result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not infer `workspace_id` from endpoint text, account display name, or `ProductData`. |
| `WorkspaceMemberHandle` | Typed member create/list/detail result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `user_id` / `userID` or canonical role ids, and do not infer them from display labels. |
| `CatalogHandle`, `DatabaseHandle`, `TableHandle`, `VolumeHandle`, `FolderHandle`, `FileHandle` | Catalog tree/list/create result, database create/list/children result, table create/list/info result, volume create/list result, folder create/list result, file upload/list/info result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent catalog, database, table, volume, folder, file, `table_id`, `folder_id`, or `file_id` values; do not parse ids from names, paths, preview URLs, or `ProductData`. |
| `WorkflowHandle`, `WorkflowRunHandle`, `WorkflowRunNodeHandle`, `WorkflowRunRerunHandle` | Workflow deploy/list/get result, run/list/detail result, node detail/rerun-plan result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `workflow-id`, `execution-id`, `node-id`, `node_key`, `node-execution-id`, `rerun_id`, or derive node ids from display labels. |
| `ArtifactHandle`, `ArtifactBlockHandle`, `ArtifactNodeHandle`, `ArtifactNodeBlockHandle` | File artifact lookup result, lineage artifact overview result, output-block list result, node detail result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `artifact-id`, `node-id`, `block-id`, `block_id`, or parse ids from rendered artifact titles. |
| `CustomOperatorHandle`, `WorkflowTemplateHandle`, `WorkitemAPIServiceHandle` | Custom-operator create/list/get result, workflow-template create/list/get result, WorkItem catalog publish/list result, caller-provided ids from application state or reviewed user input, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `operator-id`, `template-id`, `node-id`, service `version`, or silently fill publish/invoke fields. |
| `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle` | Semantic-model create/list/get result, entry list/create result, caller-provided ids from application state or reviewed user input, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `semantic-model-id`, `entry_id`, or infer model ids from semantic model display names. |
| `ConnectorHandle`, `ConnectorFileHandle`, `ImportTaskHandle`, `ExportTaskHandle` | Connector create/list/get result, connector file upload/list result, import/export task create/list/info result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `connector-id`, `conn-file-id`, import task ids, or export task ids. |
| `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, `ProviderRouterHandle` | Provider family `kind` entry point plus backend setup/create/list/get result, router get/update result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent backend ids, provider family names, `kind`, or secret references. |
| `ComputeResourceHandle` | Compute resource create/list/get result, caller-provided ids from application state or reviewed user input, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `resource-id` or infer compute ids from worker image names. |
| `ConsumerMemoryHandle` | Consumer subject id from consumer memory/profile typed result, application state, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent a subject id or infer it from memory content or profile text. |
| `DataSharePublishHandle`, `DataShareSubscriptionHandle` | Data-share publish create/list/detail result, subscription create/list result, caller-provided ids from application state or reviewed user input, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `publish-id`, `subscription-id`, target workspace ids, or object ids. |
| `AgentHandle` | Agent `selector` from card/list/configuration result, product agent code or id from UI-selected resource, application configuration, or explicit user input. When binding with `WorkspaceHandle.AgentByCodeInWorkspace`, `agent_workspace_id` must also come from UI-selected resource, application configuration, or explicit user input (use `"system"` only for system-provided agents). | Do not invent agent codes, `agent_id`, `selector`, `agent_workspace_id`, or promote Agent resource proxy ids as stable product handles. |
| `SQLMetadataHandle`, `SQLDatabaseMetadataHandle`, `SQLTableMetadataHandle`, `SQLWorkbookHandle`, `SQLWorkbookVersionHandle`, `SQLQueryHandle`, `SQLStatementHandle` | SQL metadata browse result, workbook create/list/detail result, version create/list result, query execute/history/detail result, statement result/history/detail result, UI-selected resource, or explicit user input. | Do not invent `statement-id`, workbook ids, `version_id`, query ids, database/table names, or derive database/table names from display labels. |

Source Table IDs are stable MatrixOne `rel_id` values. |

### Example Input Variable Source Matrix

User-story snippets also use named non-ID inputs for payloads, config enums, credentials, and external content. These variables must be supplied by the application, selected by the user, or returned by an SDK/backend discovery call before the snippet runs; the SDK must not invent or silently default them.

| Go variable | Python variable | Source |
|-------------|-----------------|--------|
| `productEndpoint`, `apiKey`, `timeout` | `product_endpoint`, `api_key`, `timeout` | Application configuration, user/session secret storage, or test harness setup. |
| `workspaceName`, `catalogName`, `databaseName`, `tableName`, `volumeName`, `fileName`, `localFilePath` | `workspace_name`, `catalog_name`, `database_name`, `table_name`, `volume_name`, `file_name`, `local_file_path` | User-selected workspace/data names, local file input, UI state, imported file metadata, or explicit user input. |
| `tableColumns`, `tablePageSize`, `tablePreviewLines`, `excelSheetName` | `table_columns`, `table_page_size`, `table_preview_lines`, `excel_sheet_name` | Caller-reviewed table schema, pagination/preview limits, and spreadsheet sheet selection from file preview metadata, UI state, or explicit user input. |
| `userDisplayName`, `userEmail`, `assignedRoleIDs` | `user_display_name`, `user_email`, `assigned_role_ids` | Member display data, email, and canonical role ids from typed IAM results, UI-selected resources, or explicit user input. |
| `tableDataURL` | `table_data_url` | Caller-provided external file URL from upload/import UI state, storage picker result, or explicit user input. |

| `ConsumerMemoryFulltextSearchInput`, `ConsumerMemoryStructuredQueryInput` | `ConsumerMemoryFulltextSearchInput`, `ConsumerMemoryStructuredQueryInput` | Caller-reviewed keyword, pagination, and exact top-level scalar metadata filters. The typed input validates Product API field count, key format, scalar type, encoded size, and limits before sending. |
| `sourceType`, `usageType` | `source_type`, `usage_type` | Connector source/usage type values selected from backend-supported connector metadata or explicit user input. |

| `modelEndpoint` | `model_endpoint` | Provider backend endpoint from application configuration, secret storage, or explicit user input. |

| `agentInputAnswers` | `agent_input_answers` | Agent task input form values, A2A input-request schema, UI state, or explicit user input. |

## Capability Source

The capability inventory starts from backend route registration, not frontend code and not a handwritten SDK registry.

Current backend evidence:

- `moi-backend/pkg/authzpolicy/registrar.go` wraps product route registration through `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, and `Any`.
- `moi-backend/pkg/authzpolicy/policies.go` records product route paths, entry mode, and canonical enforcement metadata.
- Handler packages such as `workflow`, `catalog`, `session`, `llm`, `compute`, `agentresource`, `sqleditor`, `explore`, and `workspacemember` attach the actual handlers.
- `moi-backend/cmd/sdk-route-manifest` builds the product Gin router in test mode and exports `router.Routes()` as the authoritative route manifest.
- The route-alignment source scanner cross-checks that runtime manifest so unregistered handler files do not become SDK coverage facts. Public, protocol, bootstrap, internal, and intentionally unregistered direct routes are allowed only through explicit route-alignment exemptions with reasons.

The generated backend route manifest should be grouped into capability areas for reports and documentation. Those groups are derived output. They must not become a second source of truth.

## Relation Types

Use these relation types when documenting an SDK method:

| Type | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| `product-owned` | Product backend owns the state and UI read contract. `moi-core` may not be involved. |
| `core-backed` | `moi-core` owns durable resource metadata or runtime state, and product backend exposes it through product routes. |
| `core-assisted` | Product backend owns the UI operation and calls `moi-core` for part of the work. |
| `backend-proxy-to-core` | Product backend currently forwards a product route to `moi-core` with product auth and workspace context. This is still a product endpoint for the SDK, but the proxy must preserve UI visibility and auth behavior. |
| `non-core-service` | The route depends on another backend service such as connector, SQL editor, or other product infrastructure rather than `moi-core`. |

The Product SDK client calls only the product endpoint. It may use a UI token/session or a user-scoped API key accepted by `moi-backend`. It may reuse shared/generated `moi-core` model types where they are already the backend contract, but it must not create a core SDK client or call catalog/mowl/core endpoints directly.

Apps product routes are not Product SDK operations. The route manifest must mark `/newmoi/apps/**` as `not_applicable`, and the Go SDK, Python SDK, CLI, capability lists, and smoke suite must not expose app contracts or app handlers.

## Capability Areas

| Area | SDK needs | Product route groups | UI-visible result | Relationship to `moi-core` |
|------|-----------|----------------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|
| Identity, workspace, RBAC, audit | Login/session helper, user API key auth, workspace list/create/update/delete, member lifecycle and canonical role assignment, operation logs | `/newmoi/account/**`, `/newmoi/user/**`, `/newmoi/user/me/**`, `/newmoi/workspaces/**`, `/newmoi/audit/**`, `/newmoi/log/**` | Workspace switcher, member pages, canonical IAM role/policy pages, audit/log pages | Product SDK owns account and supported member operations; canonical role/policy control remains in Core through the Backend BFF. API key auth must still go through product PEP/audit and must not become direct core catalog access. |
| Catalog, files, data assets | Catalog/database/volume/table CRUD, folder CRUD, file upload/create/list/info/update/delete/download/preview, table data/load/preview/download, data asset and file artifact lookup | `/newmoi/catalog/**` | Catalog tree, database/table/volume detail pages, file browser, file preview, data asset panels | `core-backed` and `core-assisted`. Backend maps product routes to `moi-core` catalog, database, volume, table, volume-file, and fileservice behavior while enforcing product RBAC and UI read models. Folder is a product view over core volume semantics. |
| Knowledge and semantic models | Semantic model CRUD, entry CRUD, import/export, validation, table/file source binding | `/newmoi/semantic-models/**` | Knowledge base list/detail, semantic model editor, downstream workflow/agent source pickers | `core-backed`. Backend exposes product knowledge routes over `moi-core` semantic model resources and applies product object permissions. |
| Workflow design and runtime | Workitem catalog, custom operator CRUD/test/enable/disable, workflow deployment, workflow app list/get/update/delete, run/list/get/refresh/result/import-context/import-files/node detail, retry/cancel/delete, node rerun and rerun branch operations | `/newmoi/workflow/v2/**`, `/newmoi/workflow-templates/**` | Workflow list, editor, node panel, custom operator page, execution list/detail, run result pages | `core-assisted` and `core-backed`. `moi-core/mowl` and catalog own low-level workflow/runtime/workitem/custom-operator resources. Product backend owns the UI-visible workflow app, editor metadata, permissions, and execution read contract. Creating only a core workflow/task is not product-complete. |
| Agent resources and conversations | Agent/resource CRUD, runtime manifests/providers/tasks/turn snapshots, task templates, workflow bindings, connections, conversations, feedback, files, knowledge bases, model configs, operations, runtime policy profiles, skills, tools, A2A card/send/stream | `/newmoi/workspaces/:id/{agents,agent-runtime,agent-runtime-manifests,agent-runtime-providers,agent-runtime-tasks,agent-runtime-turn-snapshots,agent-task-templates,agent-workflow-bindings,connections,conversations,feedback,files,knowledge-bases,model-configs,operations,runtime-policy-profiles,skills,tools}/**`, `/newmoi/agents/**` | Agent management pages, conversation list/detail, runtime/task status, skill/tool panels | Mainly `backend-proxy-to-core` for agent resource/read-model routes today, plus `core-assisted` for A2A. Product backend must still enforce product auth, workspace ID consistency, and UI visibility. If a proxied core resource is not enough for the UI, the backend contract must be promoted before the SDK exposes it. |
| Lineage and artifact review | File lineage overview, artifact overview/output blocks, block revisions, effective revision switch, node detail, node output blocks, node rerun, revision-aware rerun | `/newmoi/lineage/**` | File lineage panels, artifact/node inspection, rerun-from-artifact flows | `core-backed` through workflow/runtime/artifact lineage data. Backend owns product route shape and permission checks. SDK should expose read/revision/rerun operations only through these product routes. |
| Model, embedding, parser, provider routing | LLM backend CRUD, backend endpoint create/list/status, router get/update, model list, backend setup/probe, embedding backend CRUD/router/probe, parser backend CRUD/router | `/newmoi/llm/**`, `/newmoi/embedding/**`, `/newmoi/parser/**`, `/newmoi/models` | Model management pages, provider/backend status, model selectors used by workflow and agent pages | `core-backed` and `core-assisted`. Backend uses `moi-core` model/provider/router APIs and protects secrets, status, and workspace scoping through product permissions. SDK must not write provider secrets directly to core. |
| Connectors, load tasks, export tasks | Connector CRUD/validate, connector file upload/preview/download/delete/stream, DB/schema/table browsing, import migration, MO target database/table mapping, load task CRUD/pause/resume/retry/status/files/runs, export task create/list/info/files/delete/rerun/state, processed volume files | `/newmoi/connectors/**`, `/newmoi/task/**`, `/newmoi/export/**` | Data connection pages, import/export task pages, task run status, catalog assets created by imports | Mostly `non-core-service`, with `core-assisted` when import/export creates or reads catalog/file/table resources. Connector services and product task tables own task visibility; core catalog owns resulting data assets where applicable. |
| SQL editor, metadata, query history | Workbook CRUD/versioning, metadata database/table/column/DDL/drop helpers, query execute/describe/kill/result/download/ws, query history overview/list/detail/profile | `/newmoi/workbook/**`, `/newmoi/meta/**`, `/newmoi/query/**` | SQL editor, workbook list/detail, metadata explorer, query result and history pages | Mostly `product-owned` and `non-core-service`. Backend executes against tenant MatrixOne connections and records product query/workbook/history state. `moi-core` is only a helper where backend needs system metadata or shared IDs. |
| Compute resources | Create/list/get/update/delete, worker image list, delete preflight, suspend/resume/retry | `/newmoi/compute-resources/**` | Compute resource pages, worker image picker, status panels, delete-preflight warnings | Product route is authoritative. Relationship to `moi-core` is `core-assisted` only where workflow/mowl/worker runtime consumes these resources. Backend owns UI-visible resource status and binding checks. |
| Data sharing | Publish summary/list/check/create/update/delete, subscription list/subscribe/unsubscribe | `/newmoi/data-share/**` | Data share publish/subscription pages | Primarily `non-core-service` or product metadata over database publication/subscription behavior. Any core/catalog IDs must be surfaced only through product routes. |
| Consumer memory and profiles | Consumer memory retrieve/search/fulltext/query, consumer profile snapshot/render | `/newmoi/memories/**`, `/newmoi/consumer-profiles/**` | Consumer memory and profile panels | `non-core-service`. Backend resolves the workspace Memoria instance and proxies memory reads; consumer profile snapshot/render is owned by Backend storage and services. The SDK only calls Product APIs and never talks to Memoria directly. |

### Capability Area Promotion Map

Use this map to audit whether each capability area is covered by promoted user-story tasks and outcome-level journeys. A capability area is SDK-friendly only when its promoted tasks exist in the User Story Matrix and its outcome usage is present in at least one Cross-Domain User Journey, or when a route-aligned exception is explicit.

| Capability area | Promoted User Story Matrix tasks | Cross-Domain User Journeys | Route-aligned exception |
|-----------------|----------------------------------|----------------------------|-------------------------|
| Identity, workspace, RBAC, audit | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity; Manage workspace members | Data onboarding to queryable analytics; Workflow execution with traceable outputs; Governed knowledge-assisted exploration; Connector data movement and sharing; Workflow platform extension and API publishing; Workspace administration and operations | None. |
| Catalog, files, data assets | Browse catalog resources and operate tables; Manage volumes, folders, and files | Data onboarding to queryable analytics; Workflow execution with traceable outputs; Connector data movement and sharing | None. |
| Knowledge and semantic models | Manage semantic models and entries | Governed knowledge-assisted exploration | None. |
| Workflow design and runtime | Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows; Design workflow custom operators and templates; Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Workflow execution with traceable outputs; Workflow platform extension and API publishing | None. |
| Agent resources and conversations | Use product agents through A2A | Governed knowledge-assisted exploration | Agent resource CRUD/runtime proxy routes remain route-aligned until backend promotes stable product contracts. |
| Lineage and artifact review | Review lineage artifacts and rerun from nodes/blocks | Workflow execution with traceable outputs | None. |
| Model, embedding, parser, provider routing | Configure model providers and routers | Workflow platform extension and API publishing; Workspace administration and operations | None. |
| Connectors, load tasks, export tasks | Manage connectors plus import/export tasks; Manage volumes, folders, and files | Connector data movement and sharing | None. |
| SQL editor, metadata, query history | Work in the SQL editor and query history | Data onboarding to queryable analytics; Connector data movement and sharing | None. |
| Compute resources | Manage compute resources | Workflow execution with traceable outputs; Workspace administration and operations | None. |
| Data sharing | Publish and subscribe data shares | Connector data movement and sharing; Workspace administration and operations | None. |
| Consumer memory and profiles | Retrieve consumer memories and render profiles | Governed knowledge-assisted exploration | None. |

## SDK Surface Shape

The route-aligned service shape follows the product domains above. This surface
remains the lower-level escape hatch for explicit route control, generated route
coverage, and domains that do not yet have a promoted user-story facade. AI
callers and new user-facing examples should start from the User Story Matrix
when a handle/spec entry exists.

Handle service accessors such as `CatalogHandle.Service` / `CatalogHandle.service`
are route-aligned escape hatches only. They exist so advanced callers can reach
the underlying scoped service after the selected User Story Flow Recipe proves
that no promoted handle/spec method covers the requested route-level control.
They must not appear in task quick starts, README Example task indexes,
Representative Method Coverage, Supplemental Handle Method Coverage, or
promoted copyable examples. AI-written code must prefer the typed handle method
named by the matched user task and use the service accessor only after documenting
the explicit route-level need.

### Route-Aligned Service Promotion Map

Use this map to audit every public route-aligned `Client.<domain>` service before treating it as user-friendly. Each service must either point to promoted User Story Matrix tasks or declare an explicit route-aligned exclusion. AI-written code must use the promoted task entries for user-facing workflows and use the route-aligned service only for explicit route-level control.

| Go Client service | Python Client service | Capability area or exclusion | Promoted user-story tasks | AI rule |
|-------------------|-----------------------|------------------------------|---------------------------|---------|
| `Client.Workspaces` | `client.workspaces` | Identity, workspace, RBAC, audit | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Prefer `Client.EnsureWorkspace`, `Client.ensure_workspace`, and `WorkspaceHandle` for workspace bootstrap. |
| `Client.Account` | `client.account` | Identity, workspace, RBAC, audit | Bootstrap a UI workspace and inspect identity | Prefer `WorkspaceHandle.DBConnection` / `db_connection` for workspace DB connection flows. |
| None. | `client.auth` | Python-only bootstrap route-aligned exclusion | None. | Keep auth bootstrap routes as explicit route-level controls; prefer typed client construction, auth options / and workspace bootstrap helpers for user-facing workflows. |
| None. | `client.system` | Python-only diagnostics route-aligned exclusion | None. | Keep system/version diagnostics as explicit route-level controls; prefer typed endpoint/bootstrap readiness checks before route-level system calls. |
| `Client.WorkspaceMembers` | `client.workspace_members` | Identity, workspace, RBAC, audit | Manage workspace members | Prefer `WorkspaceMemberHandle` for member lifecycle flows; use `Client.IAM` / `client.iam` for canonical role bindings. |
| `Client.IAM` | `client.iam` | Identity, workspace, RBAC, audit | Manage workspace members | Prefer typed workspace-scoped IAM schema, role lifecycle, and object-grant operations; preserve lifecycle and policy CAS versions from typed read results. |
| `Client.Catalog` | `client.catalog` | Catalog, files, data assets | Browse catalog resources and operate tables; Manage volumes, folders, and files | Prefer catalog/database/table/volume/folder/file handles before route-level catalog calls. |
| `Client.Knowledge` | `client.knowledge` | Knowledge and semantic models | Manage semantic models and entries | Prefer `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, and `SemanticEntryInput` for semantic model lifecycle flows. |
| `Client.Workflows` | `client.workflows` | Workflow design and runtime | Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows; Design workflow custom operators and templates; Publish and invoke WorkItem API services | Prefer workflow, run, custom-operator, template, and WorkItem API service handles for workflow user tasks. |
| `Client.Agents` | `client.agents` | Agent resources and conversations | Use product agents through A2A | Prefer `AgentHandle`, `AgentTextMessage`, `AgentTaskRequest`, and `AgentInputSubmission`; resource proxy routes remain route-aligned. |
| `Client.Webapp` | `client.webapp` | Agent resources and conversations | Use product agents through A2A | Prefer AgentHandle for publisher-authenticated workflows; keep anonymous public link/session and artifact calls route-aligned, and never attach product credentials. |
| `Client.Models` | `client.models` | Model, embedding, parser, provider routing | Configure model providers and routers | Prefer `ModelProviderHandle`, `ProviderBackendHandle`, and `ProviderRouterHandle` for provider lifecycle and router tasks. |
| `Client.Connectors` | `client.connectors` | Connectors, load tasks, export tasks | Manage connectors plus import/export tasks; Manage volumes, folders, and files | Prefer `ConnectorHandle`, connector file handles, import specs, and export specs for connector user tasks. |
| `Client.SQL` | `client.sql` | SQL editor, metadata, query history | Work in the SQL editor and query history | Prefer SQL metadata, workbook, query, and statement handles for SQL editor workflows. |
| `Client.Compute` | `client.compute` | Compute resources | Manage compute resources | Prefer `ComputeResourceHandle` for compute lifecycle and workflows. |
| `Client.Dashboard` | `client.dashboard` | Workflow design and runtime | Deploy, run, inspect, rerun, and cancel workflows | Prefer workflow and run handles for user-facing task lifecycle work; use dashboard summary APIs only for explicit route-level status panel coverage. |
| `Client.DataShare` | `client.data_share` | Data sharing | Publish and subscribe data shares | Prefer `DataSharePublishHandle` and `DataShareSubscriptionHandle` for share workflows. |
| `Client.Memory` | `client.memory` | Consumer memory and profiles | Retrieve consumer memories and render profiles | Prefer `ConsumerMemoryHandle` for semantic retrieval, typed keyword/metadata queries, typed structured queries, and profile render workflows. Use route-aligned services only when explicit route parity is required. |

### Route-Aligned Service Examples

These examples are route-reachability anchors only. They are not quick-start
entries, not copy anchors, and not task-level examples; use them only after the
Route-Aligned Service Promotion Map proves no promoted handle/spec entry covers
the requested outcome or explicit route-level control is required.

`go
client, err := sdk.New(productEndpoint, sdk.WithAPIKey(apiKey), sdk.WithTimeout(timeout))
client.Workspaces()
client.Account()
client.WorkspaceMembers(workspaceID)
client.IAM(workspaceID)
client.Catalog(workspaceID)
client.Knowledge(workspaceID)
client.Workflows(workspaceID)
client.Agents(workspaceID)
client.Webapp()
client.Models(workspaceID)
client.Connectors(workspaceID)
client.SQL(workspaceID)
client.Compute(workspaceID)
client.Memory(workspaceID)
client.Dashboard(workspaceID)
client.DataShare(workspaceID)
`

`python
client = sdk.Client(product_endpoint, sdk.with_api_key(api_key), sdk.with_timeout(timeout))
client.workspaces()
client.account()
client.system()
client.workspace_members(workspace_id)
client.iam(workspace_id)
client.catalog(workspace_id)
client.knowledge(workspace_id)
client.workflows(workspace_id)
client.agents(workspace_id)
client.webapp()
client.models(workspace_id)
client.connectors(workspace_id)
client.sql(workspace_id)
client.compute(workspace_id)
client.memory(workspace_id)
client.dashboard(workspace_id)
client.data_share(workspace_id)
`

User-story facades sit above the route-aligned services. They must compose
existing product routes and return handles for user-visible resources instead
of exposing backend route names as the first user experience:

Example selection rule: the large user-story snippets below are a reference
catalog, not one complete runnable workflow. For a user request, first choose
the Cross-Domain User Journey or User Story Matrix task, then copy only the
minimal ordered lines required by that task's User Story Flow Recipe. Do not
combine unrelated lines from the catalog into one generated workflow.

`go
workspace, err := client.EnsureWorkspace(ctx, workspaceName)
profile, err := workspace.DBConnection(ctx)
db, err := workspace.DBConnection(ctx)
catalog, err := workspace.PrepareCatalog(ctx, catalogName)
database, err := catalog.PrepareDatabase(ctx, databaseName)
table, createdTable, err := database.CreateTable(ctx, tableName, tableColumns, sdk.WithTableDataFileURL(tableDataURL))
rows, err := table.Data(ctx, sdk.WithTableDataPageSize(tablePageSize))
previewRows, err := table.Preview(ctx, sdk.WithTablePreviewLines(tablePreviewLines))
volume, err := database.PrepareVolume(ctx, volumeName)
volume, err = workspace.PrepareDataVolume(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName)
uploaded, err := volume.UploadBytes(ctx, fileName, csvBytes)
file, err := volume.UploadBytesHandle(ctx, fileName, csvBytes)
localFile, err := volume.UploadFileHandle(ctx, localFilePath)
volumePreview, err := volume.UploadBytesPreview(ctx, fileName, csvBytes)
localVolumePreview, err := volume.UploadFilePreview(ctx, localFilePath)
file, err = workspace.UploadBytesToDataVolume(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName, fileName, csvBytes)
localFile, err = workspace.UploadFileToDataVolume(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName, localFilePath)
previewed, err := workspace.UploadBytesPreviewToDataVolume(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName, fileName, csvBytes)
localPreviewed, err := workspace.UploadFilePreviewToDataVolume(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName, localFilePath)
volume, err = workspace.AttachDataVolumeWorkflow(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName, workflowID)
volume, err = workspace.DetachDataVolumeWorkflow(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName, workflowID)
path, err := volume.FullPath(ctx)
refs, err := volume.Refs(ctx)
path, err = workspace.DataVolumeFullPath(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName)
refs, err = workspace.DataVolumeRefs(ctx, catalogName, databaseName, volumeName)
info, err := file.Info(ctx)
// First deployment: let the server allocate the ID. Pass an existing ID to publish a new version.
workflow, deployed, err := workspace.DeployWorkflow(ctx, workflowName, dslYAML, "")
workflowID := workflow.ID()
workflow, err = workspace.Workflow(workflowID)
runData, err := workflow.Run(ctx, sdk.WithWorkflowRunInput(workflowRunInput))
fileLineage, err := file.LineageOverview(ctx)
artifact, err := workspace.Artifact(artifactID)
artifactBlocks, err := artifact.OutputBlocks(ctx)
artifactNode, err := artifact.Node(nodeID)
nodeRerun, err := artifactNode.Rerun(ctx, sdk.WithLineageRerunMode(lineageRerunMode))
run, err := workflow.RunHandle(executionID)
runNode, err := run.NodeHandle(nodeKey)
rerunPlan, err := runNode.RerunPlan(ctx, sdk.WithQueryParam("source_node_execution_id", nodeExecutionID))
createdRerun, err := runNode.CreateRerun(ctx, workflowRerunMode, nodeExecutionID, sdk.WithExpectedWorkflowRerunContractHash(rerunPlan.GetPlan().GetRerunContractHash()))
rerun, err := run.Rerun(rerunID)
rerunInfo, err := rerun.Info(ctx)
result, err := run.Result(ctx)
cancelled, err := run.Cancel(ctx, sdk.WithWorkflowCancelReason(workflowCancelReason))
operatorSpec := sdk.CustomOperatorSpec{Name: operatorName, Identifier: operatorIdentifier, Description: operatorDescription, InputSchema: inputSchema, OutputSchema: outputSchema}
