The names can change during API design. Go and Python coverage must not diverge.

The SDK method style must stay aligned with `moi-core` SDKs. Required arguments are explicit. Optional fields and behavior use typed options:

`go
run, err := workflow.Run(ctx,
 sdk.WithWorkflowRunInput(input),
)
`

`python
run = workflow.run(
 sdk.with_workflow_run_input(input),
)
`

Do not introduce a separate request-object-first or untyped-map-first public style for Product SDK methods when `moi-core` already uses options for the same class of optional parameters.

Low-level escape-hatch examples:

Use these only when the corresponding user-story handle does not exist or when
the caller needs explicit route-level control. New task-level documentation
should prefer the handle/spec examples above.

`go
workspace, err := client.Workspaces().Create(ctx, workspaceName)
err = client.Workspaces().Update(ctx, workspace.GetId(), sdk.WithWorkspaceName(renamedWorkspaceName))
users, err := client.WorkspaceMembers(workspace.GetId()).Members(ctx)
nameAvailable, err := workspace.DataShare().CheckPublishName(ctx, sdk.WithQueryParam("name", publishName))
`

```python
users = client.workspace_members(workspace.id).members()
```

## Method Documentation Template

Every SDK method design should include:

| Field | Required content |
|-------|------------------|
| Capability area | One of the areas in this document. |
| User Story Matrix task | Promoted task name when the method is part of a high-level facade; otherwise document the route-aligned exception. |
| Product operation | User-visible action, not internal core step. |
| Handle/spec entry point | Go/Python handle, spec, or route-aligned service entry exposed to callers. |
| User Story Outcome Contract | User-visible outcome, continuation handle/spec, and caller-owned inputs or decisions when the method is promoted. |
| Return shape | Compatible Go/Python call shape such as handle, handle+result, handle-list+result, typed-result, stream, scalar, or error-only. |
| AI usage boundary | Whether AI-written code should prefer this method, use a promoted handle/spec instead, or treat it as an explicit route-level escape hatch. |
| Backend route mapping | Method/path list from the generated backend route manifest. |
| UI read contract | Product route or backend read model used by the UI to show the result. |
| UI surface | Page or panel where the result appears. |
| Source rule | Where existing ids, caller inputs, selected resources, credentials, and enum values must come from. |
| Failure boundary | Explicit errors for missing ids, ambiguous resources, invalid input, route-aligned exceptions, or unsupported promoted behavior. |
| Stability evidence | User Story Stability Evidence row and Go/Python facade tests when the method is promoted. |
| `moi-core` relationship | `product-owned`, `core-backed`, `core-assisted`, `backend-proxy-to-core`, or `non-core-service`. |
| Core resources touched | Core resource names only if the backend touches core. |
| SDK options | Go option type plus `WithX` helpers, and Python `with_x` helpers. |
| Permission model | Workspace scope, object type, global/object privileges, and audit/log behavior. |
| Async model | Task/run/status/polling or streaming contract, if any. |
| Integration test | Representative SDK write followed by product UI read contract. |

This template is per SDK method. It is not a route registry. Route alignment still decides whether the SDK covers all required backend product routes.

## First Implementation Order

Start with the areas that currently create the biggest gap between core SDK operations and UI visibility:

1. Identity/workspace bootstrap and permission boundary checks.
2. Catalog/files and knowledge resources required by workflow/agent inputs.
3. Workflow design/runtime, including custom operators and execution inspection.
4. Agent A2A task operations that already have stable product contracts.
5. Model/provider/parser/embedding configuration needed by workflow and agent.
6. Agent resource and runtime user stories only after the backend promotes stable product request/response contracts beyond the current `ANY` proxy routes.

After those are stable, expand route coverage by the generated route alignment report until every `sdk_exposure: required` product route has Go and Python SDK coverage or an explicit backend-owned exemption.

## Backend API Promotion Checklist

Use this checklist when upstream/backend adds or changes a product API. Passing route alignment only proves the route is reachable from the low-level SDK service; it does not prove the SDK is friendly, typed, or ready for users.

| Step | Required evidence | Gate that should fail if missing |
|------|-------------------|----------------------------------|
| Classify the API | Decide whether the API is Product SDK `not_applicable`, route-level control only, Agent resource proxy, or a user-facing product task. Document exclusions explicitly instead of promoting them. | Route alignment and capability maturity exemptions. |
| Add route coverage | Map the backend route in Go and Python route-aligned services through the product endpoint and product RBAC layer. Do not call `moi-core/catalog` directly. | Route alignment. |
| Promote typed contracts | If the API returns or accepts product data for a non-exempt user task, define shared product protobuf contracts under `sdk/proto/product`, regenerate Go/Python models, and remove `ProductData`, raw request bodies, and generic containers from the promoted path. | Capability maturity. |
| Design the user task | Add or update the User Story Matrix row with Go/Python handle/spec entry points. Keep low-level `client.<domain>(workspaceID)` services out of promoted rows. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Add flow guidance | Add or update the AI Task Selection Guide, AI Intent Disambiguation Matrix, User Context Entry Matrix, User Story Ergonomic Usage Tiers, User Story Operation Intent Coverage, User Story Capability Availability Coverage, User Story Abstraction Fitness, and User Story Flow Recipes so AI code knows the ordered calls, source rules, minimal-path/advanced-control/escape-boundary tiers, operation-intent lifecycle coverage, capability availability checks, user-shaped abstraction proof, context-to-starting-object rule, and overlapping-intent decision rule. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define capability availability | Add or update the User Story Capability Availability Coverage row so availability probe, unavailable signal, required dependency/configuration, forbidden availability workaround, and AI-safe unavailable behavior are explicit before choosing method names against the current product endpoint, backend version, feature configuration, workspace, and dependent service state. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define state handoff | Add or update the User Story State Handoff Coverage row so carried typed state, public accessor/result source, next-call binding, forbidden handoff shortcut, and AI-safe handoff behavior are explicit before passing values between SDK calls. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define selection disambiguation | Add or update the User Story Selection Disambiguation Coverage row so selection input, uniqueness rule, pagination/search boundary, ambiguous/missing selection handling, and forbidden selection shortcut are explicit before selecting one resource from list, search, name, or paginated results. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define ordering recency | Add or update the User Story Ordering and Recency Coverage row so ordering intent, authoritative order source, recency/time-window source, forbidden ordering shortcut, and AI-safe ordering behavior are explicit before honoring latest, recent, oldest, first, top, sorted, priority, or time-window wording. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define completion outcome | Add or update the User Story Outcome Contract so the task names the user-visible result, continuation handle/spec, and caller-owned inputs or decisions before it is considered complete. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define interaction mode | Add or update the User Story Interaction Mode Coverage row so the task states synchronous, long-running, streaming, pagination, batch, destructive, cancel, force, and approval behavior before AI-written code chooses supplemental methods. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define execution budget | Add or update the User Story Execution Budget Coverage row so timeout/deadline source, polling/stream stop condition, page/batch limit, retry budget, and approval/escalation boundary are explicit before polling, streaming, paginating, retrying, or waiting on long-running work. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define pagination partial results | Add or update the User Story Pagination and Partial Result Coverage row so paginated/partial surfaces, continuation token/source, complete-result proof, forbidden partial-result shortcut, and AI-safe pagination behavior are explicit before treating first pages, previews, streams, batches, or capped reads as complete. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define error recovery | Add or update the User Story Error Recovery Coverage row so retryable failures, user-correctable failures, forbidden recovery behavior, and AI-safe next action are explicit before adding retries, reruns, prompts, or fallback behavior. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define permission scope | Add or update the User Story Permission and Scope Coverage row so product scope, permission-sensitive operations, caller-owned authorization boundary, forbidden scope/RBAC behavior, and AI-safe permission handling are explicit before issuing permission-sensitive calls or choosing workspace/resource scope. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define ownership collaboration | Add or update the User Story Ownership and Collaboration Coverage row so ownership evidence, collaborator/shared scope, caller-owned decision boundary, forbidden cross-user shortcut, and AI-safe collaboration behavior are explicit before modifying, exposing, deleting, sharing, or continuing work on shared workspace resources. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define input construction | Add or update the User Story Input Construction Coverage row so required caller inputs, derived typed-result inputs, option/enum/secret rules, forbidden construction behavior, and AI-safe construction are explicit before constructing specs, options, enum values, secrets, or payloads. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define value format units | Add or update the User Story Value Format and Units Coverage row so value/format contract, unit/timezone authority, enum/identifier format, forbidden format shortcut, and AI-safe value behavior are explicit before serializing identifiers, timestamps, durations, byte sizes, limits, enum values, URLs, SQL, cron, or payload fields. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define defaults and omissions | Add or update the User Story Default and Omission Coverage row so optional omissions, invalid empty/zero values, backend/default authority, forbidden defaulting behavior, and AI-safe omission behavior are explicit before relying on omitted fields, empty values, zero values, or backend defaults. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define completion verification | Add or update the User Story Completion Verification Coverage row so completion signal, verification call, async/delete/cancel handling, forbidden completion shortcut, and AI-safe stop condition are explicit before treating writes, async starts, deletes, cancels, or error-only calls as complete. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define result accessors | Add or update the User Story Result Accessor Coverage row so public result/accessor source, user-readable fields, continuation fields, forbidden extraction shortcut, and AI-safe accessor behavior are explicit before reading returned values. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define output consumption | Add or update the User Story Output Consumption Coverage row so user-facing output, continuation output, sensitive/internal output boundary, forbidden output consumption, and AI-safe consumption behavior are explicit before displaying or reusing returned values. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define sensitive data handling | Add or update the User Story Sensitive Data Handling Coverage row so sensitive inputs, sensitive outputs, logging/example boundary, persistence/AI-context boundary, and AI-safe handling are explicit before logging, persisting, showing examples, or passing SDK inputs/outputs into AI context. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define automation safety | Add or update the User Story Automation Safety Coverage row so repeat-safe behavior, idempotency key or lookup, external/cost side-effect boundary, forbidden automation shortcut, and AI-safe automation behavior are explicit before repeating or automating SDK calls. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define concurrent submission | Add or update the User Story Concurrent Submission Coverage row so operation identity, parallel-safe reads, serialized mutation boundary, forbidden duplicate submission, and AI-safe in-flight behavior are explicit before parallel SDK calls, UI double-clicks, background polls, async retries, or multiple AI workers act on the same task. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define impact assessment | Add or update the User Story Impact Assessment Coverage row so impact source, affected scope, cost/external side effects, uncertainty boundary, and AI-safe impact summary are explicit before asking for confirmation or executing high-impact SDK operations. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define confirmation preview | Add or update the User Story Confirmation and Preview Coverage row so confirmation trigger, preview/preflight source, user-visible confirmation summary, forbidden implicit approval, and AI-safe confirmation behavior are explicit before destructive, cost-incurring, external, data-moving, permission-changing, or irreversible SDK operations. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define abort cleanup | Add or update the User Story Abort and Cleanup Coverage row so abort trigger, cleanup/compensation target, state proof before cleanup, forbidden rollback shortcut, and AI-safe abort behavior are explicit before abandoning, canceling, compensating, or cleaning up partial SDK work. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define progress visibility | Add or update the User Story Progress Visibility Coverage row so progress signal, status/read call, user-visible update, forbidden silent wait, and AI-safe progress reporting are explicit before waiting on, streaming, paginating, batching, deleting, canceling, or otherwise monitoring SDK work. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define diagnostic handoff | Add or update the User Story Diagnostic Handoff Coverage row so safe diagnostic context, correlation/status handle, sensitive diagnostic boundary, forbidden diagnostic shortcut, and AI-safe diagnostic handoff are explicit before handing failures or stuck tasks to a user, support engineer, log search, or later AI step. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define freshness conflict | Add or update the User Story Freshness and Conflict Coverage row so fresh-state source, conflict/stale signal, destructive/update conflict boundary, forbidden stale-state shortcut, and AI-safe conflict handling are explicit before updating, deleting, canceling, rerunning, publishing, subscribing, executing, or otherwise acting on previously read state. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Compose outcomes | Add or update Cross-Domain User Journeys, Cross-Domain Journey Example Anchors, Cross-Domain Journey Risk Coverage, and the Capability Area Promotion Map when the task participates in an outcome-level workflow. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Implement language parity | Add Go and Python facade methods, handle/spec types, package exports, compatible parameter names, compatible parameter shapes, compatible return shapes, and representative facade tests with matching semantics. | User-story facade alignment and language SDK tests. |
| Prove stability | Add or update the User Story Stability Evidence row with the real Go/Python facade tests, stable proof, and failure boundary for the promoted task. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define representative methods | Add or update the User Story Representative Method Coverage row with the minimum Go/Python calls that must appear in language README examples for the promoted task. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Define example scenario | Add or update the User Story Example Scenario Coverage row so user scenario, copyable example anchor, inputs to replace, expected user-visible output, and AI adaptation boundary are explicit before adapting examples. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Document examples | Update root, Go, and Python quick-start/example docs with minimal snippets that follow the selection rule, AI Intent Disambiguation Matrix, User Context Entry Matrix, User Story Ergonomic Usage Tiers, User Story Operation Intent Coverage, User Story Capability Availability Coverage, User Story Abstraction Fitness, User Story Example Scenario Coverage, State Handoff Coverage, Selection Disambiguation Coverage, Ordering and Recency Coverage, Cross-Domain Journey Example Anchors, Interaction Mode Coverage, Execution Budget Coverage, Pagination and Partial Result Coverage, Error Recovery Coverage, Permission and Scope Coverage, Ownership and Collaboration Coverage, Input Construction Coverage, Value Format and Units Coverage, Default and Omission Coverage, Completion Verification Coverage, Result Accessor Coverage, Output Consumption Coverage, Sensitive Data Handling Coverage, Automation Safety Coverage, Concurrent Submission Coverage, Impact Assessment Coverage, Confirmation and Preview Coverage, Abort and Cleanup Coverage, Progress Visibility Coverage, Diagnostic Handoff Coverage, Freshness and Conflict Coverage, and Risk Coverage, and avoid invented ids, copyable runtime identifiers, raw maps, silently defaulted input variables, and error-only write results as sources. | User-story facade alignment. |
| Verify end to end | Run `make test` from `sdk/` so route alignment, capability maturity, Go tests, Python tests, and user-story facade gates all execute together. | SDK test suite. |

## CI Gate Relationship

The route alignment gate should use this document only for grouping/report labels. It must fail based on generated facts:

- backend route exists and is SDK-required
- Go SDK method maps to that route
- Python SDK method maps to that route
- mapped Go/Python SDK methods exist in source
- mapped Go/Python `sdk_method` entries stay out of promoted examples unless the same outcome is backed by user-story handle/spec evidence
- mapped Go/Python route entries use explicit language, HTTP method, path, and kind fields, and each route key has matching Go/Python kind semantics; product paths must stay under `/newmoi`; `ANY` is allowed only for Agent resource proxy routes; `resource` routes must use `ANY`; the gate reads Go `RouteMappings()` directly and parses Python route dictionaries by field name so field order cannot hide drift
- route mapping metadata is for alignment gates and diagnostics only; it is not a user-story SDK entry point, AI task-selection source, or example anchor
- SDK methods do not call core endpoints directly
- direct Gin product routes do not bypass the alignment source set

Adding a new backend product route for low-level reachability does not require
editing this document when it fits an existing product domain and stays out of
friendly examples. Promoting that route as user-friendly SDK behavior does
require User Story Matrix, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, safety coverage,
Representative Method Coverage or Supplemental Handle Method Coverage, example
anchors, tests, and either Route-Aligned Service Promotion Map or User Story Gap
Register evidence. A new route-aligned `sdk_method` must remain a route coverage
fact, not an AI copy anchor, until that evidence exists.


The gate does not parse README snippets or Example task index Replace/Result/source columns to prove documentation variable completeness. README examples and example indexes remain documentation aids, while API/SDK implementation gates stay focused on backend route reachability, Go/Python route mappings, promoted facade presence, cross-language shape parity, and raw-shape regressions.

The Supplemental Handle Method Coverage gate checks every documented `Handle.Method` entry against the actual Go and Python SDK handle methods, so supplemental guidance cannot advertise methods that users or AI cannot call.

The public handle task-coverage gate checks non-common Go and Python handle methods against User Story Representative Method Coverage and Supplemental Handle Method Coverage, so new task-specific facade methods cannot appear in source without a user-story task boundary. Representative coverage may satisfy a bare method name only when that method name is unique across public handle methods; repeated method names must be covered as exact `Handle.Method` entries in Supplemental Handle Method Coverage. Common method coverage only applies to handles named by the User Story Matrix. Common lifecycle, read, selection, navigation, and inspection methods remain governed by the full User Story coverage matrices instead of turning the supplemental table into a method catalog.

The user-story entry discovery gate scans exported Go and Python SDK symbols that look like high-level user entries: `*Handle`, `*Spec`, `*Input`, `*Message`, `*Request`, `*Submission`, `*Config`, `*Selector`, `*Profile`, `*Preview`, `*Sources`, `*Switch`, user-facing file wrapper types, and exported low-level protocol constants such as A2A method values. It also scans direct `Client` methods that return handles or typed user-story results, such as `Client.Workspace` / `client.workspace` and `DBConnectionResult` / `db_connection`. A discovered symbol or direct client entry must either appear in the User Story Matrix or be listed in the explicit low-level/user-story exception table below with the recommended promoted entry point. This prevents a new facade-shaped SDK type, protocol constant, or client-level entry from becoming invisible to AI task selection.

The raw facade input gate scans public `*Handle` methods for open request shapes such as `map[string]any`, `dict[str, Any]`, `Any`, or JSON-RPC payloads. A raw handle method must have a typed companion such as `Create...FromSpec`, `...WithSources`, `...WithInput`, or `UpdateConfig`; otherwise the promoted user story is not considered AI-friendly. Open payload fields may exist only at explicit `Spec`, `Input`, `Message`, `Request`, `Submission`, or `Config` boundaries where the product protocol requires caller-owned schema, config, metadata, answers, or service payloads. Those boundaries must still be named in the User Story Matrix or the exception table, must validate required fields locally through a named boundary method such as `Validate`, `Body`, `Params`, `SourceConfig`, `Updates`, `Map`, `validate`, `params`, `param`, `updates`, or `source_config`, and must have source/failure rules in User Story Stability Evidence.

The promoted return-type gate scans public `*Handle` methods and direct `Client` user-story entries so user-friendly facades cannot return `ProductData`, `RawMessage`, `any`, `map[string]any`, `Any`, or generic dictionaries. Open payload objects remain allowed only at the explicit construction boundaries described above; returned values must be typed products, handles, scalars, streams, or error-only completion signals.

The promoted code-block safety gate scans AI-copyable fenced examples so snippets cannot use direct HTTP, database, storage, local-file, AI-provider, LangChain, subprocess, shell, regex, raw JSON, URL parsing, string-splitting, background concurrency, timer, automatic retry/backoff, or force shortcut clients/helpers, `TODO`, `FIXME`, ellipsis placeholders, angle-bracket placeholders, local UUID/random/time id generation, blank-identifier ignored returns, broad Python exception swallowing, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `moi-core/catalog`, core catalog ports, `panic`, `print`, `log.Print`, `time.Sleep`, `sleep`, infinite loops, hard-coded API keys, tokens, passwords, or secrets as SDK facade bypasses, typed accessor bypasses, incomplete copy anchors, fabricated state, shortcut endpoint selection, duplicate submission, error handling, output, or progress. Examples must use a caller-owned product endpoint variable, source request/message ids from application request tracing, idempotency key storage, or explicit user/session state, return typed results/errors, observe progress through typed status/result/read calls, and keep credentials as caller-owned variables sourced from configuration or secret storage.

| Symbol | Classification | Recommended user-story entry |
|--------|----------------|------------------------------|
| `ConsumerMemoryFulltextSearchInput` | Typed caller-owned keyword and scalar Metadata filter boundary for one consumer. | Use `ConsumerMemoryHandle.FulltextSearch` / `fulltext_search`; the handle supplies the subject scope and the input validates the public filter contract. |
| `ConsumerMemoryStructuredQueryInput` | Typed caller-owned exact query, pagination, and scalar Metadata filter boundary for one consumer. | Use `ConsumerMemoryHandle.Query` / `query`; the handle supplies the subject scope and the input validates the public filter contract. |
| `ImportTaskCreateSpec` | Low-level route create-task payload. | Use `FileImportTaskSpec` through `ConnectorHandle.CreateFileImportTask` / `create_file_import_task` for the promoted file-import task. |
| `ExportTaskCreateSpec` | Low-level route create-export-task payload. | Use `ExportTaskSpec` through `ConnectorHandle.CreateExportTaskFromSpec` / `create_export_task_from_spec` for the promoted export task. |
| `ComputeResourceSpec` | Route-level compute resource spec contract. | Use `ComputeResourceHandle` for promoted compute lifecycle work; use `Client.Compute` / `client.compute` only for explicit route-level spec administration. |
| `DynamicServiceInvokeSpec` | Route-level workflow dynamic-service invoke payload. | Use `Client.Workflows` / `client.workflows` only when explicit route-level dynamic-service control is required. |
| `AgentSelector` | Low-level A2A selector carried by `AgentHandle`. | Use `WorkspaceHandle.AgentByCode` / `agent_by_code` or `WorkspaceHandle.AgentByID` / `agent_by_id` to bind an `AgentHandle` before promoted A2A operations. |
| `A2AMethodSend`, `A2A_METHOD_SEND` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC method constant. | Use `AgentHandle.SendText` / `send_text` with `AgentTextMessage` for the promoted send-message operation. |
| `A2AMethodStream`, `A2A_METHOD_STREAM` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC method constant. | Use `AgentHandle.StreamText` / `stream_text` with `AgentTextMessage` for the promoted streaming operation. |
| `A2AMethodTasksGet`, `A2A_METHOD_TASKS_GET` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC method constant. | Use `AgentHandle.GetTask` / `get_task` with `AgentTaskRequest` for the promoted task-status operation. |
| `A2AMethodTasksCancel`, `A2A_METHOD_TASKS_CANCEL` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC method constant. | Use `AgentHandle.CancelTask` / `cancel_task` with `AgentTaskRequest` for the promoted task-cancel operation. |
| `A2AMethodTasksResubscribe`, `A2A_METHOD_TASKS_RESUBSCRIBE` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC method constant. | Use `AgentHandle.ResubscribeTask` / `resubscribe_task` with `AgentTaskRequest` for the promoted task-resubscribe operation. |
| `A2AMethodInputSubmit`, `A2A_METHOD_INPUT_SUBMIT` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC method constant. | Use `AgentHandle.SubmitInput` / `submit_input` with `AgentInputSubmission` for the promoted input-submit operation. |
| `A2ARequest` | Low-level A2A JSON-RPC envelope. | Use `AgentTextMessage`, `AgentTaskRequest`, or `AgentInputSubmission` through `AgentHandle` for promoted A2A operations. |
| `AgentA2ARequest` | Low-level selector plus A2A JSON-RPC envelope. | Use `AgentHandle` plus the typed A2A helpers unless explicit route-level A2A control is required. |
| `ChannelInstanceCreateRequest`, `ChannelInstanceUpdateRequest` | Unified route-level channel instance payloads; `channel_type` selects the provider-specific `config` and `secrets` fields. | Use `Client.Agents` / `client.agents` only when explicit route-level channel administration is required; promoted agent workflows should use `AgentHandle` A2A helpers after an agent is already configured. |
| `CallbackRuleCreateRequest`, `CallbackRuleUpdateRequest` | Route-level callback routing rule payloads for workspace/provider webhook administration. | Use `Client.Agents` / `client.agents` only when explicit route-level callback routing administration is required; promoted agent workflows should use `AgentHandle` A2A helpers after routing is already configured. |
| `AgentTeamCreateRequest`, `AgentTeamUpdateRequest`, `AgentTeamSessionCreateRequest`, `AgentTeamSessionRenameRequest`, `AgentTeamMessageRequest` | Route-level Agent Team lifecycle, Session, and message payloads. | Use `Client.Agents` / `client.agents` for explicit Agent Team administration and shared-Session messaging; these operations do not target a single `AgentHandle`. |
| `IngressConnectionCreateRequest`, `IngressConnectionUpdateRequest`, `IngressConnectionVerifyRequest`, `IngressCallbackConfig`, `IngressMailPollingConfig`, `IngressManagedStreamConfig` | Route-level ingress lifecycle payloads for callback, mail polling, and provider-managed stream receivers. | Use `Client.Agents` / `client.agents` for explicit ingress administration; callback and mail polling configurations reference a ChannelInstance, while managed stream credentials belong directly to the IngressConnection. |
| `WeComQRBindingResult` | Short-lived WeCom QR credential binding for an ingress target. | Start `ResolveWeComQRBinding` / `resolve_we_com_qr_binding` with an empty session token, display `qr_url`, then poll with the returned token until authorized or expired. Authorization requires the same target permission as ingress creation on every call. Keep tokens and fresh credentials in memory, never logs; pass the authorized BotID/Secret to the existing managed-stream connection creation operation after user confirmation. |
| `SemanticModelSourceInput` | Route-level knowledge source payload for semantic model source routes. `catalog_file` requires `file_id` + `volume_id`. | Use `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, and `SemanticEntryInput` for promoted semantic model entry workflows; use `Client.Knowledge` / `client.knowledge` only when explicit route-level source control is required. |
| `SemanticModelSourceSelectionInput` | Route-level catalog source selection payload for knowledge source preview routes. | Use `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, and `SemanticEntryInput` for promoted semantic model entry workflows; use `Client.Knowledge` / `client.knowledge` only when explicit route-level source selection preview is required. |
| `SemanticModelSegmentUpdateInput` | Route-level knowledge source segment edit payload. | Use `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, and `SemanticEntryInput` for promoted semantic model entry workflows; use `Client.Knowledge` / `client.knowledge` only when explicit route-level segment control is required. |
| `SemanticModelSegmentCreateInput` | Route-level knowledge source segment creation payload. | Use `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, and `SemanticEntryInput` for promoted semantic model entry workflows; use `Client.Knowledge` / `client.knowledge` only when explicit route-level segment control is required. |
| `SemanticModelSourcePreview` | Route-level knowledge source document preview submessage. | Use `SemanticModelHandle`, `SemanticEntryHandle`, and `SemanticEntryInput` for promoted semantic model entry workflows; use `Client.Knowledge` / `client.knowledge` only when explicit route-level source document control is required. |
| `StructuredSourceObjectsRequest` | Route-level structured data loading source object discovery payload. | Use `Client.Connectors` / `client.connectors` only when explicit route-level structured source selection control is required; promoted structured load workflows should use the task/create UI or a future structured-load facade. |
| `StructuredSourceSchemaRequest` | Route-level structured data loading source schema discovery payload. | Use `Client.Connectors` / `client.connectors` only when explicit route-level structured source schema control is required; promoted structured load workflows should use the task/create UI or a future structured-load facade. |
| `StructuredBackfillStartResolveRequest` | Route-level structured data loading backfill start resolve payload. | Use `Client.Connectors` / `client.connectors` only when explicit route-level backfill start resolution is required; promoted structured load workflows should use the task/create UI or a future structured-load facade. |
| `DrawingSearchRequest` | Route-level drawing search payload for multipart POST `/newmoi/drawings/search` (`knowledge_base_id`, `query`, `file`, `top_k`). Query image is a path/stream/bytes; `query_image_file_id` is not a public product field. | Use `Client.Knowledge` / `client.knowledge` `SearchDrawings` / `search_drawings` only when explicit route-level drawing search is required; this is not a promoted user-story facade. |

The capability maturity report is a separate Go checker:

`bash
cd sdk
make capability-maturity-report
`

It reports remaining raw SDK surfaces:

- Go methods accepting `body any`.
- Go methods returning `RawMessage`.
- Go methods returning `ProductData` instead of domain-specific typed product results.
- Go methods accepting or returning `any` / `map[string]any` product data instead of typed product contracts.
- Go generic proxy methods such as `ResourceAny`.
- Python methods accepting `dict[str, Any]` request bodies.
- Python methods returning `Any` instead of typed product result contracts.

This report fails CI for `ProductData` responses, raw request bodies, generic request/response containers, and generic proxy methods unless the method has an explicit Product SDK design exemption in the checker. Go raw response findings are capped at zero. Apps routes are absent from the SDK rather than exempted from maturity checks.

Current hard maturity baseline after the identity/bootstrap plus audit log, catalog file/folder/tag/ref-list, catalog small-result, connector/import/export nil-data, data-share nil-data, RBAC nil-data, compute delete nil-data, app exclusion, and ProductData pass: `sdk/capability-maturity/known-findings.json` contains zero non-exempt findings. Go raw responses are capped at 0 for non-app SDK methods. Account/workspace bootstrap responses, audit log responses, catalog file upload/info/preview-link/trigger-workflow responses, catalog folder/tag small-result responses, catalog/database/volume/table/folder ref-list responses, path responses, URL responses, and boolean data responses are generated from Product SDK protobuf definitions. Catalog file create/update/delete/delete-ref, catalog folder/tag write-only operations, catalog/table/volume nil-data write operations, connector/import/export nil-data operations, data-share nil-data operations, RBAC nil-data operations, and compute delete nil-data are exposed as error-only SDK calls. Any new non-exempt `ProductData`, raw request body, generic request/response container, or generic proxy finding fails the gate; current reported raw/generic/ProductData findings are the explicit Agent resource proxy exemptions listed below.

The current explicit design exemption is the agent workspace resource proxy. Backend registers those routes as `Any` proxied resources, so their request/response contracts belong to the proxied resource owner rather than to one promoted Product SDK operation. These methods are route-aligned escape hatches only and must not appear in the User Story Matrix or task quick-start tables.

| Finding kind | Go symbol | Python symbol | Reason |
|--------------|-----------|---------------|--------|
| `go-raw-request` | `AgentService.ResourcePost`, `AgentService.ResourcePut`, `AgentService.ResourcePatch`, `AgentService.ResourceAny` | - | Agent resource APIs are backend `Any` proxies; request bodies are owned by each proxied resource contract. |
| `go-generic-request` | `AgentService.ResourcePost`, `AgentService.ResourcePut`, `AgentService.ResourcePatch`, `AgentService.ResourceAny` | - | Agent resource APIs are backend `Any` proxies; request bodies are owned by each proxied resource contract. |
| `go-generic-proxy` | `AgentService.ResourceAny` | - | `moi-backend` registers these routes as `Any` proxied resources. |
| `go-product-data-response` | `AgentService.ResourceGet`, `AgentService.ResourcePost`, `AgentService.ResourcePut`, `AgentService.ResourcePatch`, `AgentService.ResourceDelete`, `AgentService.ResourceAny` | - | The proxied resource owns its response contract. |
| `python-raw-request` | - | `AgentService.resource_post`, `AgentService.resource_put`, `AgentService.resource_patch`, `AgentService.resource_any` | Agent resource APIs are backend `Any` proxies; request bodies are owned by each proxied resource contract. |
| `python-generic-proxy` | - | `AgentService.resource_any` | `moi-backend` registers these routes as `Any` proxied resources. |
| `python-generic-response` | - | `AgentService.resource_get`, `AgentService.resource_post`, `AgentService.resource_put`, `AgentService.resource_patch`, `AgentService.resource_delete`, `AgentService.resource_any` | The proxied resource owns its response contract. |

### User Story Gap Register

Use this register for known gaps that are intentionally not considered user-friendly yet. Do not remove a row until the matching User Story Matrix row, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, full User Story Coverage suite, Stability Evidence, Representative Method Coverage, Example Scenario Coverage, route-aligned classification, quick-start docs, and tests prove the promoted path is complete. Until then, the current safe path is a route-aligned escape hatch or an explicit typed handle path with sourced inputs.

| Gap | Current safe path | Do not promote until | Required evidence |
|-----|-------------------|----------------------|-------------------|
| Agent resource CRUD/runtime | Keep `AgentService.Resource*` / `resource_*` route-aligned escape hatch calls out of task quick starts; use promoted `AgentHandle` A2A methods only for card/send/stream/task/input-submit workflows. | Backend promotes stable product contracts for the target agent resource/runtime user task instead of `Any` proxy ownership. | User Story Matrix row, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, full User Story Coverage suite, Stability Evidence, Representative Method Coverage, Example Scenario Coverage, Go/Python facade tests, route alignment, quick-start docs, and capability maturity exemption removal. |
| Connector external integration coverage | Use typed connector/import/export handles and specs with caller-sourced credentials, storage config, and metadata; do not claim full external-system coverage from unit examples. | Default integration credentials or test doubles can exercise source browse, import, export, retry, and state transitions without external secrets. | Product endpoint integration test, User Story Matrix update, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, full User Story Coverage suite, User Story Stability Evidence update, Representative Method Coverage, Example Scenario Coverage, Go/Python facade tests, Example Input Variable Source Matrix update, route alignment, and quick-start docs for any new connector routes. |
| SQL editor websocket coverage | Use promoted SQL metadata/workbook/query/statement handles for HTTP result/history/profile flows; keep websocket streaming out of promoted examples. | Backend exposes a stable streaming contract and the SDK has explicit Go/Python streaming APIs rather than ad hoc websocket snippets. | Streaming contract docs, Go/Python facade tests, integration test, User Story Matrix update, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, full User Story Coverage suite, Representative Method Coverage, Example Scenario Coverage, quick-start docs, and source rules for connection lifecycle inputs. |
| Workspace route classification | Use runtime route alignment plus existing `WorkspaceHandle` bootstrap/admin helpers as the current typed handle path; do not infer that every `/newmoi/workspaces/**` route is a promoted user task. | The runtime route manifest classification identifies which workspace routes are user-facing product operations versus bootstrap/admin/internal routes. | Route alignment source/runtime agreement, Route-Aligned Service Promotion Map update, User Story Matrix rows for promoted routes, Flow Recipe, Outcome Contract, full User Story Coverage suite, Example Scenario Coverage, Go/Python facade tests, quick-start docs, and explicit exceptions for non-promoted workspace routes. |

## Open Questions

Each open question must name its backing User Story Gap Register row so users and AI do not treat unresolved coverage as promoted SDK behavior.

| Question | Backing gap | Resolution evidence |
|----------|-------------|---------------------|
| Which `/newmoi/workspaces/**` routes are product user-facing and which are bootstrap/admin-only after the backend route manifest is generated? | Workspace route classification | Route manifest classification, route alignment source/runtime agreement, Route-Aligned Service Promotion Map update, and User Story Matrix rows only for promoted user-facing routes. |
| Which agent proxied resources are already sufficient UI contracts, and which need product backend promotion before SDK exposure? | Agent resource CRUD/runtime | Stable product contracts beyond `Any` proxy ownership, capability maturity exemption removal, Go/Python facade tests, and quick-start docs for promoted resource/runtime tasks. |
| Which connector/import/export flows can be exercised in the default integration suite without external credentials? | Connector external integration coverage | Integration test coverage with default credentials or test doubles, route alignment for new connector routes, and Example Input Variable Source Matrix updates for external inputs. |
| Whether SQL editor websocket coverage should be exposed as a streaming SDK API in Phase 1 or marked as required later with a backend-owned reason. | SQL editor websocket coverage | Streaming contract docs, explicit Go/Python streaming facade tests, integration test coverage, and source rules for websocket connection lifecycle inputs. |
